					<h1 id="firstHeading" class="firstHeading mw-first-heading"><span class="mw-page-title-main">Hungary</span></h1>
							
<div id="p-lang-btn" class="vector-dropdown mw-portlet mw-portlet-lang"  >
	<input type="checkbox" id="p-lang-btn-checkbox" role="button" aria-haspopup="true" data-event-name="ui.dropdown-p-lang-btn" class="vector-dropdown-checkbox mw-interlanguage-selector" aria-label="Go to an article in another language. Available in 272 languages"   >
	<label id="p-lang-btn-label" for="p-lang-btn-checkbox" class="vector-dropdown-label cdx-button cdx-button--fake-button cdx-button--fake-button--enabled cdx-button--weight-quiet cdx-button--action-progressive mw-portlet-lang-heading-272" aria-hidden="true"  ><span class="vector-icon mw-ui-icon-language-progressive mw-ui-icon-wikimedia-language-progressive"></span>

<span class="vector-dropdown-label-text">272 languages</span>
	</label>
	<div class="vector-dropdown-content">

<p><b>Hungary</b> (<a href="/wiki/Hungarian_language" title="Hungarian language">Hungarian</a>: <i lang="hu">Magyarország</i> <small></small><span title="Representation in the International Phonetic Alphabet (IPA)" class="IPA" lang="hu-Latn-fonipa"><a href="/wiki/Help:IPA/Hungarian" title="Help:IPA/Hungarian">[ˈmɒɟɒrorsaːɡ]</a></span>&#32;<span data-nosnippet="" id="ooui-php-1" class="ext-phonos ext-phonos-PhonosButton noexcerpt ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel oo-ui-widget oo-ui-widget-enabled oo-ui-buttonElement oo-ui-buttonElement-frameless oo-ui-iconElement oo-ui-buttonWidget" data-ooui="{&quot;_&quot;:&quot;mw.Phonos.PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;href&quot;:&quot;\/\/upload.wikimedia.org\/wikipedia\/commons\/transcoded\/2\/20\/Hu-Magyarorsz%C3%A1g.ogg\/Hu-Magyarorsz%C3%A1g.ogg.mp3&quot;,&quot;rel&quot;:[&quot;nofollow&quot;],&quot;framed&quot;:false,&quot;icon&quot;:&quot;volumeUp&quot;,&quot;data&quot;:{&quot;ipa&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;text&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;lang&quot;:&quot;en&quot;,&quot;wikibase&quot;:&quot;&quot;,&quot;file&quot;:&quot;Hu-Magyarorsz\u00e1g.ogg&quot;},&quot;classes&quot;:[&quot;ext-phonos&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton&quot;,&quot;noexcerpt&quot;,&quot;ext-phonos-PhonosButton-emptylabel&quot;]}"><a role="button" tabindex="0" href="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/transcoded/2/20/Hu-Magyarorsz%C3%A1g.ogg/Hu-Magyarorsz%C3%A1g.ogg.mp3" rel="nofollow" aria-label="Play audio" class="oo-ui-buttonElement-button"><span class="oo-ui-iconElement-icon oo-ui-icon-volumeUp"></span><span class="oo-ui-labelElement-label"></span><span class="oo-ui-indicatorElement-indicator oo-ui-indicatorElement-noIndicator"></span></a></span><sup class="ext-phonos-attribution noexcerpt"><a href="/wiki/File:Hu-Magyarorsz%C3%A1g.ogg" title="File:Hu-Magyarország.ogg">i</a></sup>) is a <a href="/wiki/Landlocked_country" title="Landlocked country">landlocked country</a> in <a href="/wiki/East-Central_Europe" title="East-Central Europe">East-Central Europe</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Flaw_2-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Flaw-2">&#91;2&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-14" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-14">&#91;13&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-15" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-15">&#91;14&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-16" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-16">&#91;15&#93;</a></sup> Spanning 93,030 square kilometres (35,920&#160;sq&#160;mi) of the <a href="/wiki/Pannonian_Basin" title="Pannonian Basin">Carpathian Basin</a>, it is bordered by <a href="/wiki/Slovakia" title="Slovakia">Slovakia</a> to the north, <a href="/wiki/Ukraine" title="Ukraine">Ukraine</a> to the northeast, <a href="/wiki/Romania" title="Romania">Romania</a> to the east and southeast, <a href="/wiki/Serbia" title="Serbia">Serbia</a> to the south, <a href="/wiki/Croatia" title="Croatia">Croatia</a> and <a href="/wiki/Slovenia" title="Slovenia">Slovenia</a> to the southwest, and <a href="/wiki/Austria" title="Austria">Austria</a> to the west. Hungary has a population of 9.7 million, mostly ethnic <a href="/wiki/Hungarians" title="Hungarians">Hungarians</a> and a significant <a href="/wiki/Romani_people_in_Hungary" title="Romani people in Hungary">Romani minority</a>. <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_language" title="Hungarian language">Hungarian</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Hungary" title="Languages of Hungary">official language</a>, is the world's most widely spoken <a href="/wiki/Uralic_languages" title="Uralic languages">Uralic language</a> and among the few non-<a href="/wiki/Indo-European_languages" title="Indo-European languages">Indo-European languages</a> widely spoken in Europe.<sup id="cite_ref-17" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-17">&#91;16&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Budapest" title="Budapest">Budapest</a> is the country's capital and <a href="/wiki/List_of_cities_and_towns_of_Hungary" title="List of cities and towns of Hungary">largest city</a>; other major urban areas include <a href="/wiki/Debrecen" title="Debrecen">Debrecen</a>, <a href="/wiki/Szeged" title="Szeged">Szeged</a>, <a href="/wiki/Miskolc" title="Miskolc">Miskolc</a>, <a href="/wiki/P%C3%A9cs" title="Pécs">Pécs</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Gy%C5%91r" title="Győr">Győr</a>.
</p><p>The territory of present-day Hungary has for centuries been a crossroads for various peoples, including <a href="/wiki/Celts" title="Celts">Celts</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ancient_Rome" title="Ancient Rome">Romans</a>, <a href="/wiki/Germanic_peoples" title="Germanic peoples">Germanic tribes</a>, <a href="/wiki/Huns" title="Huns">Huns</a>, <a href="/wiki/West_Slavs" title="West Slavs">West Slavs</a> and the <a href="/wiki/Pannonian_Avars" title="Pannonian Avars">Avars</a>. The foundation of the Hungarian state was established in the late 9th century AD with the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_conquest_of_the_Carpathian_Basin" title="Hungarian conquest of the Carpathian Basin">conquest of the Carpathian Basin</a> by Hungarian grand prince <a href="/wiki/%C3%81rp%C3%A1d" title="Árpád">Árpád</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-18" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-18">&#91;17&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Acta_orientalia_Academiae_Scientiarum_Hungaricae_19-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Acta_orientalia_Academiae_Scientiarum_Hungaricae-19">&#91;18&#93;</a></sup> His great-grandson <a href="/wiki/Stephen_I_of_Hungary" title="Stephen I of Hungary">Stephen I</a> ascended the throne in 1000, converting his realm to a <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary" title="Kingdom of Hungary">Christian kingdom</a>. By the 12th century, Hungary became a regional power, reaching its <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary_(1301%E2%80%931526)" title="Kingdom of Hungary (1301–1526)">cultural and political height in the 15th century</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-20" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-20">&#91;19&#93;</a></sup> Following the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Moh%C3%A1cs" title="Battle of Mohács">Battle of Mohács</a> in 1526, it was partially <a href="/wiki/Ottoman_Hungary" title="Ottoman Hungary">occupied by the Ottoman Empire</a> (1541–1699). Hungary came under <a href="/wiki/Habsburg_monarchy" title="Habsburg monarchy">Habsburg rule</a> at the turn of the 18th century, <a href="/wiki/Austro-Hungarian_Compromise_of_1867" title="Austro-Hungarian Compromise of 1867">later joining with</a> the <a href="/wiki/Austrian_Empire" title="Austrian Empire">Austrian Empire</a> to form <a href="/wiki/Austria-Hungary" title="Austria-Hungary">Austria-Hungary</a>, a <a href="/wiki/Great_power" title="Great power">major power</a> into the early 20th century.<sup id="cite_ref-21" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-21">&#91;20&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Austria-Hungary collapsed after <a href="/wiki/World_War_I" title="World War I">World War I</a>, and the subsequent <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Trianon" title="Treaty of Trianon">Treaty of Trianon</a> established Hungary's current borders, resulting in the loss of 71% of its territory, 58% of its population, and <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_diaspora" title="Hungarian diaspora">32% of ethnic Hungarians</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-22" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-22">&#91;21&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-23" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-23">&#91;22&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-24" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-24">&#91;23&#93;</a></sup> Following the tumultuous <a href="/wiki/Hungary_between_the_World_Wars" title="Hungary between the World Wars">interwar period</a>, Hungary joined the <a href="/wiki/Axis_powers" title="Axis powers">Axis powers</a> in <a href="/wiki/World_War_II" title="World War II">World War II</a>, suffering significant damage and casualties.<sup id="cite_ref-Montgomery_25-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Montgomery-25">&#91;24&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Thomas,_pg._11_26-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Thomas,_pg._11-26">&#91;25&#93;</a></sup> Postwar Hungary became a <a href="/wiki/Satellite_state" title="Satellite state">satellite state</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Soviet_Union" title="Soviet Union">Soviet Union</a>, leading to the establishment of the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_People%27s_Republic" title="Hungarian People&#39;s Republic">Hungarian People's Republic</a>. Following the failed <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Revolution_of_1956" title="Hungarian Revolution of 1956">1956 revolution</a>, Hungary became a <a href="/wiki/Goulash_Communism" title="Goulash Communism">comparatively freer</a>, though still repressed, member of the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Bloc" title="Eastern Bloc">Eastern Bloc</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Removal_of_Hungary%27s_border_fence_with_Austria" title="Removal of Hungary&#39;s border fence with Austria">removal of Hungary's border fence with Austria</a> accelerated the collapse of the Eastern Bloc and <a href="/wiki/Dissolution_of_the_Soviet_Union" title="Dissolution of the Soviet Union">subsequently the Soviet Union</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-27" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-27">&#91;26&#93;</a></sup> On 23 October 1989, Hungary again became a <a href="/wiki/Democracy" title="Democracy">democratic</a> <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_republic" title="Parliamentary republic">parliamentary republic</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-28" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-28">&#91;27&#93;</a></sup> Hungary joined the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a> in 2004 and has been part of the <a href="/wiki/Schengen_Area" title="Schengen Area">Schengen Area</a> since 2007.<sup id="cite_ref-29" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-29">&#91;28&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Hungary is a <a href="/wiki/Middle_power" title="Middle power">middle power</a> in international affairs, owing mostly to its <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Hungary" title="Culture of Hungary">cultural</a> and economic influence.<sup id="cite_ref-30" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-30">&#91;29&#93;</a></sup> It is a <a href="/wiki/World_Bank_high-income_economy" title="World Bank high-income economy">high-income economy</a> with <a href="/wiki/Healthcare_in_Hungary" title="Healthcare in Hungary">universal health care</a> and tuition-free <a href="/wiki/List_of_universities_and_colleges_in_Hungary" title="List of universities and colleges in Hungary">secondary education</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-auto_31-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-europa_32-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-europa-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> Hungary has a long history of significant contributions to <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_art" title="Hungarian art">arts</a>, <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Hungary" title="Music of Hungary">music</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_literature" title="Hungarian literature">literature</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hungary_at_the_Olympics" title="Hungary at the Olympics">sports</a>, <a href="/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Hungary" title="Science and technology in Hungary">science and technology</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-33" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-33">&#91;32&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-34" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-34">&#91;33&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Britannic_.=_35-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Britannic_.=-35">&#91;34&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-36" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-36">&#91;35&#93;</a></sup> It is a <a href="/wiki/World_Tourism_rankings" title="World Tourism rankings">popular</a> <a href="/wiki/Tourism_in_Hungary" title="Tourism in Hungary">tourist destination</a> in Europe, drawing 24.5 million international tourists in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-37" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-37">&#91;36&#93;</a></sup> It is a member of numerous international organisations, including the <a href="/wiki/Council_of_Europe" title="Council of Europe">Council of Europe</a>, <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a>, <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a>, <a href="/wiki/World_Health_Organization" title="World Health Organization">World Health Organization</a>, <a href="/wiki/World_Trade_Organization" title="World Trade Organization">World Trade Organization</a>, <a href="/wiki/World_Bank" title="World Bank">World Bank</a>, <a href="/wiki/Asian_Infrastructure_Investment_Bank" title="Asian Infrastructure Investment Bank">Asian Infrastructure Investment Bank</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Visegr%C3%A1d_Group" title="Visegrád Group">Visegrád Group</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-38" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-38">&#91;37&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r886046785">.mw-parser-output .toclimit-2 .toclevel-1 ul,.mw-parser-output .toclimit-3 .toclevel-2 ul,.mw-parser-output .toclimit-4 .toclevel-3 ul,.mw-parser-output .toclimit-5 .toclevel-4 ul,.mw-parser-output .toclimit-6 .toclevel-5 ul,.mw-parser-output .toclimit-7 .toclevel-6 ul{display:none}</style><div class="toclimit-3"><meta property="mw:PageProp/toc" /></div>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Etymology">Etymology</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Name_of_Hungary" title="Name of Hungary">Name of Hungary</a></div>
<p>The "H" in the name of Hungary (and Latin <i>Hungaria</i>) is most likely derived from historical associations with the <a href="/wiki/Huns" title="Huns">Huns</a>, who had settled Hungary prior to the <a href="/wiki/Pannonian_Avars" title="Pannonian Avars">Avars</a>. The rest of the word comes from the Latinised form of <a href="/wiki/Medieval_Greek" title="Medieval Greek">Byzantine Greek</a> <i>Oungroi</i> (Οὔγγροι). The Greek name might be borrowed from <a href="/wiki/Old_Church_Slavonic" title="Old Church Slavonic">Old Bulgarian</a> <i>ągrinŭ</i>, in turn borrowed from <a href="/wiki/Oghuric_languages" title="Oghuric languages">Oghur-Turkic</a> <i><a href="/wiki/Onogurs" title="Onogurs">Onogur</a></i> ('ten [tribes of the] Ogurs').<sup id="cite_ref-39" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-39">&#91;38&#93;</a></sup> <i><a href="/wiki/Onogurs" title="Onogurs">Onogur</a></i> was the collective name for the tribes who later joined the <a href="/wiki/Bulgars" title="Bulgars">Bulgar</a> tribal confederacy that ruled the eastern parts of Hungary after the Avars.<sup id="cite_ref-Király_40-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Király-40">&#91;39&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-41" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-41">&#91;40&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Peter_Benjamin_Golden" title="Peter Benjamin Golden">Peter B. Golden</a> also considers Árpád Berta's theory, who has suggested that the name derives from <a href="/wiki/Khazar_language" title="Khazar language">Khazar Turkic</a> ongar (oŋ "right," oŋar- "to make something better, to put (it) right," oŋgar-&#160; "to make something better, to put (it) right," oŋaru "towards the right") "right wing". This points to the idea that the Magyar Union before the Conquest formed the "right wing" (= western wing) of the <a href="/wiki/Khazars" title="Khazars">Khazar</a> military forces.<sup id="cite_ref-42" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-42">&#91;41&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The Hungarian <a href="/wiki/Endonym_and_exonym" title="Endonym and exonym">endonym</a> is <span title="Hungarian-language text"><i lang="hu">Magyarország</i></span>, composed of <i>magyar</i> ('Hungarian') and <i>ország</i> ('country'). The name "Magyar", which refers to the people of the country, more accurately reflects the name of the country in some other languages such as <a href="/wiki/Turkish_language" title="Turkish language">Turkish</a>, <a href="/wiki/Persian_language" title="Persian language">Persian</a> and other languages as <i>Magyaristan</i> or <i>Land of Magyars</i> or similar. The word <i>magyar</i> is taken from the name of one of the seven major semi-nomadic Hungarian tribes, <i>magyeri</i>.<sup id="cite_ref-43" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-43">&#91;42&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-44" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-44">&#91;43&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-45" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-45">&#91;44&#93;</a></sup> The first element <i>magy</i> is likely from Proto-Ugric *<i>mäńć-</i> 'man, person', also found in the name of the <a href="/wiki/Mansi_people" title="Mansi people">Mansi people</a> (<i>mäńćī, mańśi, måńś</i>). The second element <i>eri</i>, 'man, men, lineage', survives in Hungarian <i>férj</i> 'husband', and is cognate with <a href="/wiki/Mari_language" title="Mari language">Mari</a> <i>erge</i> 'son', <a href="/wiki/Finnish_language" title="Finnish language">Finnish</a> archaic <i>yrkä</i> 'young man'.<sup id="cite_ref-46" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-46">&#91;45&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="History">History</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/History_of_Hungary" title="History of Hungary">History of Hungary</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Before_895">Before 895</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_prehistory" title="Hungarian prehistory">Hungarian prehistory</a> and <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_mythology" title="Hungarian mythology">Hungarian mythology</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Roman_provinces_of_Illyricum,_Macedonia,_Dacia,_Moesia,_Pannonia_and_Thracia.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b1/Roman_provinces_of_Illyricum%2C_Macedonia%2C_Dacia%2C_Moesia%2C_Pannonia_and_Thracia.jpg/220px-Roman_provinces_of_Illyricum%2C_Macedonia%2C_Dacia%2C_Moesia%2C_Pannonia_and_Thracia.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="178" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b1/Roman_provinces_of_Illyricum%2C_Macedonia%2C_Dacia%2C_Moesia%2C_Pannonia_and_Thracia.jpg/330px-Roman_provinces_of_Illyricum%2C_Macedonia%2C_Dacia%2C_Moesia%2C_Pannonia_and_Thracia.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b1/Roman_provinces_of_Illyricum%2C_Macedonia%2C_Dacia%2C_Moesia%2C_Pannonia_and_Thracia.jpg/440px-Roman_provinces_of_Illyricum%2C_Macedonia%2C_Dacia%2C_Moesia%2C_Pannonia_and_Thracia.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2817" data-file-height="2284" /></a><figcaption>Roman provinces: Illyricum, Macedonia, Dacia, Moesia, Pannonia, Thracia</figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Roman_Empire" title="Roman Empire">Roman Empire</a> conquered the territory between the <a href="/wiki/Alps" title="Alps">Alps</a> and the area west of the <a href="/wiki/Danube" title="Danube">Danube</a> River from 16 to 15 BC, the Danube being the frontier of the empire.<sup id="cite_ref-Kershaw_47-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kershaw-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> In 14 BC, <a href="/wiki/Pannonia" title="Pannonia">Pannonia</a>, the western part of the <a href="/wiki/Pannonian_Basin" title="Pannonian Basin">Carpathian Basin</a>, which includes today's west of Hungary, was recognised by emperor <a href="/wiki/Augustus" title="Augustus">Augustus</a> in the <i><a href="/wiki/Res_Gestae_Divi_Augusti" title="Res Gestae Divi Augusti">Res Gestae Divi Augusti</a></i> as part of the Roman Empire.<sup id="cite_ref-Kershaw_47-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kershaw-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> The area south-east of <a href="/wiki/Pannonia" title="Pannonia">Pannonia</a> was organised as the Roman province <a href="/wiki/Moesia" title="Moesia">Moesia</a> in 6 BC.<sup id="cite_ref-Kershaw_47-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kershaw-47">&#91;46&#93;</a></sup> An area east of the river <a href="/wiki/Tisza" title="Tisza">Tisza</a> became the Roman province of <a href="/wiki/Dacia" title="Dacia">Dacia</a> in 106 AD, which included today's east Hungary. It remained under Roman rule until 271.<sup id="cite_ref-Scarre_48-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Scarre-48">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup> From 235, the Roman Empire went through troubled times, caused by revolts, rivalry and rapid succession of emperors. The Western Roman Empire collapsed in the 5th century under the stress of the migration of <a href="/wiki/Germanic_peoples" title="Germanic peoples">Germanic tribes</a> and <a href="/wiki/Carpi_people" title="Carpi people">Carpian</a> pressure.<sup id="cite_ref-Scarre_48-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Scarre-48">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>This period brought many invaders into Central Europe, beginning with the <a href="/wiki/Huns" title="Huns">Hunnic Empire</a> (c. 370–469). The most powerful ruler of the Hunnic Empire was <a href="/wiki/Attila" title="Attila">Attila</a> the Hun (434–453), who later became a central figure in Hungarian mythology.<sup id="cite_ref-Kelly_49-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Kelly-49">&#91;48&#93;</a></sup> After the disintegration of the Hunnic Empire, the <a href="/wiki/Gepids" title="Gepids">Gepids</a>, an Eastern Germanic tribe, who had been vassalised by the Huns, established their own kingdom in the Carpathian Basin.<sup id="cite_ref-Bona_The_Gepids_during_and_after_the_Hun_Period_50-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Bona_The_Gepids_during_and_after_the_Hun_Period-50">&#91;49&#93;</a></sup> Other groups which reached the Carpathian Basin during the Migration Period were the <a href="/wiki/Goths" title="Goths">Goths</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vandals" title="Vandals">Vandals</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lombards" title="Lombards">Lombards</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Early_Slavs" title="Early Slavs">Slavs</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Scarre_48-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Scarre-48">&#91;47&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the 560s, the <a href="/wiki/Pannonian_Avars" title="Pannonian Avars">Avars</a> founded the Avar Khaganate, a state that maintained supremacy in the region for more than two centuries. The <a href="/wiki/Franks" title="Franks">Franks</a> under <a href="/wiki/Charlemagne" title="Charlemagne">Charlemagne</a> defeated the Avars in a series of campaigns during the 790s.<sup id="cite_ref-Gubcsi_51-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Gubcsi-51">&#91;50&#93;</a></sup> Between 804 and 829, the <a href="/wiki/First_Bulgarian_Empire" title="First Bulgarian Empire">First Bulgarian Empire</a> conquered the lands east of the Danube and took over the rule of the local Slavic tribes and remnants of the Avars.<sup id="cite_ref-Skutsch_52-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Skutsch-52">&#91;51&#93;</a></sup> By the mid-9th century, the <a href="/wiki/Slavs_in_Lower_Pannonia#Principality" title="Slavs in Lower Pannonia">Balaton Principality</a>, also known as Lower Pannonia, was established west of the Danube as part of the Frankish <a href="/wiki/March_of_Pannonia" title="March of Pannonia">March of Pannonia</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Luthar_53-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Luthar-53">&#91;52&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="Middle_Ages_.28895.E2.80.931526.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Middle_Ages_(895–1526)">Middle Ages (895–1526)</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Principality_of_Hungary" title="Principality of Hungary">Principality of Hungary</a> and <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary_in_the_Middle_Ages" class="mw-redirect" title="Kingdom of Hungary in the Middle Ages">Kingdom of Hungary in the Middle Ages</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kalandozasok.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/99/Kalandozasok.jpg/220px-Kalandozasok.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="151" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/99/Kalandozasok.jpg/330px-Kalandozasok.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/99/Kalandozasok.jpg/440px-Kalandozasok.jpg 2x" data-file-width="724" data-file-height="496" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Hungarian_invasions_of_Europe" title="Hungarian invasions of Europe">Hungarian raids in the 10th century</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>The freshly unified <a href="/wiki/Magyar_tribes" title="Magyar tribes">Hungarians</a><sup id="cite_ref-54" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-54">&#91;53&#93;</a></sup> led by <a href="/wiki/%C3%81rp%C3%A1d" title="Árpád">Árpád</a> (by tradition a descendant of Attila), <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_conquest_of_the_Carpathian_Basin" title="Hungarian conquest of the Carpathian Basin">settled in the Carpathian Basin</a> starting in 895.<sup id="cite_ref-A_Country_Study:_Hungary_55-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-A_Country_Study:_Hungary-55">&#91;54&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Eliznik.org.uk_56-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Eliznik.org.uk-56">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup> According to the <a href="/wiki/Finno-Ugric_languages" title="Finno-Ugric languages">Finno-Ugrian theory</a>, they originated from an ancient <a href="/wiki/Uralic_languages" title="Uralic languages">Uralic</a>-speaking population that formerly inhabited the forested area between the <a href="/wiki/Volga" title="Volga">Volga</a> River and the <a href="/wiki/Ural_Mountains" title="Ural Mountains">Ural Mountains</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-57" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-57">&#91;56&#93;</a></sup> As a federation of united tribes, <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_conquest_of_the_Carpathian_Basin" title="Hungarian conquest of the Carpathian Basin">Hungary was established in 895</a>, some 50 years after the division of the <a href="/wiki/Carolingian_Empire" title="Carolingian Empire">Carolingian Empire</a> at the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Verdun" title="Treaty of Verdun">Treaty of Verdun</a> in 843, before the unification of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_England" title="Kingdom of England">Anglo-Saxon kingdoms</a>.
</p><p>Initially, the rising <a href="/wiki/Principality_of_Hungary" title="Principality of Hungary">Principality of Hungary</a> ("Western Tourkia" in medieval Greek sources)<sup id="cite_ref-58" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-58">&#91;57&#93;</a></sup> was a state created by a semi-nomadic people. It accomplished an enormous transformation into a Christian realm during the 10th century.<sup id="cite_ref-Stephen_Wyley_59-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Stephen_Wyley-59">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> This state was well-functioning, and the nation's military power allowed the Hungarians to conduct successful <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_invasions_of_Europe" title="Hungarian invasions of Europe">fierce campaigns and raids</a>, from <a href="/wiki/Constantinople" title="Constantinople">Constantinople</a> to as far as today's Spain.<sup id="cite_ref-Stephen_Wyley_59-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Stephen_Wyley-59">&#91;58&#93;</a></sup> The Hungarians defeated <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_invasions_of_Europe" title="Hungarian invasions of Europe">three major East Frankish imperial armies</a> between 907 and 910.<sup id="cite_ref-60" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-60">&#91;59&#93;</a></sup> A defeat at the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Lechfeld" title="Battle of Lechfeld">Battle of Lechfeld</a> in 955 signaled a provisory end to most campaigns on foreign territories, at least towards the west.
</p>
<h4><span id="Age_of_.C3.81rp.C3.A1dian_kings"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Age_of_Árpádian_kings">Age of Árpádian kings</span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary_(1000%E2%80%931301)" title="Kingdom of Hungary (1000–1301)">Kingdom of Hungary (1000–1301)</a> and <a href="/wiki/%C3%81rp%C3%A1d_dynasty" title="Árpád dynasty">Árpád dynasty</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Istvan-ChroniconPictum.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d9/Istvan-ChroniconPictum.jpg/170px-Istvan-ChroniconPictum.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="166" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d9/Istvan-ChroniconPictum.jpg/255px-Istvan-ChroniconPictum.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d9/Istvan-ChroniconPictum.jpg/340px-Istvan-ChroniconPictum.jpg 2x" data-file-width="511" data-file-height="500" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Stephen_I_of_Hungary" title="Stephen I of Hungary">King Saint Stephen</a>, the first <a href="/wiki/King_of_Hungary" title="King of Hungary">King of Hungary</a>, converted the nation to Christianity.</figcaption></figure>
<p>In 972, the ruling prince (<a href="/wiki/Hungarian_language" title="Hungarian language">Hungarian</a>: <i lang="hu">fejedelem</i>) <a href="/wiki/G%C3%A9za,_Grand_Prince_of_the_Hungarians" title="Géza, Grand Prince of the Hungarians">Géza</a> of the <a href="/wiki/%C3%81rp%C3%A1d_dynasty" title="Árpád dynasty">Árpád dynasty</a> officially started to integrate Hungary into Christian Western Europe.<sup id="cite_ref-61" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-61">&#91;60&#93;</a></sup> His first-born son, <a href="/wiki/Stephen_I_of_Hungary" title="Stephen I of Hungary">Saint Stephen I</a>, became the first <a href="/wiki/King_of_Hungary" title="King of Hungary">King of Hungary</a> after defeating his <a href="/wiki/Paganism" title="Paganism">pagan</a> uncle <a href="/wiki/Kopp%C3%A1ny" title="Koppány">Koppány</a>, who also claimed the throne. Under Stephen, Hungary was recognised as a Catholic <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Majesty" title="Apostolic Majesty">Apostolic Kingdom</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-62" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-62">&#91;61&#93;</a></sup> Applying to <a href="/wiki/Pope_Sylvester_II" title="Pope Sylvester II">Pope Sylvester II</a>, Stephen received the insignia of royalty (including probably a part of the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Crown_of_Hungary" title="Holy Crown of Hungary">Holy Crown of Hungary</a>, currently kept in the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Parliament_Building" title="Hungarian Parliament Building">Hungarian Parliament</a>) from the papacy.
</p><p>By 1006, Stephen consolidated his power and started sweeping reforms to convert Hungary into a Western <a href="/wiki/Feudalism" title="Feudalism">feudal state</a>. The country switched to using Latin for administration purposes, and until as late as 1844, Latin remained the official language of administration. Around this time, Hungary began to become a powerful kingdom.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (September 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> <a href="/wiki/Ladislaus_I_of_Hungary" title="Ladislaus I of Hungary">Ladislaus I</a> extended Hungary's frontier in <a href="/wiki/Transylvania" title="Transylvania">Transylvania</a> and invaded <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Croatia_(925%E2%80%931102)" title="Kingdom of Croatia (925–1102)">Croatia</a> in 1091.<sup id="cite_ref-63" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-63">&#91;62&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-64" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-64">&#91;63&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-65" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-65">&#91;64&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-66" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-66">&#91;65&#93;</a></sup> The Croatian campaign culminated in the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Gvozd_Mountain" title="Battle of Gvozd Mountain">Battle of Gvozd Mountain</a> in 1097 and a <a href="/wiki/Croatia_in_personal_union_with_Hungary" title="Croatia in personal union with Hungary">personal union of Croatia and Hungary</a> in 1102, ruled by <a href="/wiki/Coloman,_King_of_Hungary" title="Coloman, King of Hungary">Coloman</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-HR-HU-Heka_67-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-HR-HU-Heka-67">&#91;66&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:A_Szent_Korona_el%C3%B6lr%C5%91l_2.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/A_Szent_Korona_el%C3%B6lr%C5%91l_2.jpg/170px-A_Szent_Korona_el%C3%B6lr%C5%91l_2.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="198" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/A_Szent_Korona_el%C3%B6lr%C5%91l_2.jpg/255px-A_Szent_Korona_el%C3%B6lr%C5%91l_2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/2/21/A_Szent_Korona_el%C3%B6lr%C5%91l_2.jpg/340px-A_Szent_Korona_el%C3%B6lr%C5%91l_2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1500" data-file-height="1750" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Holy_Crown_of_Hungary" title="Holy Crown of Hungary">Holy Crown</a> (<i>Szent Korona</i>), one of the key symbols of Hungary</figcaption></figure>
<p>The most powerful and wealthiest king of the Árpád dynasty was <a href="/wiki/B%C3%A9la_III_of_Hungary" title="Béla III of Hungary">Béla III</a>, who disposed of the equivalent of 23 tonnes of silver per year, according to a contemporary <a href="/wiki/Income_register_of_B%C3%A9la_III_of_Hungary" title="Income register of Béla III of Hungary">income register</a>. This exceeded the income of the French king (estimated at 17 tonnes) and was double the receipts of the English Crown.<sup id="cite_ref-68" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-68">&#91;67&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Andrew_II_of_Hungary" title="Andrew II of Hungary">Andrew II</a> issued the <i><a href="/wiki/Diploma_Andreanum" title="Diploma Andreanum">Diploma Andreanum</a></i> which secured the special privileges of the <a href="/wiki/Transylvanian_Saxons" title="Transylvanian Saxons">Transylvanian Saxons</a> and is considered the first <a href="/wiki/Autonomous_administrative_division" title="Autonomous administrative division">autonomy</a> law in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-69" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-69">&#91;68&#93;</a></sup> He led the <a href="/wiki/Fifth_Crusade" title="Fifth Crusade">Fifth Crusade</a> to the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Land" title="Holy Land">Holy Land</a> in 1217, setting up the largest royal army in the history of Crusades. His <a href="/wiki/Golden_Bull_of_1222" title="Golden Bull of 1222">Golden Bull of 1222</a> was the first constitution in <a href="/wiki/Continental_Europe" title="Continental Europe">Continental Europe</a>. The lesser nobles also began to present Andrew with grievances, a practice that evolved into the institution of the parliament (<i>parlamentum publicum</i>).
</p><p>In 1241–1242, the kingdom received a major blow with the <a href="/wiki/Mongolian_invasion_of_Hungary" class="mw-redirect" title="Mongolian invasion of Hungary">Mongol (Tatar) invasion</a>. Up to half of Hungary's population of 2 million were victims of the invasion.<sup id="cite_ref-70" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-70">&#91;69&#93;</a></sup> King Béla IV let <a href="/wiki/Cumans" title="Cumans">Cumans</a> and <a href="/wiki/Jasz_people" title="Jasz people">Jassic people</a> into the country, who were fleeing the Mongols.<sup id="cite_ref-71" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-71">&#91;70&#93;</a></sup> Over the centuries, they were fully assimilated into the Hungarian population.<sup id="cite_ref-72" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-72">&#91;71&#93;</a></sup> After the Mongols retreated, King Béla ordered the construction of hundreds of stone castles and fortifications, to defend against a possible second Mongol invasion. The <a href="/wiki/Second_Mongol_invasion_of_Hungary" title="Second Mongol invasion of Hungary">Mongols returned to Hungary</a> in 1285, but the newly built stone-castle systems and new tactics (using a higher proportion of heavily armed knights) stopped them. The invading Mongol force was defeated<sup id="cite_ref-73" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-73">&#91;72&#93;</a></sup> near Pest by the royal army of King <a href="/wiki/Ladislaus_IV_of_Hungary" title="Ladislaus IV of Hungary">Ladislaus IV</a>. As with later invasions, it was repelled handily, the Mongols losing much of their invading force.
</p>
<h4><span class="mw-headline" id="Age_of_elected_kings">Age of elected kings</span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary_(1301%E2%80%931526)" title="Kingdom of Hungary (1301–1526)">Kingdom of Hungary (1301–1526)</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ottoman%E2%80%93Hungarian_Wars" class="mw-redirect" title="Ottoman–Hungarian Wars">Ottoman–Hungarian Wars</a></div>
<figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Nagy_lajos.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/Nagy_lajos.jpg/229px-Nagy_lajos.jpg" decoding="async" width="229" height="179" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/Nagy_lajos.jpg/344px-Nagy_lajos.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/7b/Nagy_lajos.jpg/458px-Nagy_lajos.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2304" data-file-height="1800" /></a><figcaption>A map of the lands ruled by <a href="/wiki/Louis_I_of_Hungary" title="Louis I of Hungary">Louis the Great</a> in Pallas's Great Encyclopedia</figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary" title="Kingdom of Hungary">Kingdom of Hungary</a> reached one of its greatest extents during the Árpádian kings, yet royal power was weakened at the end of their rule in 1301. After a destructive period of <a href="/wiki/Interregnum" title="Interregnum">interregnum</a> (1301–1308), the first <a href="/wiki/Capetian_House_of_Anjou" title="Capetian House of Anjou">Angevin</a> king, <a href="/wiki/Charles_I_of_Hungary" title="Charles I of Hungary">Charles I of Hungary</a> – a bilineal descendant of the <a href="/wiki/%C3%81rp%C3%A1d_dynasty" title="Árpád dynasty">Árpád dynasty</a> – successfully restored royal power and defeated oligarch rivals, the so-called "little kings". The second Angevin Hungarian king, <a href="/wiki/Louis_I_of_Hungary" title="Louis I of Hungary">Louis the Great</a> (1342–1382), led many successful military campaigns from Lithuania to southern Italy (<a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Naples" title="Kingdom of Naples">Kingdom of Naples</a>) and was also <a href="/wiki/List_of_Polish_monarchs" title="List of Polish monarchs">King of Poland</a> from 1370. After King Louis died without a male heir, the country was stabilised only when <a href="/wiki/Sigismund,_Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Sigismund, Holy Roman Emperor">Sigismund of Luxembourg</a> (1387–1437) succeeded to the throne, who in 1433 also became <a href="/wiki/Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Holy Roman Emperor">Holy Roman Emperor</a>. Sigismund was also (in several ways) a bilineal descendant of the Árpád dynasty.
</p>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Matthiascorvinusmap01.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/Matthiascorvinusmap01.jpg/275px-Matthiascorvinusmap01.jpg" decoding="async" width="275" height="209" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/Matthiascorvinusmap01.jpg/413px-Matthiascorvinusmap01.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/Matthiascorvinusmap01.jpg/550px-Matthiascorvinusmap01.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1135" data-file-height="862" /></a><figcaption>Map of the lands ruled by <a href="/wiki/Matthias_Corvinus" title="Matthias Corvinus">Matthias Corvinus</a>. Designed by Dr. Lajos Baróti.</figcaption></figure>
<p>The first Hungarian <a href="/wiki/Bible_translations" title="Bible translations">Bible translation</a> was completed in 1439. For half a year in 1437, there was an antifeudal and anticlerical <a href="/wiki/Transylvanian_peasant_revolt" title="Transylvanian peasant revolt">peasant revolt in Transylvania</a> which was strongly influenced by <a href="/wiki/Hussite" class="mw-redirect" title="Hussite">Hussite</a> ideas.
</p><p>From a small noble family in Transylvania, <a href="/wiki/John_Hunyadi" title="John Hunyadi">John Hunyadi</a> grew to become one of the country's most powerful lords, thanks to his outstanding capabilities as a mercenary commander. He was elected governor, then regent. He was a successful crusader against the <a href="/wiki/Ottoman_Turks" title="Ottoman Turks">Ottoman Turks</a>, one of his greatest victories being the <a href="/wiki/Siege_of_Belgrade_(1456)" title="Siege of Belgrade (1456)">siege of Belgrade</a> in 1456.
</p><p>The last strong king of medieval Hungary was the <a href="/wiki/Renaissance" title="Renaissance">Renaissance</a> king <a href="/wiki/Matthias_Corvinus" title="Matthias Corvinus">Matthias Corvinus</a> (1458–1490), son of John Hunyadi. His election was the first time that a member of the nobility mounted to the Hungarian royal throne without dynastic background. He was a successful military leader and an enlightened patron of the arts and learning.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica1_74-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica1-74">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> His library, the <a href="/wiki/Bibliotheca_Corviniana" title="Bibliotheca Corviniana">Bibliotheca Corviniana</a>, was Europe's greatest collection of historical chronicles, philosophic and scientific works in the 15th century, and second only in size to the <a href="/wiki/Vatican_Library" title="Vatican Library">Vatican Library</a>. Items from the Bibliotheca Corviniana were inscribed on <a href="/wiki/UNESCO" title="UNESCO">UNESCO</a>'s <a href="/wiki/Memory_of_the_World_Programme" title="Memory of the World Programme">Memory of the World Register</a> in 2005.<sup id="cite_ref-75" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-75">&#91;74&#93;</a></sup> The serfs and common people considered him a just ruler because he protected them from excessive demands and other abuses by the magnates.<sup id="cite_ref-76" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-76">&#91;75&#93;</a></sup> Under his rule, in 1479, the Hungarian army destroyed the Ottoman and Wallachian troops at the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Breadfield" title="Battle of Breadfield">Battle of Breadfield</a>. Abroad he defeated the Polish and German imperial armies of Frederick at Breslau (<a href="/wiki/Wroc%C5%82aw" title="Wrocław">Wrocław</a>). Matthias' mercenary standing army, the <a href="/wiki/Black_Army_of_Hungary" title="Black Army of Hungary">Black Army of Hungary</a>, was an unusually large army for its time, and it conquered <a href="/wiki/Vienna" title="Vienna">Vienna</a> as well as parts of Austria and <a href="/wiki/Bohemia" title="Bohemia">Bohemia</a>.
</p>
<h4><span id="Decline_.281490.E2.80.931526.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Decline_(1490–1526)">Decline (1490–1526)</span></h4>
<p>King Matthias died without lawful sons, and the Hungarian magnates procured the accession of the Pole <a href="/wiki/Vladislaus_II_of_Hungary" title="Vladislaus II of Hungary">Vladislaus II</a> (1490–1516), supposedly because of his weak influence on Hungarian aristocracy.<sup id="cite_ref-britannica1_74-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-britannica1-74">&#91;73&#93;</a></sup> Hungary's international role declined, its political stability was shaken, and social progress was deadlocked.<sup id="cite_ref-77" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-77">&#91;76&#93;</a></sup> In 1514, the weakened old King Vladislaus II faced a major peasant rebellion led by <a href="/wiki/Gy%C3%B6rgy_D%C3%B3zsa" title="György Dózsa">György Dózsa</a>, which was ruthlessly crushed by the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_nobility" title="Hungarian nobility">nobles</a>, led by <a href="/wiki/John_Z%C3%A1polya" title="John Zápolya">John Zápolya</a>. The resulting degradation of order paved the way for Ottoman preeminence. In 1521, the strongest Hungarian fortress in the South, Nándorfehérvár (today's <a href="/wiki/Belgrade" title="Belgrade">Belgrade</a>, Serbia), <a href="/wiki/Siege_of_Belgrade_(1521)" title="Siege of Belgrade (1521)">fell to the Turks</a>. The early appearance of Protestantism further worsened internal relations in the country.
</p>
<h3><span id="Ottoman_wars_.281526.E2.80.931699.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Ottoman_wars_(1526–1699)">Ottoman wars (1526–1699)</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary_(1526%E2%80%931867)" title="Kingdom of Hungary (1526–1867)">Kingdom of Hungary (1526–1867)</a>, <a href="/wiki/Ottoman_Hungary" title="Ottoman Hungary">Ottoman Hungary</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Hungarian_Kingdom" title="Eastern Hungarian Kingdom">Eastern Hungarian Kingdom</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Principality_of_Transylvania_(1570%E2%80%931711)" title="Principality of Transylvania (1570–1711)">Principality of Transylvania (1570–1711)</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ottoman%E2%80%93Habsburg_wars" title="Ottoman–Habsburg wars">Ottoman–Habsburg wars</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Sz%C3%A9kely,_Bertalan_-_The_Women_of_Eger_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/Sz%C3%A9kely%2C_Bertalan_-_The_Women_of_Eger_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg/170px-Sz%C3%A9kely%2C_Bertalan_-_The_Women_of_Eger_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="220" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/Sz%C3%A9kely%2C_Bertalan_-_The_Women_of_Eger_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg/255px-Sz%C3%A9kely%2C_Bertalan_-_The_Women_of_Eger_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/b3/Sz%C3%A9kely%2C_Bertalan_-_The_Women_of_Eger_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg/340px-Sz%C3%A9kely%2C_Bertalan_-_The_Women_of_Eger_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1884" data-file-height="2437" /></a><figcaption>Painting commemorating the <a href="/wiki/Siege_of_Eger_(1552)" title="Siege of Eger (1552)">siege of Eger</a>, a major victory against the Ottomans</figcaption></figure>
<p>After some <a href="/wiki/Ottoman_wars_in_Europe" title="Ottoman wars in Europe">150 years of wars</a> with the Hungarians and other states, the Ottomans gained a decisive victory over the Hungarian army at the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Moh%C3%A1cs" title="Battle of Mohács">Battle of Mohács</a> in 1526, where King <a href="/wiki/Louis_II_of_Hungary" title="Louis II of Hungary">Louis II</a> died while fleeing. Amid political chaos, the divided Hungarian nobility elected two kings simultaneously, <a href="/wiki/John_Z%C3%A1polya" title="John Zápolya">John Zápolya</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ferdinand_I,_Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Ferdinand I, Holy Roman Emperor">Ferdinand I</a> of the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Habsburg" title="House of Habsburg">Habsburg dynasty</a>. With the conquest of <a href="/wiki/Buda" title="Buda">Buda</a> by the Turks in 1541, Hungary was divided into three parts and remained so until the end of the 17th century. The north-western part, termed as <a href="/wiki/Royal_Hungary" class="mw-redirect" title="Royal Hungary">Royal Hungary</a>, was annexed by the Habsburgs who ruled as kings of Hungary. The eastern part of the kingdom became independent as the <a href="/wiki/Principality_of_Transylvania_(1570%E2%80%931711)" title="Principality of Transylvania (1570–1711)">Principality of Transylvania</a>, under Ottoman (and later Habsburg) <a href="/wiki/Suzerainty" title="Suzerainty">suzerainty</a>. The remaining central area, including the capital Buda, was known as the Pashalik of Buda.
</p><p>The vast majority of the seventeen and nineteen thousand Ottoman soldiers in service in the Ottoman fortresses in the territory of Hungary were Orthodox and Muslim Balkan Slavs rather than ethnic Turkish people.<sup id="cite_ref-78" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-78">&#91;77&#93;</a></sup> Orthodox Southern Slavs were also acting as <a href="/wiki/Akinji" title="Akinji">akinjis</a> and other light troops intended for pillaging in the territory of present-day Hungary.<sup id="cite_ref-79" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-79">&#91;78&#93;</a></sup> In 1686, the <a href="/wiki/Holy_League_(1684)" title="Holy League (1684)">Holy League's</a> army, containing over 74,000 men from various nations, <a href="/wiki/Siege_of_Buda_(1686)" title="Siege of Buda (1686)">reconquered Buda</a> from the Turks. After some more crushing <a href="/wiki/Austro-Turkish_War_(1716%E2%80%931718)" title="Austro-Turkish War (1716–1718)">defeats of the Ottomans</a> in the next few years, the entire Kingdom of Hungary was removed from Ottoman rule by 1718. The last raid into Hungary by the Ottoman vassals <a href="/wiki/Crimean_Khanate" title="Crimean Khanate">Tatars</a> from <a href="/wiki/Crimea" title="Crimea">Crimea</a> took place in 1717.<sup id="cite_ref-80" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-80">&#91;79&#93;</a></sup> The constrained Habsburg Counter-Reformation efforts in the 17th century reconverted the majority of the kingdom to Catholicism. The ethnic composition of Hungary was fundamentally changed as a consequence of the prolonged warfare with the Turks. A large part of the country became devastated, population growth was stunted, and many smaller settlements perished.<sup id="cite_ref-81" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-81">&#91;80&#93;</a></sup> The Austrian-Habsburg government settled large groups of <a href="/wiki/Serbs" title="Serbs">Serbs</a> and other Slavs in the depopulated south, and settled <a href="/wiki/Germans_of_Hungary" title="Germans of Hungary">Germans</a> (called <a href="/wiki/Danube_Swabians" title="Danube Swabians">Danube Swabians</a>) in various areas, but Hungarians were not allowed to settle or re-settle in the south of the Carpathian Basin.<sup id="cite_ref-82" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-82">&#91;81&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span id="From_the_18th_century_to_World_War_I_.281699.E2.80.931918.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="From_the_18th_century_to_World_War_I_(1699–1918)">From the 18th century to World War I (1699–1918)</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/R%C3%A1k%C3%B3czi%27s_War_of_Independence" title="Rákóczi&#39;s War of Independence">Rákóczi's War of Independence</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Reform_Era" title="Hungarian Reform Era">Hungarian Reform Era</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Revolution_of_1848" title="Hungarian Revolution of 1848">Hungarian Revolution of 1848</a>, <a href="/wiki/Lands_of_the_Crown_of_Saint_Stephen" title="Lands of the Crown of Saint Stephen">Lands of the Crown of Saint Stephen</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Hungary_in_World_War_I" title="Hungary in World War I">Hungary in World War I</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:M%C3%A1nyoki,_%C3%81dam_-_Portrait_of_Prince_Ferenc_R%C3%A1k%C3%B3czi_II_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/M%C3%A1nyoki%2C_%C3%81dam_-_Portrait_of_Prince_Ferenc_R%C3%A1k%C3%B3czi_II_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg/170px-M%C3%A1nyoki%2C_%C3%81dam_-_Portrait_of_Prince_Ferenc_R%C3%A1k%C3%B3czi_II_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="207" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/M%C3%A1nyoki%2C_%C3%81dam_-_Portrait_of_Prince_Ferenc_R%C3%A1k%C3%B3czi_II_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg/255px-M%C3%A1nyoki%2C_%C3%81dam_-_Portrait_of_Prince_Ferenc_R%C3%A1k%C3%B3czi_II_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5d/M%C3%A1nyoki%2C_%C3%81dam_-_Portrait_of_Prince_Ferenc_R%C3%A1k%C3%B3czi_II_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg/340px-M%C3%A1nyoki%2C_%C3%81dam_-_Portrait_of_Prince_Ferenc_R%C3%A1k%C3%B3czi_II_-_Google_Art_Project.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2568" data-file-height="3124" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Francis_II_R%C3%A1k%C3%B3czi" title="Francis II Rákóczi">Francis II Rákóczi</a>, leader of the war of independence against Habsburg rule in 1703–1711</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Gr%C3%B3f_Sz%C3%A9chenyi_Istv%C3%A1n.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Gr%C3%B3f_Sz%C3%A9chenyi_Istv%C3%A1n.jpg/170px-Gr%C3%B3f_Sz%C3%A9chenyi_Istv%C3%A1n.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="217" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Gr%C3%B3f_Sz%C3%A9chenyi_Istv%C3%A1n.jpg/255px-Gr%C3%B3f_Sz%C3%A9chenyi_Istv%C3%A1n.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cb/Gr%C3%B3f_Sz%C3%A9chenyi_Istv%C3%A1n.jpg/340px-Gr%C3%B3f_Sz%C3%A9chenyi_Istv%C3%A1n.jpg 2x" data-file-width="700" data-file-height="893" /></a><figcaption>Count <a href="/wiki/Istv%C3%A1n_Sz%C3%A9chenyi" title="István Széchenyi">István Széchenyi</a> offered one year's income to establish the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Academy_of_Sciences" title="Hungarian Academy of Sciences">Hungarian Academy of Sciences</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Jakobey_Buda_ostroma_2.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Jakobey_Buda_ostroma_2.jpg/230px-Jakobey_Buda_ostroma_2.jpg" decoding="async" width="230" height="171" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Jakobey_Buda_ostroma_2.jpg/345px-Jakobey_Buda_ostroma_2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/b/bf/Jakobey_Buda_ostroma_2.jpg/460px-Jakobey_Buda_ostroma_2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1992" data-file-height="1480" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Siege_of_Buda_(1849)" title="Siege of Buda (1849)">siege of Buda</a> in May 1849</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ethnic_Map_of_Hungary_1910_with_Counties.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6e/Ethnic_Map_of_Hungary_1910_with_Counties.png/220px-Ethnic_Map_of_Hungary_1910_with_Counties.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="145" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6e/Ethnic_Map_of_Hungary_1910_with_Counties.png/330px-Ethnic_Map_of_Hungary_1910_with_Counties.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/6e/Ethnic_Map_of_Hungary_1910_with_Counties.png/440px-Ethnic_Map_of_Hungary_1910_with_Counties.png 2x" data-file-width="6208" data-file-height="4104" /></a><figcaption>Ethnic and political situation in the Kingdom of Hungary according to the 1910 census</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:E._Poulton_Portrait_of_Lajos_Kossuth_1860s.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5a/E._Poulton_Portrait_of_Lajos_Kossuth_1860s.jpg/170px-E._Poulton_Portrait_of_Lajos_Kossuth_1860s.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="220" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5a/E._Poulton_Portrait_of_Lajos_Kossuth_1860s.jpg/255px-E._Poulton_Portrait_of_Lajos_Kossuth_1860s.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/5a/E._Poulton_Portrait_of_Lajos_Kossuth_1860s.jpg/340px-E._Poulton_Portrait_of_Lajos_Kossuth_1860s.jpg 2x" data-file-width="445" data-file-height="577" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Lajos_Kossuth" title="Lajos Kossuth">Lajos Kossuth</a>, Regent-President during the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Revolution_of_1848" title="Hungarian Revolution of 1848">Hungarian Revolution of 1848</a></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Austria-Hungary_map.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/Austria-Hungary_map.svg/170px-Austria-Hungary_map.svg.png" decoding="async" width="170" height="131" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/Austria-Hungary_map.svg/255px-Austria-Hungary_map.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/66/Austria-Hungary_map.svg/340px-Austria-Hungary_map.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="783" data-file-height="605" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Lands_of_the_Crown_of_Saint_Stephen" title="Lands of the Crown of Saint Stephen">Lands of the Crown of Saint Stephen</a> consisted of the territories of the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary" title="Kingdom of Hungary">Kingdom of Hungary</a> (16) and the <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Croatia-Slavonia" title="Kingdom of Croatia-Slavonia">Kingdom of Croatia-Slavonia</a> (17).</figcaption></figure>
<p>Between 1703 and 1711, there was a large-scale <a href="/wiki/R%C3%A1k%C3%B3czi%27s_War_of_Independence" title="Rákóczi&#39;s War of Independence">war of independence</a> led by <a href="/wiki/Francis_II_R%C3%A1k%C3%B3czi" title="Francis II Rákóczi">Francis II Rákóczi</a>, who after the dethronement of the Habsburgs in 1707 at the Diet of <a href="/wiki/%C3%93nod" title="Ónod">Ónod</a>, took power provisionally as the ruling prince for the wartime period, but refused the Hungarian crown and the title "king". The uprisings lasted for years. The Hungarian <a href="/wiki/Kuruc" title="Kuruc">Kuruc</a> army, although taking over most of the country, lost the main <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Tren%C4%8D%C3%ADn" title="Battle of Trenčín">battle at Trencsén</a> (1708). Three years later, because of the growing desertion, defeatism, and low morale, the Kuruc forces finally surrendered.<sup id="cite_ref-83" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-83">&#91;82&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During the <a href="/wiki/Napoleonic_Wars" title="Napoleonic Wars">Napoleonic Wars</a> and afterward, the Hungarian Diet had not convened for decades.<sup id="cite_ref-84" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-84">&#91;83&#93;</a></sup> In the 1820s, the emperor was forced to convene the Diet, which marked the beginning of a Reform Period (1825–1848, <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_language" title="Hungarian language">Hungarian</a>: <i lang="hu">reformkor</i>). Count <a href="/wiki/Istv%C3%A1n_Sz%C3%A9chenyi" title="István Széchenyi">István Széchenyi</a>, one of the most prominent statesmen of the country, recognised the urgent need for modernisation and his message got through. The Hungarian Parliament was reconvened in 1825 to handle financial needs. A liberal party emerged and focused on providing for the peasantry. <a href="/wiki/Lajos_Kossuth" title="Lajos Kossuth">Lajos Kossuth</a>—a famous journalist at that time—emerged as a leader of the lower <a href="/wiki/Gentry" title="Gentry">gentry</a> in the Parliament. A remarkable upswing started as the nation concentrated its forces on modernisation even though the Habsburg monarchs obstructed all important liberal laws relating to <a href="/wiki/Civil_and_political_rights" title="Civil and political rights">civil and political rights</a> and economic reforms. Many reformers (<a href="/wiki/Lajos_Kossuth" title="Lajos Kossuth">Lajos Kossuth</a>, <a href="/wiki/Mih%C3%A1ly_T%C3%A1ncsics" title="Mihály Táncsics">Mihály Táncsics</a>) were imprisoned by the authorities.
</p><p>On 15 March 1848, mass demonstrations in Pest and Buda enabled Hungarian reformists to push through a list of <a href="/wiki/12_points_of_the_Hungarian_Revolutionaries_of_1848" title="12 points of the Hungarian Revolutionaries of 1848">12 demands</a>. Under Governor and President <a href="/wiki/Lajos_Kossuth" title="Lajos Kossuth">Lajos Kossuth</a> and Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Lajos_Batthy%C3%A1ny" title="Lajos Batthyány">Lajos Batthyány</a>, the House of Habsburg was dethroned. The Habsburg ruler and his advisors skillfully manipulated the Croatian, Serbian and Romanian peasantry, led by priests and officers firmly loyal to the Habsburgs, and induced them to rebel against the Hungarian government, though the Hungarians were supported by the vast majority of the Slovak, German and <a href="/wiki/Rusyns" title="Rusyns">Rusyn</a> nationalities and by all the Jews of the kingdom, as well as by a large number of Polish, Austrian and Italian volunteers.<sup id="cite_ref-85" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-85">&#91;84&#93;</a></sup> In July 1849 the Hungarian Parliament proclaimed and enacted the first laws of ethnic and <a href="/wiki/Minority_rights" title="Minority rights">minority rights</a> in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-86" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-86">&#91;85&#93;</a></sup> Many members of the nationalities gained the coveted highest positions within the Hungarian Army, like General <a href="/wiki/J%C3%A1nos_Damjanich" title="János Damjanich">János Damjanich</a>, an ethnic Serb who became a Hungarian national hero through his command of the 3rd Hungarian Army Corps or <a href="/wiki/J%C3%B3zef_Bem" title="Józef Bem">Józef Bem</a>, who was Polish and also became a national hero in Hungary. The Hungarian forces (<i><a href="/wiki/Royal_Hungarian_Landwehr" class="mw-redirect" title="Royal Hungarian Landwehr">Honvédség</a></i>) defeated Austrian armies. To counter the successes of the Hungarian revolutionary army, Habsburg Emperor <a href="/wiki/Franz_Joseph_I_of_Austria" title="Franz Joseph I of Austria">Franz Joseph I</a> asked for help from the "Gendarme of Europe", Tsar <a href="/wiki/Nicholas_I_of_Russia" title="Nicholas I of Russia">Nicholas I</a>, whose Russian armies invaded Hungary. This made <a href="/wiki/Art%C3%BAr_G%C3%B6rgei" title="Artúr Görgei">Artúr Görgey</a> surrender in August 1849. The leader of the Austrian army, <a href="/wiki/Julius_Jacob_von_Haynau" title="Julius Jacob von Haynau">Julius Jacob von Haynau</a>, became governor of Hungary for a few months and ordered the execution of <a href="/wiki/The_13_Martyrs_of_Arad" title="The 13 Martyrs of Arad">the 13 Martyrs of Arad</a>, leaders of the Hungarian army, and Prime Minister Batthyány in October 1849. Kossuth escaped into exile. Following the war of 1848–1849, the whole country was in "passive resistance".
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ferenc_J%C3%B3zsef_koron%C3%A1z%C3%A1sa_Bud%C3%A1n.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/18/Ferenc_J%C3%B3zsef_koron%C3%A1z%C3%A1sa_Bud%C3%A1n.jpg/220px-Ferenc_J%C3%B3zsef_koron%C3%A1z%C3%A1sa_Bud%C3%A1n.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="142" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/18/Ferenc_J%C3%B3zsef_koron%C3%A1z%C3%A1sa_Bud%C3%A1n.jpg/330px-Ferenc_J%C3%B3zsef_koron%C3%A1z%C3%A1sa_Bud%C3%A1n.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/18/Ferenc_J%C3%B3zsef_koron%C3%A1z%C3%A1sa_Bud%C3%A1n.jpg/440px-Ferenc_J%C3%B3zsef_koron%C3%A1z%C3%A1sa_Bud%C3%A1n.jpg 2x" data-file-width="894" data-file-height="577" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Coronation_of_the_Hungarian_monarch" title="Coronation of the Hungarian monarch">Coronation</a> of <a href="/wiki/Franz_Joseph_I_of_Austria" title="Franz Joseph I of Austria">Francis Joseph I</a> and <a href="/wiki/Empress_Elisabeth_of_Austria" title="Empress Elisabeth of Austria">Elisabeth Amalie</a> at <a href="/wiki/Matthias_Church" title="Matthias Church">Matthias Church</a>, <a href="/wiki/Buda" title="Buda">Buda</a>, 8 June 1867</figcaption></figure>
<p>Because of external and internal problems, reforms seemed inevitable, and major military defeats of Austria forced the Habsburgs to negotiate the <a href="/wiki/Austro-Hungarian_Compromise_of_1867" title="Austro-Hungarian Compromise of 1867">Austro-Hungarian Compromise of 1867</a>, by which the dual monarchy of <a href="/wiki/Austria-Hungary" title="Austria-Hungary">Austria-Hungary</a> was formed. This empire had the second largest area in Europe (after the <a href="/wiki/Russian_Empire" title="Russian Empire">Russian Empire</a>), and it was the third most populous (after Russia and the <a href="/wiki/German_Empire" title="German Empire">German Empire</a>). The two realms were governed separately by two parliaments from two capital cities, with a common monarch and common external and military policies. Economically, the empire was a customs union. The old Hungarian Constitution was restored, and Franz Joseph I was crowned as <a href="/wiki/King_of_Hungary" title="King of Hungary">King of Hungary</a>. The era witnessed impressive economic development. The formerly backward Hungarian economy became relatively modern and industrialised by the turn of the 20th century, although agriculture remained dominant until 1890. In 1873, the old capital Buda and <a href="/wiki/%C3%93buda" title="Óbuda">Óbuda</a> were officially united with <a href="/wiki/Pest,_Hungary" title="Pest, Hungary">Pest</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-87" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-87">&#91;86&#93;</a></sup> thus creating the new metropolis of Budapest. Many of the state institutions and the modern administrative system of Hungary were established during this period.
</p><p>After the <a href="/wiki/Assassination_of_Archduke_Franz_Ferdinand" title="Assassination of Archduke Franz Ferdinand">assassination of Archduke Franz Ferdinand</a> in Sarajevo, Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Istv%C3%A1n_Tisza" title="István Tisza">István Tisza</a> and his cabinet tried to avoid the outbreak and escalating of a war in Europe, but their diplomatic efforts were unsuccessful. Austria-Hungary drafted 9&#160;million (fighting forces: 7.8&#160;million) soldiers in World War I (over 4&#160;million from the Kingdom of Hungary) on the side of Germany, Bulgaria, and Turkey. The troops raised in the Kingdom of Hungary spent little time defending the actual territory of Hungary, with the exceptions of the <a href="/wiki/Brusilov_offensive" title="Brusilov offensive">Brusilov offensive</a> in June 1916 and a few months later when the Romanian army made an attack into Transylvania,<sup id="cite_ref-88" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-88">&#91;87&#93;</a></sup><sup class="noprint Inline-Template" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability#Self-published_sources" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability"><span title="The material near this tag may rely on a self-published source. (January 2021)">self-published source?</span></a></i>&#93;</sup> both of which were repelled. The <a href="/wiki/Central_Powers" title="Central Powers">Central Powers</a> conquered Serbia. Romania declared war. The Central Powers conquered southern Romania and the Romanian capital <a href="/wiki/Bucharest" title="Bucharest">Bucharest</a>. In 1916 Emperor Joseph died, and the new monarch <a href="/wiki/Charles_I_of_Austria" title="Charles I of Austria">Charles IV</a> sympathised with the pacifists. With great difficulty, the Central Powers stopped and repelled the attacks of the Russian Empire.
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Front_(World_War_I)" title="Eastern Front (World War I)">Eastern Front</a> of the Allied (<a href="/wiki/Allies_of_World_War_I" title="Allies of World War I">Entente</a>) Powers completely collapsed. The Austro-Hungarian Empire then withdrew from all defeated countries. On the Italian front, the Austro-Hungarian army made no progress against Italy after January 1918. Despite great success on the Eastern Front, Germany suffered complete defeat on the <a href="/wiki/Western_Front_(World_War_I)" title="Western Front (World War I)">Western Front</a>. By 1918, the economic situation had deteriorated (strikes in factories were organised by leftist and pacifist movements) and uprisings in the army had become commonplace. In the capital cities, the Austrian and Hungarian leftist liberal movements (the maverick parties) and their leaders supported the separatism of ethnic minorities. Austria-Hungary signed a general armistice in <a href="/wiki/Padua" title="Padua">Padua</a> on 3 November 1918.<sup id="cite_ref-89" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-89">&#91;88&#93;</a></sup> In October 1918, Hungary's union with Austria was dissolved.
</p>
<h3><span id="Between_the_World_Wars_.281918.E2.80.931941.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Between_the_World_Wars_(1918–1941)">Between the World Wars (1918–1941)</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Hungary_between_the_World_Wars" title="Hungary between the World Wars">Hungary between the World Wars</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_interwar_economy" title="Hungarian interwar economy">Hungarian interwar economy</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Trianon" title="Treaty of Trianon">Treaty of Trianon</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Trianon_consequences.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d3/Trianon_consequences.png/220px-Trianon_consequences.png" decoding="async" width="220" height="149" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d3/Trianon_consequences.png/330px-Trianon_consequences.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/d3/Trianon_consequences.png/440px-Trianon_consequences.png 2x" data-file-width="1140" data-file-height="774" /></a><figcaption>With the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Trianon" title="Treaty of Trianon">Treaty of Trianon</a>, Hungary lost 72% of its territory, its sea ports, and 3,425,000 ethnic Hungarians.<sup id="cite_ref-90" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-90">&#91;89&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-91" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-91">&#91;90&#93;</a></sup><style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r981673959">.mw-parser-output .legend{page-break-inside:avoid;break-inside:avoid-column}.mw-parser-output .legend-color{display:inline-block;min-width:1.25em;height:1.25em;line-height:1.25;margin:1px 0;text-align:center;border:1px solid black;background-color:transparent;color:black}.mw-parser-output .legend-text{}</style><div class="legend"><span class="legend-color mw-no-invert" style="background-color:#cd3318; color:white;">&#160;</span>&#160;Majority Hungarian areas (according to the 1910 census) detached from Hungary</div></figcaption></figure>
<p>Following the First World War, Hungary underwent a period of profound political upheaval, beginning with the <a href="/wiki/Aster_Revolution" title="Aster Revolution">Aster Revolution</a> in 1918, which brought the social-democratic <a href="/wiki/Mih%C3%A1ly_K%C3%A1rolyi" title="Mihály Károlyi">Mihály Károlyi</a> to power as prime minister. The <a href="/wiki/Royal_Hungarian_Honv%C3%A9d" title="Royal Hungarian Honvéd">Hungarian Royal Honvéd army</a> still had more than 1,400,000 soldiers<sup id="cite_ref-92" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-92">&#91;91&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-93" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-93">&#91;92&#93;</a></sup> when Károlyi was installed. Károlyi yielded to U.S. President <a href="/wiki/Woodrow_Wilson" title="Woodrow Wilson">Woodrow Wilson</a>'s demand for <a href="/wiki/Pacifism" title="Pacifism">pacifism</a> by ordering the disarmament of the Hungarian army. This happened under the direction of <a href="/wiki/B%C3%A9la_Linder" title="Béla Linder">Béla Linder</a>, minister of war in the Károlyi government.<sup id="cite_ref-Dixon_1986_94-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Dixon_1986-94">&#91;93&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-Sharp_2008_95-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Sharp_2008-95">&#91;94&#93;</a></sup> Disarmament of its army meant that Hungary was to remain without a national defence at a time of particular vulnerability. During the rule of Károlyi's pacifist cabinet, Hungary lost control over approximately 75% of its former pre-WW1 territories (325,411 square kilometres (125,642&#160;sq&#160;mi)) without a fight and was subject to foreign occupation. The <a href="/wiki/Little_Entente" title="Little Entente">Little Entente</a>, sensing an opportunity, invaded the country from three sides—<a href="/wiki/Hungarian-Romanian_War" class="mw-redirect" title="Hungarian-Romanian War">Romania invaded Transylvania</a>, Czechoslovakia annexed <a href="/wiki/Upper_Hungary" title="Upper Hungary">Upper Hungary</a> (today's Slovakia), and a joint <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Serbia" title="Kingdom of Serbia">Serb</a>-<a href="/wiki/French_Army" title="French Army">French</a> coalition annexed <a href="/wiki/Vojvodina" title="Vojvodina">Vojvodina</a> and other southern regions. In March 1919, communists led by <a href="/wiki/B%C3%A9la_Kun" title="Béla Kun">Béla Kun</a> ousted the Károlyi government and proclaimed the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Soviet_Republic" title="Hungarian Soviet Republic">Hungarian Soviet Republic</a> (<i>Tanácsköztársaság</i>), followed by a thorough <a href="/wiki/Red_Terror_(Hungary)" title="Red Terror (Hungary)">Red Terror</a> campaign. Despite some successes on the Czechoslovak front, Kun's forces were ultimately unable to resist the Romanian invasion; by August 1919, Romanian troops occupied Budapest and ousted Kun.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Portraits_de_Mikl%C3%B3s_Horthy.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Portraits_de_Mikl%C3%B3s_Horthy.jpg/170px-Portraits_de_Mikl%C3%B3s_Horthy.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="229" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Portraits_de_Mikl%C3%B3s_Horthy.jpg/255px-Portraits_de_Mikl%C3%B3s_Horthy.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/ec/Portraits_de_Mikl%C3%B3s_Horthy.jpg/340px-Portraits_de_Mikl%C3%B3s_Horthy.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1416" data-file-height="1904" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Mikl%C3%B3s_Horthy" title="Miklós Horthy">Miklós Horthy</a>, Regent of the Kingdom of Hungary (1920–1944)</figcaption></figure>
<p>In November 1919, rightist forces led by former Austro-Hungarian admiral <a href="/wiki/Mikl%C3%B3s_Horthy" title="Miklós Horthy">Miklós Horthy</a> entered Budapest; exhausted by the war and its aftermath, the populace accepted Horthy's leadership. In January 1920, parliamentary elections were held, and Horthy was proclaimed regent of the reestablished <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Hungary_(1920%E2%80%931946)" title="Kingdom of Hungary (1920–1946)">Kingdom of Hungary</a>, inaugurating the so-called "Horthy era" (<i>Horthy-kor</i>). The new government worked quickly to normalise foreign relations while turning a blind eye to a <a href="/wiki/White_Terror_(Hungary)" title="White Terror (Hungary)">White Terror</a> that swept through the countryside; extrajudicial killings of suspected communists and Jews lasted well into 1920. On 4 June 1920, the <a href="/wiki/Treaty_of_Trianon" title="Treaty of Trianon">Treaty of Trianon</a> established new borders for Hungary. The country lost 71% of its territory and 66% of its pre-war population, as well as many sources of raw materials and its sole port at <a href="/wiki/Rijeka" title="Rijeka">Fiume</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Macartney37_96-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Macartney37-96">&#91;95&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-97" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-97">&#91;96&#93;</a></sup> Though the revision of the treaty quickly rose to the top of the national political agenda, the Horthy government was not willing to resort to military intervention to do so.
</p><p>The initial years of the Horthy regime were preoccupied with putsch attempts by <a href="/wiki/Charles_IV_of_Hungary" class="mw-redirect" title="Charles IV of Hungary">Charles IV</a>, the Austro-Hungarian <a href="/wiki/Pretender" title="Pretender">pretender</a>; continued suppression of communists; and a migration crisis triggered by the Trianon territorial changes. Though free elections continued, Horthy's personality and those of his personally selected prime ministers dominated the political scene. The government's actions continued to drift right with the passage of <a href="/wiki/Antisemitism" title="Antisemitism">antisemitic</a> laws and, because of the continued isolation of the Little Entente, economic and then political gravitation towards <a href="/wiki/Kingdom_of_Italy#Fascist_regime_(1922–1943)" title="Kingdom of Italy">Italy</a> and <a href="/wiki/Nazi_Germany" title="Nazi Germany">Germany</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Great_Depression" title="Great Depression">Great Depression</a> further exacerbated the situation, and the popularity of fascist politicians increased, such as <a href="/wiki/Gyula_G%C3%B6mb%C3%B6s" title="Gyula Gömbös">Gyula Gömbös</a> and <a href="/wiki/Ferenc_Sz%C3%A1lasi" title="Ferenc Szálasi">Ferenc Szálasi</a>, promising economic and social recovery.
</p><p>Horthy's nationalist agenda reached its apogee in 1938 and 1940, when the Nazis rewarded Hungary's staunchly pro-Germany foreign policy in the <a href="/wiki/First_Vienna_Award" title="First Vienna Award">First</a> and <a href="/wiki/Second_Vienna_Award" title="Second Vienna Award">Second Vienna Awards</a>, peacefully restoring ethnic-Hungarian-majority areas lost after Trianon. In 1939, Hungary regained further territory from Czechoslovakia <a href="/wiki/Slovak%E2%80%93Hungarian_War" title="Slovak–Hungarian War">through force</a>. Hungary <a href="/wiki/Tripartite_Pact" title="Tripartite Pact">formally joined</a> the <a href="/wiki/Axis_powers" title="Axis powers">Axis powers</a> on 20 November 1940 and in 1941 participated in the <a href="/wiki/Invasion_of_Yugoslavia" title="Invasion of Yugoslavia">invasion of Yugoslavia</a>, gaining some of its former territories in the south.
</p>
<h3><span id="World_War_II_.281941.E2.80.931945.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="World_War_II_(1941–1945)">World War II (1941–1945)</span></h3>
