<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Hungary_during_World_War_II" class="mw-redirect" title="Hungary during World War II">Hungary during World War II</a>, <a href="/wiki/Holocaust_in_Hungary" class="mw-redirect" title="Holocaust in Hungary">Holocaust in Hungary</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Soviet_occupation_of_Hungary" class="mw-redirect" title="Soviet occupation of Hungary">Soviet occupation of Hungary</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Hungary_1941-44_Administrative_Map.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Hungary_1941-44_Administrative_Map.png/310px-Hungary_1941-44_Administrative_Map.png" decoding="async" width="310" height="167" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Hungary_1941-44_Administrative_Map.png/465px-Hungary_1941-44_Administrative_Map.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1f/Hungary_1941-44_Administrative_Map.png/620px-Hungary_1941-44_Administrative_Map.png 2x" data-file-width="4000" data-file-height="2157" /></a><figcaption>Kingdom of Hungary, 1941–1944</figcaption></figure>
<p>Hungary formally entered World War II as an Axis power on 26 June 1941, declaring war on the Soviet Union after unidentified planes bombed <a href="/wiki/Ko%C5%A1ice" title="Košice">Kassa</a>, <a href="/wiki/Munkacheve" class="mw-redirect" title="Munkacheve">Munkács</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Rakhiv" title="Rakhiv">Rahó</a>. Hungarian troops fought on the <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Front_(World_War_II)" title="Eastern Front (World War II)">Eastern Front</a> for two years. Despite early success at the <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Uman" title="Battle of Uman">Battle of Uman</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-J._Lee_Ready_1995_page_130_98-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-J._Lee_Ready_1995_page_130-98">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> the government began seeking a secret peace pact with <a href="/wiki/Allies_of_World_War_II" title="Allies of World War II">the Allies</a> after the <a href="/wiki/Second_Army_(Hungary)" title="Second Army (Hungary)">Second Army</a> suffered catastrophic losses <a href="/wiki/Ostrogozhsk%E2%80%93Rossosh_offensive" title="Ostrogozhsk–Rossosh offensive">at the River Don</a> in January 1943. Learning of the planned defection, German troops <a href="/wiki/Operation_Margarethe" class="mw-redirect" title="Operation Margarethe">occupied Hungary</a> on 19 March 1944 to guarantee Horthy's compliance. In October, as the Soviet front approached, and the government made further efforts to disengage from the war, German troops ousted Horthy and installed a puppet government under Szálasi's fascist <a href="/wiki/Arrow_Cross_Party" title="Arrow Cross Party">Arrow Cross Party</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-J._Lee_Ready_1995_page_130_98-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-J._Lee_Ready_1995_page_130-98">&#91;97&#93;</a></sup> Szálasi pledged all the country's capabilities in service of the German war machine. By October 1944, the Soviets had reached the river Tisza, and despite <a href="/wiki/Battle_of_Debrecen" title="Battle of Debrecen">some losses</a>, succeeded in encircling and <a href="/wiki/Siege_of_Budapest" title="Siege of Budapest">besieging Budapest</a> in December.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Bundesarchiv_Bild_101I-680-8285A-08,_Budapest,_Festnahme_von_Juden.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/64/Bundesarchiv_Bild_101I-680-8285A-08%2C_Budapest%2C_Festnahme_von_Juden.jpg/220px-Bundesarchiv_Bild_101I-680-8285A-08%2C_Budapest%2C_Festnahme_von_Juden.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="142" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/64/Bundesarchiv_Bild_101I-680-8285A-08%2C_Budapest%2C_Festnahme_von_Juden.jpg/330px-Bundesarchiv_Bild_101I-680-8285A-08%2C_Budapest%2C_Festnahme_von_Juden.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/64/Bundesarchiv_Bild_101I-680-8285A-08%2C_Budapest%2C_Festnahme_von_Juden.jpg/440px-Bundesarchiv_Bild_101I-680-8285A-08%2C_Budapest%2C_Festnahme_von_Juden.jpg 2x" data-file-width="796" data-file-height="512" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Jewish" class="mw-redirect" title="Jewish">Jewish</a> women being arrested on Wesselényi Street in <a href="/wiki/Budapest" title="Budapest">Budapest</a> during <a href="/wiki/The_Holocaust" title="The Holocaust">the Holocaust</a>, <abbr title="circa">c.</abbr> 20–22 October 1944</figcaption></figure>
<p>After German occupation, Hungary participated in <a href="/wiki/The_Holocaust" title="The Holocaust">the Holocaust</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-bbc-no-warning-to-hungary-jews_99-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-bbc-no-warning-to-hungary-jews-99">&#91;98&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-100" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-100">&#91;99&#93;</a></sup> During the German occupation in May–June 1944, the Arrow Cross and Hungarian police deported nearly 440,000 Jews, mainly to <a href="/wiki/Auschwitz_concentration_camp" title="Auschwitz concentration camp">Auschwitz</a>. Nearly all of them were murdered.<sup id="cite_ref-101" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-101">&#91;100&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-102" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-102">&#91;101&#93;</a></sup> The Swedish Diplomat <a href="/wiki/Raoul_Wallenberg" title="Raoul Wallenberg">Raoul Wallenberg</a> managed to save a considerable number of Hungarian Jews by giving them Swedish passports.<sup id="cite_ref-103" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-103">&#91;102&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Rezs%C5%91_Kasztner" title="Rezső Kasztner">Rezső Kasztner</a>, one of the leaders of the Hungarian <a href="/wiki/Aid_and_Rescue_Committee" title="Aid and Rescue Committee">Aid and Rescue Committee</a>, bribed senior <a href="/wiki/Schutzstaffel" title="Schutzstaffel">SS officers</a> such as <a href="/wiki/Adolf_Eichmann" title="Adolf Eichmann">Adolf Eichmann</a> to allow some Jews to escape.<sup id="cite_ref-104" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-104">&#91;103&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-105" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-105">&#91;104&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-106" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-106">&#91;105&#93;</a></sup> The Horthy government's complicity in the Holocaust remains a point of controversy and contention.
</p><p>The war left Hungary devastated, destroying over 60% of the economy and causing significant <a href="/wiki/World_War_II_casualties#endnote_Hungary" title="World War II casualties">loss of life</a>. In addition to the over 600,000 Hungarian Jews killed,<sup id="cite_ref-ind09/96_107-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-ind09/96-107">&#91;106&#93;</a></sup> as many as 280,000<sup id="cite_ref-EU38_108-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-EU38-108">&#91;107&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-109" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-109">&#91;108&#93;</a></sup> other Hungarians were raped, murdered and executed or deported for slave labour by Czechoslovaks,<sup id="cite_ref-110" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-110">&#91;109&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-111" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-111">&#91;110&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-112" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-112">&#91;111&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-113" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-113">&#91;112&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-114" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-114">&#91;113&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-115" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-115">&#91;114&#93;</a></sup> Soviet <a href="/wiki/Red_Army" title="Red Army">Red Army</a> troops,<sup id="cite_ref-116" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-116">&#91;115&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-117" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-117">&#91;116&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-118" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-118">&#91;117&#93;</a></sup> and Yugoslavs.<sup id="cite_ref-119" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-119">&#91;118&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>On 13 February 1945, Budapest surrendered; by April, German troops left the country under Soviet military occupation. 200,000 Hungarians were expelled from Czechoslovakia in exchange for 70,000 Slovaks living in Hungary. 202,000 ethnic Germans were expelled to Germany,<sup id="cite_ref-120" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-120">&#91;119&#93;</a></sup> and through the 1947 <a href="/wiki/Paris_Peace_Treaties,_1947" title="Paris Peace Treaties, 1947">Paris Peace Treaties</a>, Hungary was again reduced to its immediate post-Trianon borders.
</p>
<h3><span id="Communism_.281945.E2.80.931989.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Communism_(1945–1989)">Communism (1945–1989)</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Republic_(1946%E2%80%931949)" class="mw-redirect" title="Hungarian Republic (1946–1949)">Hungarian Republic (1946–1949)</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_People%27s_Republic" title="Hungarian People&#39;s Republic">Hungarian People's Republic</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Revolution_of_1956" title="Hungarian Revolution of 1956">Hungarian Revolution of 1956</a></div>
<p>Following the defeat of Nazi Germany, Hungary became a <a href="/wiki/Satellite_state" title="Satellite state">satellite state</a> of the Soviet Union. The Soviet leadership selected <a href="/wiki/M%C3%A1ty%C3%A1s_R%C3%A1kosi" title="Mátyás Rákosi">Mátyás Rákosi</a> to front the <a href="/wiki/Stalinism" title="Stalinism">Stalinisation</a> of the country, and Rákosi <i>de facto</i> ruled Hungary from 1949 to 1956. His government's policies of militarisation, industrialisation, collectivisation, and war compensation led to a severe decline in living standards. In imitation of Stalin's <a href="/wiki/KGB" title="KGB">KGB</a>, the Rákosi government established a secret political police, the <a href="/wiki/State_Protection_Authority" title="State Protection Authority">ÁVH</a>, to enforce the regime. In the ensuing purges, approximately 350,000 officials and intellectuals were imprisoned or executed from 1948 to 1956.<sup id="cite_ref-121" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-121">&#91;120&#93;</a></sup> Many freethinkers, democrats, and Horthy-era dignitaries were secretly arrested and extrajudicially interned in domestic and foreign <a href="/wiki/Gulag" title="Gulag">gulags</a>. Some 600,000 Hungarians were deported to Soviet labour camps, where at least 200,000 died.<sup id="cite_ref-122" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-122">&#91;121&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Sz%C3%A9tl%C5%91tt_harckocsi_a_M%C3%B3ricz_Zsigmond_k%C3%B6rt%C3%A9ren.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Sz%C3%A9tl%C5%91tt_harckocsi_a_M%C3%B3ricz_Zsigmond_k%C3%B6rt%C3%A9ren.jpg/220px-Sz%C3%A9tl%C5%91tt_harckocsi_a_M%C3%B3ricz_Zsigmond_k%C3%B6rt%C3%A9ren.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="152" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Sz%C3%A9tl%C5%91tt_harckocsi_a_M%C3%B3ricz_Zsigmond_k%C3%B6rt%C3%A9ren.jpg/330px-Sz%C3%A9tl%C5%91tt_harckocsi_a_M%C3%B3ricz_Zsigmond_k%C3%B6rt%C3%A9ren.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/07/Sz%C3%A9tl%C5%91tt_harckocsi_a_M%C3%B3ricz_Zsigmond_k%C3%B6rt%C3%A9ren.jpg/440px-Sz%C3%A9tl%C5%91tt_harckocsi_a_M%C3%B3ricz_Zsigmond_k%C3%B6rt%C3%A9ren.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2243" data-file-height="1552" /></a><figcaption>A destroyed Soviet tank in Budapest during the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Revolution_of_1956" title="Hungarian Revolution of 1956">Revolution of 1956</a>. <i><a href="/wiki/Time_(magazine)" title="Time (magazine)">Time</a></i><span class="nowrap" style="padding-left:0.1em;">&#39;</span>s <a href="/wiki/Time_Magazine_Person_of_the_Year" class="mw-redirect" title="Time Magazine Person of the Year">Man of the Year</a> for 1956 was the Hungarian freedom fighter.<sup id="cite_ref-123" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-123">&#91;122&#93;</a></sup></figcaption></figure>
<p>After Stalin's death in 1953, the Soviet Union pursued a programme of <a href="/wiki/De-Stalinization" title="De-Stalinization">de-Stalinisation</a> that was inimical to Rákosi, leading to his deposition. The following political cooling saw the ascent of <a href="/wiki/Imre_Nagy" title="Imre Nagy">Imre Nagy</a> to the premiership and the growing interest of students and intellectuals in political life. Nagy promised market liberalisation and political openness, while Rákosi opposed both vigorously. Rákosi eventually managed to discredit Nagy and replace him with the more hard-line <a href="/wiki/Ern%C5%91_Ger%C5%91" title="Ernő Gerő">Ernő Gerő</a>. Hungary joined the <a href="/wiki/Warsaw_Pact" title="Warsaw Pact">Warsaw Pact</a> in May 1955, as societal dissatisfaction with the regime swelled. Following the firing on peaceful demonstrations by Soviet soldiers and secret police, and rallies throughout the country on 23 October 1956, protesters took to the streets in Budapest, initiating the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Revolution_of_1956" title="Hungarian Revolution of 1956">1956 Revolution</a>. In an effort to quell the chaos, Nagy returned as premier, promised free elections, and took Hungary out of the Warsaw Pact.
</p><p>The violence nonetheless continued as revolutionary militias sprung up against the Soviet Army and the ÁVH; the roughly 3,000-strong resistance fought Soviet tanks using <a href="/wiki/Molotov_cocktail" title="Molotov cocktail">Molotov cocktails</a> and machine-pistols. Though the preponderance of the Soviets was immense, they suffered heavy losses, and by 30 October 1956, most Soviet troops had withdrawn from Budapest to garrison the countryside. For a time, the Soviet leadership was unsure how to respond but eventually decided to intervene to prevent a destabilisation of the Soviet bloc. On 4 November, reinforcements of more than 150,000 troops and 2,500 tanks entered the country from the Soviet Union.<sup id="cite_ref-124" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-124">&#91;123&#93;</a></sup> Nearly 20,000 Hungarians were killed resisting the intervention, while an additional 21,600 were imprisoned afterward for political reasons. Some 13,000 were interned and 230 brought to trial and executed. Nagy was secretly tried, found guilty, sentenced to death, and executed by hanging in June 1958. Because borders were briefly opened, nearly a quarter of a million people fled the country by the time the revolution was suppressed.<sup id="cite_ref-125" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-125">&#91;124&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h4><span id="K.C3.A1d.C3.A1r_era_.281956.E2.80.931988.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Kádár_era_(1956–1988)">Kádár era (1956–1988)</span></h4>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Goulash_Communism" title="Goulash Communism">Goulash Communism</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:J%C3%A1nos_K%C3%A1d%C3%A1r_1962.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/J%C3%A1nos_K%C3%A1d%C3%A1r_1962.jpg/170px-J%C3%A1nos_K%C3%A1d%C3%A1r_1962.jpg" decoding="async" width="170" height="235" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/J%C3%A1nos_K%C3%A1d%C3%A1r_1962.jpg/255px-J%C3%A1nos_K%C3%A1d%C3%A1r_1962.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/39/J%C3%A1nos_K%C3%A1d%C3%A1r_1962.jpg/340px-J%C3%A1nos_K%C3%A1d%C3%A1r_1962.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1380" data-file-height="1908" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/J%C3%A1nos_K%C3%A1d%C3%A1r" title="János Kádár">János Kádár</a>, General Secretary of the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Socialist_Workers%27_Party" title="Hungarian Socialist Workers&#39; Party">Hungarian Socialist Workers' Party</a> (1956–1988)</figcaption></figure>
<p>After a second, briefer period of Soviet military occupation, <a href="/wiki/J%C3%A1nos_K%C3%A1d%C3%A1r" title="János Kádár">János Kádár</a>, Nagy's former minister of state, was chosen by the Soviet leadership to head the new government and chair the new ruling <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Socialist_Workers%27_Party" title="Hungarian Socialist Workers&#39; Party">Socialist Workers' Party</a>. Kádár quickly normalised the situation. In 1963, the government granted a general amnesty and released the majority of those imprisoned for their active participation in the uprising. Kádár proclaimed a new policy line, according to which the people were no longer compelled to profess loyalty to the party if they tacitly accepted the socialist regime as a fact of life. In many speeches, he described this as, "Those who are not against us are with us." Kádár introduced new planning priorities in the economy, such as allowing farmers significant plots of private land within the collective farm system (<i>háztáji gazdálkodás</i>). The living standard rose as consumer goods and food production took precedence over military production, which was reduced to one-tenth of prerevolutionary levels.
</p><p>In 1968, the <a href="/wiki/New_Economic_Mechanism" title="New Economic Mechanism">New Economic Mechanism</a> introduced free-market elements into the socialist command economy. From the 1960s through the late 1980s, Hungary was often referred to as "the happiest barrack" within the Eastern bloc. During the latter part of the Cold War Hungary's <a href="/wiki/Lists_of_countries_by_GDP_per_capita" title="Lists of countries by GDP per capita">GDP per capita</a> was fourth only to <a href="/wiki/East_Germany" title="East Germany">East Germany</a>, <a href="/wiki/Czechoslovak_Socialist_Republic" title="Czechoslovak Socialist Republic">Czechoslovakia</a>, and the Soviet Union.<sup id="cite_ref-madison185_126-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-madison185-126">&#91;125&#93;</a></sup> As a result of this relatively high <a href="/wiki/Standard_of_living" title="Standard of living">standard of living</a>, a more liberalised economy, a less censored press, and less restricted travel rights, Hungary was generally considered one of the more liberal countries in which to live in Central Europe during communism. In 1980, Hungary sent a <a href="/wiki/Cosmonaut" class="mw-redirect" title="Cosmonaut">Cosmonaut</a> into space as part of the <a href="/wiki/Interkosmos" title="Interkosmos">Interkosmos</a>. The first Hungarian astronaut was <a href="/wiki/Bertalan_Farkas" title="Bertalan Farkas">Bertalan Farkas</a>. Hungary became <a href="/wiki/Timeline_of_space_travel_by_nationality" title="Timeline of space travel by nationality">the seventh nation to be represented in space</a> by him.<sup id="cite_ref-127" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-127">&#91;126&#93;</a></sup> In the 1980s, however, living standards steeply declined again because of <a href="/wiki/Early_1980s_recession" title="Early 1980s recession">a worldwide recession</a> to which communism was unable to respond.<sup id="cite_ref-128" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-128">&#91;127&#93;</a></sup> By the time Kádár died in 1989, the Soviet Union was in steep decline and a younger generation of reformists saw liberalisation as the solution to economic and social issues.
</p>
<h3><span id="Third_Republic_.281989.E2.80.93present.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Third_Republic_(1989–present)">Third Republic (1989–present)</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Revolutions_of_1989" title="Revolutions of 1989">Revolutions of 1989</a>, <a href="/wiki/2006_protests_in_Hungary" title="2006 protests in Hungary">2006 protests in Hungary</a>, <a href="/wiki/2015_European_migrant_crisis" title="2015 European migrant crisis">2015 European migrant crisis</a>, and <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic_in_Hungary" title="COVID-19 pandemic in Hungary">COVID-19 pandemic in Hungary</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Foundation_of_the_Visegr%C3%A1d_Group.tiff" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Foundation_of_the_Visegr%C3%A1d_Group.tiff/lossy-page1-220px-Foundation_of_the_Visegr%C3%A1d_Group.tiff.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="116" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Foundation_of_the_Visegr%C3%A1d_Group.tiff/lossy-page1-330px-Foundation_of_the_Visegr%C3%A1d_Group.tiff.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/36/Foundation_of_the_Visegr%C3%A1d_Group.tiff/lossy-page1-440px-Foundation_of_the_Visegr%C3%A1d_Group.tiff.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1020" data-file-height="540" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Visegr%C3%A1d_Group" title="Visegrád Group">Visegrád Group</a> signing ceremony in February 1991</figcaption></figure>
<p>Hungary's transition from communism to capitalism (<i>rendszerváltás</i>, "regime change") was peaceful and prompted by economic stagnation, domestic political pressure, and changing relations with other Warsaw Pact countries. Although the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Socialist_Workers%27_Party" title="Hungarian Socialist Workers&#39; Party">Hungarian Socialist Workers' Party</a> began <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Round_Table_Talks" title="Hungarian Round Table Talks">Round Table Talks</a> with various opposition groups in March 1989, the reburial of Imre Nagy as a revolutionary martyr that June is widely considered the symbolic end of communism in Hungary. Over 100,000 people attended the Budapest ceremony without any significant government interference, and many speakers openly called for Soviet troops to leave the country. Free elections were held <a href="/wiki/1990_Hungarian_parliamentary_election" title="1990 Hungarian parliamentary election">in May 1990</a>, and the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Democratic_Forum" title="Hungarian Democratic Forum">Hungarian Democratic Forum</a>, a major conservative opposition group, was elected to the head of a coalition government. <a href="/wiki/J%C3%B3zsef_Antall" title="József Antall">József Antall</a> became the first democratically elected prime minister since World War II.
</p><p>With the removal of state subsidies and rapid privatisation in 1991, Hungary was affected by a severe economic recession. The Antall government's austerity measures proved unpopular, and the Communist Party's legal and political heir, the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Socialist_Party" title="Hungarian Socialist Party">Socialist Party</a>, won the subsequent <a href="/wiki/1994_Hungarian_parliamentary_election" title="1994 Hungarian parliamentary election">1994 elections</a>. This abrupt shift in the political landscape was repeated in <a href="/wiki/1998_Hungarian_parliamentary_election" title="1998 Hungarian parliamentary election">1998</a> and <a href="/wiki/2002_Hungarian_parliamentary_election" title="2002 Hungarian parliamentary election">2002</a>; in each electoral cycle, the governing party was ousted and the erstwhile opposition elected. Like most other post-communist European states, however, Hungary broadly pursued an <a href="/wiki/European_integration" title="European integration">integrationist</a> agenda, <a href="/wiki/Enlargement_of_NATO" title="Enlargement of NATO">joining</a> <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a> in 1999 and the European Union <a href="/wiki/2003_Hungarian_European_Union_membership_referendum" title="2003 Hungarian European Union membership referendum">in 2004</a>. As a NATO member, Hungary was involved in the <a href="/wiki/Yugoslav_Wars" title="Yugoslav Wars">Yugoslav Wars</a>.
</p>
<h4><span id="Under_Viktor_Orban_.282010.E2.80.93present.29"></span><span class="mw-headline" id="Under_Viktor_Orban_(2010–present)">Under Viktor Orban (2010–present)</span></h4>
<p>In 2006, <a href="/wiki/2006_protests_in_Hungary" title="2006 protests in Hungary">major nationwide protests</a> erupted after it was revealed that Prime Minister <a href="/wiki/Ferenc_Gyurcs%C3%A1ny" title="Ferenc Gyurcsány">Ferenc Gyurcsány</a> had claimed in <a href="/wiki/%C5%90sz%C3%B6d_speech" title="Őszöd speech">a closed-door speech</a> that his party "lied" to win the <a href="/wiki/2006_Hungarian_parliamentary_election" title="2006 Hungarian parliamentary election">recent elections</a>. The popularity of left-wing parties plummeted in the ensuing political upheaval, and in 2010, <a href="/wiki/Viktor_Orb%C3%A1n" title="Viktor Orbán">Viktor Orbán</a>'s <a href="/wiki/National_conservatism" title="National conservatism">national-conservative</a> <a href="/wiki/Fidesz" title="Fidesz">Fidesz</a> party was <a href="/wiki/2010_Hungarian_parliamentary_election" title="2010 Hungarian parliamentary election">elected</a> to a parliamentary <a href="/wiki/Supermajority" title="Supermajority">supermajority</a>. The legislature consequently approved a <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Hungary" title="Constitution of Hungary">new constitution</a>, among other sweeping governmental and legal changes including the establishment of new parliamentary constituencies, decreasing the number of parliamentarians, and shifting to single-round parliamentary elections. 
</p>
<figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Police_car_at_Hungary-Serbia_border_barrier.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/Police_car_at_Hungary-Serbia_border_barrier.jpg/200px-Police_car_at_Hungary-Serbia_border_barrier.jpg" decoding="async" width="200" height="129" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/Police_car_at_Hungary-Serbia_border_barrier.jpg/300px-Police_car_at_Hungary-Serbia_border_barrier.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/61/Police_car_at_Hungary-Serbia_border_barrier.jpg/400px-Police_car_at_Hungary-Serbia_border_barrier.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="660" /></a><figcaption>Police car at Hungary-Serbia border barrier</figcaption></figure>
<p>During the <a href="/wiki/2015_European_migrant_crisis" title="2015 European migrant crisis">2015 migrant crisis</a>, the government built a <a href="/wiki/Border_barrier" title="Border barrier">border barrier</a> on the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_border_barrier" title="Hungarian border barrier">Hungarian-Croatian</a> and <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_border_barrier" title="Hungarian border barrier">Hungarian-Serbian borders</a> to prevent illegal migration.<sup id="cite_ref-129" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-129">&#91;128&#93;</a></sup> The Hungarian government also criticised the official <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a> policy for not dissuading migrants from entering Europe.<sup id="cite_ref-130" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-130">&#91;129&#93;</a></sup> The barrier became successful, as from 17 October 2015 onward, thousands of migrants were diverted daily to Slovenia instead.<sup id="cite_ref-SloveniaCroatia2_131-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-SloveniaCroatia2-131">&#91;130&#93;</a></sup> Migration became a key issue in the <a href="/wiki/2018_Hungarian_parliamentary_election" title="2018 Hungarian parliamentary election">2018 parliamentary elections</a>. Fidesz won the election again with a supermajority.<sup id="cite_ref-132" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-132">&#91;131&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In the late 2010s, Orbán's government came under increased international scrutiny over alleged rule-of-law violations. In 2018, the <a href="/wiki/European_Parliament" title="European Parliament">European Parliament</a> voted to act against Hungary under the terms of <a href="/wiki/Article_7_of_the_Treaty_on_European_Union" title="Article 7 of the Treaty on European Union">Article 7 of the Treaty on European Union</a>. Hungary has and continues to dispute these allegations.<sup id="cite_ref-133" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-133">&#91;132&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/COVID-19_pandemic" title="COVID-19 pandemic">coronavirus pandemic</a> significantly impacted Hungary. The first cases were announced in Hungary 4 March 2020,<sup id="cite_ref-134" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-134">&#91;133&#93;</a></sup> the first death eleven days later.<sup id="cite_ref-elsőhalott_135-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-elsőhalott-135">&#91;134&#93;</a></sup> On March 18, 2020, <a href="/wiki/Surgeon_general" title="Surgeon general">surgeon general</a> <a href="/wiki/Cec%C3%ADlia_M%C3%BCller" title="Cecília Müller">Cecília Müller</a> announced that the virus had spread to every part of the country.<sup id="cite_ref-136" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-136">&#91;135&#93;</a></sup> Following the approval of the first COVID-19 vaccines in the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>, free and voluntary vaccination against the disease began in Hungary on 26 December 2020. In February 2021, after Hungary became the first EU country and one of the first in the former <a href="/wiki/Warsaw_Pact" title="Warsaw Pact">Warsaw Pact</a> to authorize <a href="/wiki/Sputnik_vaccine" class="mw-redirect" title="Sputnik vaccine">Russian</a> and <a href="/wiki/CoronaVac" title="CoronaVac">Chinese vaccines</a>, it briefly enjoyed one of the highest vaccination rates in Europe.
</p><p>Hungary received criticism and accusation from several organizations to became a hybrid regime,<sup id="cite_ref-VDem2020_137-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-VDem2020-137">&#91;136&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-KrekóEnyedi_138-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KrekóEnyedi-138">&#91;137&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-139" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-139">&#91;138&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-140" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-140">&#91;139&#93;</a></sup> as well were criticized regarding her view regarding <a href="/wiki/LGBT_rights_in_Hungary" title="LGBT rights in Hungary">LGBT rights in Hungary</a>, creating a bill similar to the <a href="/wiki/Section_28" title="Section 28">Section 28</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-141" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-141">&#91;140&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Geography">Geography</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Geography_of_Hungary" title="Geography of Hungary">Geography of Hungary</a> and <a href="/wiki/Climate_of_Hungary" title="Climate of Hungary">Climate of Hungary</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/List_of_national_parks_of_Hungary" title="List of national parks of Hungary">List of national parks of Hungary</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Hungary-geographic_map-en.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/Hungary-geographic_map-en.svg/290px-Hungary-geographic_map-en.svg.png" decoding="async" width="290" height="188" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/Hungary-geographic_map-en.svg/435px-Hungary-geographic_map-en.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/1e/Hungary-geographic_map-en.svg/580px-Hungary-geographic_map-en.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1855" data-file-height="1200" /></a><figcaption>Geographic map of Hungary</figcaption></figure>
<p>Hungary is a <a href="/wiki/Landlocked_country" title="Landlocked country">landlocked country</a>. Its geography has traditionally been defined by its two main waterways, the <a href="/wiki/Danube" title="Danube">Danube</a> and <a href="/wiki/Tisza" title="Tisza">Tisza</a> rivers. The common tripartite division—<i>Dunántúl</i> ("beyond the Danube", <a href="/wiki/Transdanubia" title="Transdanubia">Transdanubia</a>), <i><a href="/wiki/Tisz%C3%A1nt%C3%BAl" title="Tiszántúl">Tiszántúl</a></i> ("beyond the Tisza"), and <i>Duna-Tisza kőze</i> ("between the Danube and Tisza")—is a reflection of this. The Danube flows north–south through the centre of contemporary Hungary, and the entire country lies within its <a href="/wiki/Drainage_basin" title="Drainage basin">drainage basin</a>.
</p><p>Transdanubia, which stretches westward from the centre of the country towards Austria, is a primarily hilly region with a terrain varied by low mountains. These include the very eastern stretch of the <a href="/wiki/Alps" title="Alps">Alps</a>, <i><a href="/wiki/Alpokalja" title="Alpokalja">Alpokalja</a></i>, in the west of the country, the <a href="/wiki/Transdanubian_Mountains" title="Transdanubian Mountains">Transdanubian Mountains</a> in the central region of Transdanubia, and the <a href="/wiki/Mecsek" title="Mecsek">Mecsek</a> Mountains and <a href="/wiki/Vill%C3%A1ny_Mountains" title="Villány Mountains">Villány Mountains</a> in the south. The highest point of the area is the Írott-kő in the Alps, at 882 metres (2,894&#160;ft). The <a href="/wiki/Little_Hungarian_Plain" title="Little Hungarian Plain">Little Hungarian Plain</a> (<i>Kisalföld</i>) is found in northern Transdanubia. <a href="/wiki/Lake_Balaton" title="Lake Balaton">Lake Balaton</a> and <a href="/wiki/Lake_H%C3%A9v%C3%ADz" title="Lake Hévíz">Lake Hévíz</a>, the largest lake in Central Europe and the largest thermal lake in the world, respectively, are in Transdanubia as well.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Balaton_Hungary_Landscape.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/Balaton_Hungary_Landscape.jpg/220px-Balaton_Hungary_Landscape.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/Balaton_Hungary_Landscape.jpg/330px-Balaton_Hungary_Landscape.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/Balaton_Hungary_Landscape.jpg/440px-Balaton_Hungary_Landscape.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3036" data-file-height="2010" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Lake_Balaton" title="Lake Balaton">Lake Balaton</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>The <i>Duna-Tisza kőze</i> and <i>Tiszántúl</i> are characterised mainly by the <a href="/wiki/Great_Hungarian_Plain" title="Great Hungarian Plain">Great Hungarian Plain</a> (<i>Alföld</i>), which stretches across most of the eastern and southeastern areas of the country. To the north of the plain are the foothills of the <a href="/wiki/Carpathian_Mountains" title="Carpathian Mountains">Carpathians</a> in a wide band near the <a href="/wiki/Slovakia" title="Slovakia">Slovakian</a> border. The <a href="/wiki/K%C3%A9kes" title="Kékes">Kékes</a> at 1,014&#160;m (3,327&#160;ft) is the tallest mountain in Hungary and is found there.
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Phytogeography" title="Phytogeography">Phytogeographically</a>, Hungary belongs to the Central European province of the <a href="/wiki/Circumboreal_Region" title="Circumboreal Region">Circumboreal Region</a> within the <a href="/wiki/Boreal_Kingdom" title="Boreal Kingdom">Boreal Kingdom</a>. According to the <a href="/wiki/World_Wide_Fund_for_Nature" title="World Wide Fund for Nature">WWF</a>, the territory of Hungary belongs to the terrestrial ecoregion of <a href="/wiki/Pannonian_mixed_forests" title="Pannonian mixed forests">Pannonian mixed forests</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-DinersteinOlson2017_142-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-DinersteinOlson2017-142">&#91;141&#93;</a></sup> It had a 2019 <a href="/wiki/Forest_Landscape_Integrity_Index" title="Forest Landscape Integrity Index">Forest Landscape Integrity Index</a> mean score of 2.25/10, ranking it 156th globally out of 172 countries.<sup id="cite_ref-FLII-Supplementary_143-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FLII-Supplementary-143">&#91;142&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Hungary has 10 national parks, 145 minor nature reserves, and 35 landscape protection areas.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Climate">Climate</span></h3>
<p>Hungary has a <a href="/wiki/Temperate_climate" title="Temperate climate">temperate seasonal climate</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-144" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-144">&#91;143&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-145" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-145">&#91;144&#93;</a></sup> with generally warm summers with low overall humidity levels but frequent rain showers and cold snowy winters. Average annual temperature is 9.7&#160;°C (49.5&#160;°F). Temperature extremes are 41.9&#160;°C (107.4&#160;°F) on 20 July 2007 at <a href="/wiki/Kiskunhalas" title="Kiskunhalas">Kiskunhalas</a> in the summer and −35&#160;°C (−31.0&#160;°F) on 16 February 1940 at <a href="/wiki/Miskolc" title="Miskolc">Miskolc</a> in the winter. Average high temperature in the summer is 23 to 28&#160;°C (73 to 82&#160;°F) and average low temperature in the winter is −3 to −7&#160;°C (27 to 19&#160;°F). The average yearly rainfall is approximately 600&#160;mm (23.6&#160;in). 
</p><p>Hungary is ranked sixth in an environmental protection index by <i>GW</i>/<i><a href="/wiki/Climate_Action_Network" title="Climate Action Network">CAN</a></i>.<sup id="cite_ref-146" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-146">&#91;145&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Government_and_politics">Government and politics</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Politics_of_Hungary" title="Politics of Hungary">Politics of Hungary</a>, <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Hungary" title="Government of Hungary">Government of Hungary</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Taxation_in_Hungary" title="Taxation in Hungary">Taxation in Hungary</a></div>
<style data-mw-deduplicate="TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti">.mw-parser-output .tmulti .multiimageinner{display:flex;flex-direction:column}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .theader{clear:both;font-weight:bold;text-align:center;align-self:center;background-color:transparent;width:100%}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .thumbcaption{background-color:transparent}.mw-parser-output .thumbcaption{text-align:left}.mw-parser-output .tmulti .trow>.thumbcaption{text-align:center}}</style><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" title="List of heads of state of Hungary">President</a> since 2022</small></div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:147px;max-width:147px"><div class="thumbimage"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Viktor_Orban_2021_crop.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0d/Viktor_Orban_2021_crop.jpg/145px-Viktor_Orban_2021_crop.jpg" decoding="async" width="145" height="194" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/0d/Viktor_Orban_2021_crop.jpg/218px-Viktor_Orban_2021_crop.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/0/0d/Viktor_Orban_2021_crop.jpg 2x" data-file-width="238" data-file-height="318" /></a></span></div><div class="thumbcaption text-align-center"><a href="/wiki/Viktor_Orb%C3%A1n" title="Viktor Orbán">Viktor Orbán</a>,<br /><small><a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Hungary" title="Prime Minister of Hungary">Prime Minister</a> since 2010</small></div></div></div></div></div>
<p>Hungary is a <a href="/wiki/Unitary_state" title="Unitary state">unitary</a>, <a href="/wiki/Parliamentary_republic" title="Parliamentary republic">parliamentary republic</a>. The Hungarian political system operates under a framework reformed in 2012; this constitutional document is the <a href="/wiki/Constitution_of_Hungary" title="Constitution of Hungary">Fundamental Law of Hungary</a>. Amendments generally require a two-thirds majority of parliament; the fundamental principles of the constitution (as expressed in the articles guaranteeing human dignity, the separation of powers, the state structure, and the <a href="/wiki/Rule_of_law" title="Rule of law">rule of law</a>) are valid in perpetuity. 199 Members of Parliament (<i>országgyűlési képviselő</i>) are elected to the highest organ of state authority, the <a href="/wiki/Unicameralism" title="Unicameralism">unicameral</a> <i>Országgyűlés</i> (<a href="/wiki/National_Assembly_(Hungary)" title="National Assembly (Hungary)">National Assembly</a>), every four years in a single-round <a href="/wiki/First-past-the-post_voting" title="First-past-the-post voting">first-past-the-post</a> election with an <a href="/wiki/Electoral_threshold" title="Electoral threshold">election threshold</a> of 5%.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:S%C3%A1ndor-palota_naps%C3%BCt%C3%A9sben.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/S%C3%A1ndor-palota_naps%C3%BCt%C3%A9sben.jpg/220px-S%C3%A1ndor-palota_naps%C3%BCt%C3%A9sben.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/S%C3%A1ndor-palota_naps%C3%BCt%C3%A9sben.jpg/330px-S%C3%A1ndor-palota_naps%C3%BCt%C3%A9sben.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9e/S%C3%A1ndor-palota_naps%C3%BCt%C3%A9sben.jpg/440px-S%C3%A1ndor-palota_naps%C3%BCt%C3%A9sben.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4448" data-file-height="2945" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/S%C3%A1ndor_Palace,_Budapest" title="Sándor Palace, Budapest">Sándor Palace</a> is the <a href="/wiki/Official_residence" title="Official residence">official residence</a> of the <a href="/wiki/President_of_Hungary" title="President of Hungary">President of Hungary</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Budapest_Hungarian_Parliament_(31363963556).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Budapest_Hungarian_Parliament_%2831363963556%29.jpg/220px-Budapest_Hungarian_Parliament_%2831363963556%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="132" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Budapest_Hungarian_Parliament_%2831363963556%29.jpg/330px-Budapest_Hungarian_Parliament_%2831363963556%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/60/Budapest_Hungarian_Parliament_%2831363963556%29.jpg/440px-Budapest_Hungarian_Parliament_%2831363963556%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3825" data-file-height="2295" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Parliament_Building" title="Hungarian Parliament Building">Hungarian Parliament Building</a> on the banks of the Danube in <a href="/wiki/Budapest" title="Budapest">Budapest</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/President_of_Hungary" title="President of Hungary">President of the Republic</a> (<i>köztársasági elnök</i>) serves as the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_state" title="Head of state">head of state</a> and is elected by the National Assembly every five years. The president is invested primarily with representative responsibilities and powers: receiving foreign heads of state, formally nominating the prime minister at the recommendation of the National Assembly, and serving as <a href="/wiki/Commander-in-chief" title="Commander-in-chief">commander-in-chief</a> of the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Ground_Forces" title="Hungarian Ground Forces">armed forces</a>. Importantly, the president is also invested with <a href="/wiki/Veto" title="Veto">veto</a> power and may send legislation to the 15-member <a href="/wiki/Constitutional_Court_of_Hungary" title="Constitutional Court of Hungary">Constitutional Court</a> for review. The third most significant governmental position in Hungary is the <a href="/wiki/List_of_speakers_of_the_National_Assembly_(Hungary)" title="List of speakers of the National Assembly (Hungary)">Speaker of the National Assembly</a>, who is elected by the National Assembly and responsible for overseeing the daily sessions of the body.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p><p>The <a href="/wiki/Prime_Minister_of_Hungary" title="Prime Minister of Hungary">prime minister</a> (<i>miniszterelnök</i>) is elected by the National Assembly, serving as the <a href="/wiki/Head_of_government" title="Head of government">head of government</a> and exercising <a href="/wiki/Executive_(government)" title="Executive (government)">executive power</a>. Traditionally, the prime minister is the leader of the largest party in parliament. The prime minister selects <a href="/wiki/Government_of_Hungary" title="Government of Hungary">Cabinet</a> ministers and has the exclusive right to dismiss them, although cabinet nominees must appear before consultative open hearings before one or more parliamentary committees, survive a vote in the National Assembly, and be formally approved by the president. The Cabinet reports to Parliament.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (February 2022)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Political_parties">Political parties</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Political_parties_in_Hungary" class="mw-redirect" title="Political parties in Hungary">Political parties in Hungary</a>, <a href="/wiki/Elections_in_Hungary" title="Elections in Hungary">Elections in Hungary</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Electoral_system_of_Hungary" title="Electoral system of Hungary">Electoral system of Hungary</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1066479718"><table class="infobox vcard"><tbody><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-above" style="font-size: 125%; background-color: #ededed"><div style="display:inline" class="fn org">Current Structure of the National Assembly of Hungary</div></th></tr><tr><th colspan="2" class="infobox-header" style="background-color: #ededed">Structure</th></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label">Seats</th><td class="infobox-data">199</td></tr><tr><td colspan="2" class="infobox-full-data"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:National_Assembly_of_Hungary_(as_of_18.03.2022).svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="Current Structure of the National Assembly of Hungary" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/National_Assembly_of_Hungary_%28as_of_18.03.2022%29.svg/250px-National_Assembly_of_Hungary_%28as_of_18.03.2022%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="250" height="128" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/National_Assembly_of_Hungary_%28as_of_18.03.2022%29.svg/375px-National_Assembly_of_Hungary_%28as_of_18.03.2022%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/National_Assembly_of_Hungary_%28as_of_18.03.2022%29.svg/500px-National_Assembly_of_Hungary_%28as_of_18.03.2022%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="360" data-file-height="185" /></a></span></td></tr><tr><th scope="row" class="infobox-label"><div style="display: inline-block; line-height: 1.2em; padding: .1em 0;">Political groups</div></th><td class="infobox-data"><b><a href="/wiki/Fourth_Orb%C3%A1n_Government" title="Fourth Orbán Government">Government</a> (135)</b>

<li><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><span class="legend-color" style="background-color:#3CB34D; color:black;border:1px solid darkgray;">&#160;</span> <a href="/wiki/Dialogue_for_Hungary" class="mw-redirect" title="Dialogue for Hungary">Dialogue</a> (6)</li>

<li><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><span class="legend-color" style="background-color:#DCDCDC; color:black;border:1px solid darkgray;">&#160;</span> <a href="/wiki/Independent_politician" title="Independent politician">Ind.</a> (2)</li></ul></li>
<li><link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r981673959"><span class="legend-color" style="background-color:#688D1B; color:black;border:1px solid darkgray;">&#160;</span> <a href="/wiki/Our_Homeland_Movement" title="Our Homeland Movement">MHM</a> (6)</li></ul>
</td></tr></tbody></table>
<p>Since the fall of communism, Hungary has a <a href="/wiki/Multi-party_system" title="Multi-party system">multi-party system</a>. The <a href="/wiki/2022_Hungarian_parliamentary_election" title="2022 Hungarian parliamentary election">last Hungarian parliamentary election</a> took place on 3 April 2022.<sup id="cite_ref-147" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-147">&#91;146&#93;</a></sup> This parliamentary election was the 8th since the <a href="/wiki/1990_Hungarian_parliamentary_election" title="1990 Hungarian parliamentary election">1990 first multi-party election</a>. The result was a victory for Fidesz–KDNP alliance, preserving its two-thirds majority with Orbán remaining prime minister.<sup id="cite_ref-148" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-148">&#91;147&#93;</a></sup> It was the third election according to the new Constitution of Hungary which went into force on 1 January 2012. The <a href="/wiki/Electoral_system_of_Hungary" title="Electoral system of Hungary">new electoral law</a> also entered into force that day. The voters elected 199 MPs instead of previous 386 lawmakers.<sup id="cite_ref-149" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-149">&#91;148&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-150" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-150">&#91;149&#93;</a></sup> Since 2014, voters of <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Hungary" title="Demographics of Hungary">ethnic minorities in Hungary</a> are able to vote on nationality lists. The minorities can obtain a preferential mandate if they reach the quarter of the ninety-third part of the list votes.<sup id="cite_ref-:1_151-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:1-151">&#91;150&#93;</a></sup> Nationalities who did not get a mandate could send a nationality spokesman to the <a href="/wiki/National_Assembly_(Hungary)" title="National Assembly (Hungary)">National Assembly</a>. The current political landscape in Hungary is dominated by the conservative Fidesz, who have a near supermajority, and three medium-sized parties, the left-wing <a href="/wiki/Democratic_Coalition_(Hungary)" title="Democratic Coalition (Hungary)">Democratic Coalition</a> (DK), the far-right <a href="/wiki/Our_Homeland_Movement" title="Our Homeland Movement">Our Homeland Movement</a> and liberal <a href="/wiki/Momentum_Movement" title="Momentum Movement">Momentum</a>.
</p><p>After the fall of the Iron Curtain and the end of communist dictatorship in 1989, a democratic form of government was established. Today's parliament is still called <i>Országgyűlés</i> just like in royal times, but in order to differentiate between the historical royal diet is referred to as the "National Assembly" now. The <a href="/wiki/Diet_of_Hungary" title="Diet of Hungary">Diet of Hungary</a> was a legislative institution in the medieval Kingdom of Hungary from the 1290s,<sup id="cite_ref-152" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-152">&#91;151&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-153" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-153">&#91;152&#93;</a></sup> and in its successor states, Royal Hungary and the Habsburg kingdom of Hungary throughout the <a href="/wiki/Early_modern_period" title="Early modern period">early modern period</a>. The articles of the 1790 diet set out that the diet should meet at least once every 3 years, but since the diet was called by the <a href="/wiki/Habsburg_monarchy" title="Habsburg monarchy">Habsburg monarchy</a>, this promise was not kept on several occasions thereafter. As a result of the Austro-Hungarian Compromise, it was reconstituted in 1867. The Latin term <i>Natio Hungarica</i> ("Hungarian nation") was used to designate the political elite which had participation in the diet, consisting of the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_nobility" title="Hungarian nobility">nobility</a>, the Catholic clergy, and a few enfranchised burghers,<sup id="cite_ref-154" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-154">&#91;153&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-155" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-155">&#91;154&#93;</a></sup> regardless of language or ethnicity.<sup id="cite_ref-156" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-156">&#91;155&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Law_and_judicial_system">Law and judicial system</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Law_of_Hungary" title="Law of Hungary">Law of Hungary</a> and <a href="/wiki/Law_enforcement_in_Hungary" title="Law enforcement in Hungary">Law enforcement in Hungary</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Kossuth_Square_12,_facade,_2015-05-12_Budapest_-_panoramio_(45).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Kossuth_Square_12%2C_facade%2C_2015-05-12_Budapest_-_panoramio_%2845%29.jpg/220px-Kossuth_Square_12%2C_facade%2C_2015-05-12_Budapest_-_panoramio_%2845%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Kossuth_Square_12%2C_facade%2C_2015-05-12_Budapest_-_panoramio_%2845%29.jpg/330px-Kossuth_Square_12%2C_facade%2C_2015-05-12_Budapest_-_panoramio_%2845%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/c9/Kossuth_Square_12%2C_facade%2C_2015-05-12_Budapest_-_panoramio_%2845%29.jpg/440px-Kossuth_Square_12%2C_facade%2C_2015-05-12_Budapest_-_panoramio_%2845%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="5733" data-file-height="3822" /></a><figcaption>The original and future seat of the <a href="/wiki/Curia_of_Hungary" title="Curia of Hungary">Curia</a>, Hungary's <a href="/wiki/Supreme_court" title="Supreme court">highest court</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>The judicial system of Hungary is a <a href="/wiki/Civil_law_(legal_system)" title="Civil law (legal system)">civil law</a> system divided between courts with regular civil and criminal jurisdiction and administrative courts with jurisdiction over litigation between individuals and the public administration. Hungarian law is codified and based on <a href="/wiki/Law_of_Germany" title="Law of Germany">German law</a> and, in a wider sense, civil law or <a href="/wiki/Roman_law" title="Roman law">Roman law</a>. The court system for civil and criminal jurisdiction consists of local courts (<i>járásbíróság</i>), regional appellate courts (<i>ítélőtábla</i>), and the supreme court (<i><a href="/wiki/Curia_of_Hungary" title="Curia of Hungary">Kúria</a></i>). Hungary's highest courts are located in Budapest.<sup id="cite_ref-157" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-157">&#91;156&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Law enforcement in Hungary is split among the police and the National Tax and Customs Administration. The <a href="/wiki/Rend%C5%91rs%C3%A9g" title="Rendőrség">Hungarian Police</a> is the main and largest state law enforcement agency in Hungary. It carries nearly all general police duties such as criminal investigation, patrol activity, traffic policing, border control. It is led by the national police commissioner under the control of the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Interior_(Hungary)" title="Ministry of Interior (Hungary)">Minister of the Interior</a>. The body is divided into county police departments which are also divided into regional and town police departments. The National Police has subordinate agencies with nationwide jurisdiction, such as the "Nemzeti Nyomozó Iroda" (National Bureau of Investigation), a civilian police force specialised in investigating serious crimes, and the <a href="/wiki/Gendarmerie" title="Gendarmerie">gendarmerie</a>-like, militarised "Készenléti rendőrség" (Stand-by Police) mainly dealing with riots and often reinforcing local police forces. Because of Hungary's accession to the <a href="/wiki/Schengen_Agreement" title="Schengen Agreement">Schengen Treaty</a>, the police and border guards were merged into a single national corps, with the border guards (<i>Határőrség Magyarországon</i>) becoming police officers. This merger took place in January 2008. The Customs and Excise Authority remained subject to the <a href="/wiki/Ministry_of_Finance_(Hungary)" title="Ministry of Finance (Hungary)">Ministry of Finance</a> under the National Tax and Customs Administration.<sup id="cite_ref-158" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-158">&#91;157&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Foreign_relations">Foreign relations</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Foreign_relations_of_Hungary" title="Foreign relations of Hungary">Foreign relations of Hungary</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Necasmeeting.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/47/Necasmeeting.jpg/220px-Necasmeeting.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/47/Necasmeeting.jpg/330px-Necasmeeting.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/47/Necasmeeting.jpg/440px-Necasmeeting.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1417" data-file-height="945" /></a><figcaption>Meeting of the leaders of the <a href="/wiki/Visegr%C3%A1d_Group" title="Visegrád Group">Visegrád Group</a>, Germany and France in 2013</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:BIMUN_2012_opening_1.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/BIMUN_2012_opening_1.jpg/220px-BIMUN_2012_opening_1.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/BIMUN_2012_opening_1.jpg/330px-BIMUN_2012_opening_1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f7/BIMUN_2012_opening_1.jpg/440px-BIMUN_2012_opening_1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1024" data-file-height="682" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> conference in the assembly hall of the <a href="/wiki/House_of_Magnates" title="House of Magnates">House of Magnates</a> in the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Parliament_Building" title="Hungarian Parliament Building">Hungarian Parliament Building</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>The <a href="/wiki/Foreign_policy" title="Foreign policy">foreign policy</a> is based on four basic commitments: to <a href="/wiki/Atlanticism" title="Atlanticism">Atlantic co-operation</a>, to <a href="/wiki/European_integration" title="European integration">European integration</a>, to <a href="/wiki/International_development" title="International development">international development</a> and to <a href="/wiki/International_law" title="International law">international law</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-159" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-159">&#91;158&#93;</a></sup> Hungary has been a member of the <a href="/wiki/United_Nations" title="United Nations">United Nations</a> since December 1955 and a member of the <a href="/wiki/European_Union" title="European Union">European Union</a>, <a href="/wiki/NATO" title="NATO">NATO</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Organisation_for_Economic_Co-operation_and_Development" class="mw-redirect" title="Organisation for Economic Co-operation and Development">OECD</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Visegr%C3%A1d_Group" title="Visegrád Group">Visegrád Group</a>, the <a href="/wiki/WTO" class="mw-redirect" title="WTO">WTO</a>, the <a href="/wiki/World_Bank" title="World Bank">World Bank</a>, the <a href="/wiki/Asian_Infrastructure_Investment_Bank" title="Asian Infrastructure Investment Bank">AIIB</a> and the <a href="/wiki/International_Monetary_Fund" title="International Monetary Fund">IMF</a>. Hungary took on the presidency of the <a href="/wiki/Council_of_the_European_Union" title="Council of the European Union">Council of the European Union</a> for half a year in 2011 and the next will be in 2024. In 2015, Hungary was the fifth largest OECD non-DAC donor of <a href="/wiki/Development_aid" title="Development aid">development aid</a> in the world, which represents <a href="/wiki/List_of_development_aid_country_donors" class="mw-redirect" title="List of development aid country donors">0.13% of its Gross National Income</a>.
</p><p>Budapest is home to more than 100 embassies and representative bodies as an international political actor.<sup id="cite_ref-160" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-160">&#91;159&#93;</a></sup> Hungary hosts the main and regional headquarters of many international organisations as well, including <a href="/wiki/European_Institute_of_Innovation_and_Technology" title="European Institute of Innovation and Technology">European Institute of Innovation and Technology</a>, <a href="/wiki/European_Police_College" class="mw-redirect" title="European Police College">European Police College</a>, <a href="/wiki/United_Nations_High_Commissioner_for_Refugees" title="United Nations High Commissioner for Refugees">United Nations High Commissioner for Refugees</a>, <a href="/wiki/Food_and_Agriculture_Organization_of_the_United_Nations" class="mw-redirect" title="Food and Agriculture Organization of the United Nations">Food and Agriculture Organization of the United Nations</a>, <a href="/wiki/International_Centre_for_Democratic_Transition" title="International Centre for Democratic Transition">International Centre for Democratic Transition</a>, <a href="/wiki/Institute_of_International_Education" title="Institute of International Education">Institute of International Education</a>, <a href="/wiki/International_Labour_Organization" title="International Labour Organization">International Labour Organization</a>, <a href="/wiki/International_Organization_for_Migration" title="International Organization for Migration">International Organization for Migration</a>, <a href="/wiki/International_Federation_of_Red_Cross_and_Red_Crescent_Societies" title="International Federation of Red Cross and Red Crescent Societies">International Red Cross</a>, Regional Environmental Center for Central and Eastern Europe, <a href="/wiki/Danube_Commission_(1948)" title="Danube Commission (1948)">Danube Commission</a> and others.<sup id="cite_ref-161" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-161">&#91;160&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Since 1989, the top foreign policy goal has been achieving integration into Western economic and security organisations. Hungary joined the <a href="/wiki/Partnership_for_Peace" title="Partnership for Peace">Partnership for Peace</a> programme in 1994 and has actively supported the <a href="/wiki/Implementation_Force" title="Implementation Force">IFOR</a> and <a href="/wiki/Stabilisation_Force_in_Bosnia_and_Herzegovina" title="Stabilisation Force in Bosnia and Herzegovina">SFOR</a> missions in Bosnia. Since 1989 Hungary has improved its often frosty neighbour relations by signing basic treaties with Romania, Slovakia, and Ukraine. These renounce all outstanding territorial claims and lay the foundation for constructive relations. However, the issue of <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_diaspora" title="Hungarian diaspora">ethnic Hungarian minority</a> rights in Romania, Slovakia, and Serbia periodically cause bilateral tensions to flare up, although relations with Serbia have more recently become extremely close due to strong Hungarian advocacy for Serbian EU membership.<sup id="cite_ref-162" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-162">&#91;161&#93;</a></sup> Since 2017, the <a href="/wiki/Hungary%E2%80%93Ukraine_relations" title="Hungary–Ukraine relations">relations with Ukraine</a> rapidly deteriorated over the issue of the <a href="/wiki/Hungarians_in_Ukraine" title="Hungarians in Ukraine">Hungarian minority in Ukraine</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-163" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-163">&#91;162&#93;</a></sup> Since 1989, Hungary has signed all of the <a href="/wiki/Organization_for_Security_and_Co-operation_in_Europe" title="Organization for Security and Co-operation in Europe">OSCE</a> documents, and served as the OSCE's Chairman-in-Office in 1997. Historically, Hungary has had particularly friendly <a href="/wiki/Hungary%E2%80%93Poland_relations" title="Hungary–Poland relations">relations with Poland</a>; this special relationship was recognised by the parliaments of both countries in 2007 with the joint declaration of 23 March as "The Day of Polish-Hungarian Friendship".<sup id="cite_ref-164" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-164">&#91;163&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Military">Military</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Defence_Forces" title="Hungarian Defence Forces">Hungarian Defence Forces</a> and <a href="/wiki/Military_history_of_Hungary" title="Military history of Hungary">Military history of Hungary</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/List_of_wars_involving_Hungary" title="List of wars involving Hungary">List of wars involving Hungary</a> and <a href="/wiki/List_of_equipment_of_the_Hungarian_Ground_Forces" title="List of equipment of the Hungarian Ground Forces">List of equipment of the Hungarian Ground Forces</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Hunspec.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/Hunspec.jpg/220px-Hunspec.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="141" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/Hunspec.jpg/330px-Hunspec.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4f/Hunspec.jpg/440px-Hunspec.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1500" data-file-height="961" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/HDF_34th_Bercs%C3%A9nyi_L%C3%A1szl%C3%B3_Special_Forces_Battalion" title="HDF 34th Bercsényi László Special Forces Battalion">HDF 34th Special Forces Battalion</a></figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:JAS_39_pair_HuAF_Kecskem%C3%A9t_2007.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/JAS_39_pair_HuAF_Kecskem%C3%A9t_2007.JPG/220px-JAS_39_pair_HuAF_Kecskem%C3%A9t_2007.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/JAS_39_pair_HuAF_Kecskem%C3%A9t_2007.JPG/330px-JAS_39_pair_HuAF_Kecskem%C3%A9t_2007.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/0/08/JAS_39_pair_HuAF_Kecskem%C3%A9t_2007.JPG/440px-JAS_39_pair_HuAF_Kecskem%C3%A9t_2007.JPG 2x" data-file-width="1548" data-file-height="1031" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/JAS_39_Gripen" class="mw-redirect" title="JAS 39 Gripen">JAS 39 Gripen</a> <a href="/wiki/Multirole_combat_aircraft" title="Multirole combat aircraft">multirole combat aircraft</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>The president holds the title of commander-in-chief of the nation's armed forces. The <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Defence_(Hungary)" title="Minister of Defence (Hungary)">Ministry of Defence</a> jointly with <a href="/wiki/Commander_of_the_Defence_Forces_(Hungary)" class="mw-redirect" title="Commander of the Defence Forces (Hungary)">chief of staff</a> administers the armed forces, including the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Ground_Forces" title="Hungarian Ground Forces">Hungarian Ground Force</a> (HDF) and the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Air_Force" title="Hungarian Air Force">Hungarian Air Force</a>. Since 2007, the Hungarian Armed Forces has been under a unified command structure. The Ministry of Defence maintains political and civil control over the army. A subordinate Joint Forces Command coordinates and commands the HDF. In 2016, the armed forces had 31,080 personnel on active duty, the operative reserve brought the total number of troops to fifty thousand. In 2016, it was planned that military spending the following year would be $1.21&#160;billion, about 0.94% of the country's GDP, well below the NATO target of 2%. In 2012, the government adopted a resolution in which it pledged to increase defence spending to 1.4% of GDP by 2022.<sup id="cite_ref-165" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-165">&#91;164&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p><a href="/wiki/Military_service" title="Military service">Military service</a> is voluntary, though <a href="/wiki/Conscription" title="Conscription">conscription</a> may occur in wartime. In a significant move for modernisation, Hungary decided in 2001 to buy 14 <a href="/wiki/Saab_JAS_39_Gripen" title="Saab JAS 39 Gripen">JAS 39 Gripen</a> fighter aircraft for about 800&#160;million EUR. Hungarian National Cyber Security Center was re-organised in 2016 in order to become more efficient through <a href="/wiki/Cyber_security" class="mw-redirect" title="Cyber security">cyber security</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-166" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-166">&#91;165&#93;</a></sup> In 2016, the Hungarian military had about 700 troops stationed in foreign countries as part of international peacekeeping forces, including 100 HDF troops in the NATO-led <a href="/wiki/International_Security_Assistance_Force" title="International Security Assistance Force">ISAF</a> force in Afghanistan, 210 Hungarian soldiers in Kosovo under command of <a href="/wiki/Kosovo_Force" title="Kosovo Force">KFOR</a>, and 160 troops in Bosnia and Herzegovina. Hungary sent a 300-strong logistics unit to Iraq in order to help the U.S. occupation with armed transport convoys, though public opinion opposed the country's participation in the war.<sup class="noprint Inline-Template Template-Fact" style="white-space:nowrap;">&#91;<i><a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Citation_needed" title="Wikipedia:Citation needed"><span title="This claim needs references to reliable sources. (April 2021)">citation needed</span></a></i>&#93;</sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Administrative_divisions">Administrative divisions</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Administrative_divisions_of_Hungary" title="Administrative divisions of Hungary">Administrative divisions of Hungary</a></div>
<p>Hungary is divided into 19 <a href="/wiki/Counties_of_Hungary" title="Counties of Hungary">counties</a> (<i>vármegye</i>). The <a href="/wiki/List_of_historical_capitals_of_Hungary" title="List of historical capitals of Hungary">capital</a> (<i>főváros</i>) <a href="/wiki/Budapest" title="Budapest">Budapest</a> is an independent entity. The counties and the capital are the 20 <a href="/wiki/Nomenclature_of_Territorial_Units_for_Statistics" title="Nomenclature of Territorial Units for Statistics">NUTS</a> third-level units of Hungary. The states are further subdivided into 174 <a href="/wiki/Districts_of_Hungary" title="Districts of Hungary">districts</a> (<i>járás</i>).<sup id="cite_ref-167" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-167">&#91;166&#93;</a></sup> The districts are further divided into <a href="/wiki/List_of_cities_and_towns_of_Hungary" title="List of cities and towns of Hungary">towns and villages</a>, of which 25 are designated <a href="/wiki/City_with_county_rights" title="City with county rights">towns with county rights</a> (<i>megyei jogú város</i>), sometimes known as "urban counties" in English. The local authorities of these towns have extended powers, but these towns belong to the territory of the respective district instead of being independent territorial units. County and district councils and municipalities have different roles and separate responsibilities relating to local government. The role of the counties are basically administrative and focus on strategic development, while preschools, public water utilities, garbage disposal, elderly care, and rescue services are administered by the municipalities.
</p><p>Since 1996, the counties and city of Budapest have been grouped into <a href="/wiki/List_of_regions_of_Hungary" title="List of regions of Hungary">seven regions</a> for statistical and development purposes. These seven regions constitute NUTS' second-level units of Hungary. They are <a href="/wiki/Central_Hungary" title="Central Hungary">Central Hungary</a>, <a href="/wiki/Central_Transdanubia" title="Central Transdanubia">Central Transdanubia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Northern_Great_Plain" title="Northern Great Plain">Northern Great Plain</a>, <a href="/wiki/Northern_Hungary" title="Northern Hungary">Northern Hungary</a>, <a href="/wiki/Southern_Transdanubia" title="Southern Transdanubia">Southern Transdanubia</a>, <a href="/wiki/Southern_Great_Plain" title="Southern Great Plain">Southern Great Plain</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Western_Transdanubia" title="Western Transdanubia">Western Transdanubia</a>.
</p>
