<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Cities_and_towns">Cities and towns</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/List_of_cities_and_towns_of_Hungary" title="List of cities and towns of Hungary">List of cities and towns of Hungary</a></div>
<figure typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Sz%C3%A9chenyi_Chain_Bridge_in_Budapest_at_night.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fd/Sz%C3%A9chenyi_Chain_Bridge_in_Budapest_at_night.jpg/300px-Sz%C3%A9chenyi_Chain_Bridge_in_Budapest_at_night.jpg" decoding="async" width="300" height="168" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fd/Sz%C3%A9chenyi_Chain_Bridge_in_Budapest_at_night.jpg/450px-Sz%C3%A9chenyi_Chain_Bridge_in_Budapest_at_night.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/fd/Sz%C3%A9chenyi_Chain_Bridge_in_Budapest_at_night.jpg/600px-Sz%C3%A9chenyi_Chain_Bridge_in_Budapest_at_night.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3580" data-file-height="2000" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Budapest" title="Budapest">Budapest</a>, the capital and most populous city of Hungary</figcaption></figure>
<p>Hungary has 3,152 <a href="/wiki/Municipalities" class="mw-redirect" title="Municipalities">municipalities</a> as of 15 July 2013: 346 towns (Hungarian term: <span title="Hungarian-language text"><i lang="hu">város</i></span>, plural: <span title="Hungarian-language text"><i lang="hu">városok</i></span>; the terminology does not distinguish between <a href="/wiki/City" title="City">cities</a> and <a href="/wiki/Town" title="Town">towns</a> – the term town is used in official translations) and 2,806 villages (Hungarian: <span title="Hungarian-language text"><i lang="hu">község</i></span>, plural: <span title="Hungarian-language text"><i lang="hu">községek</i></span>) which fully cover the territory of the country. The number of towns can change, since villages can be elevated to town status by act of the president. Budapest has a special status and is not included in any county while 23 of the towns are so-called urban counties (<span title="Hungarian-language text"><i lang="hu">megyei jogú város</i></span> – town with county rights). All county seats except Budapest are urban counties. Four of the cities (Budapest, Miskolc, <a href="/wiki/Gy%C5%91r" title="Győr">Győr</a>, and <a href="/wiki/P%C3%A9cs" title="Pécs">Pécs</a>) have agglomerations, and the Hungarian Statistical Office distinguishes seventeen other areas in earlier stages of agglomeration development.<sup id="cite_ref-portal_168-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-portal-168">&#91;167&#93;</a></sup> The largest city is Budapest, while the smallest town is <a href="/wiki/P%C3%A1lh%C3%A1za" title="Pálháza">Pálháza</a> with 1,038 inhabitants in 2010. The largest village is <a href="/wiki/Solym%C3%A1r" title="Solymár">Solymár</a> with a population of 10,123 as of 2010. There are more than 100 villages with fewer than 100 inhabitants while the smallest villages have fewer than 20 inhabitants.
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Economy">Economy</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Economy_of_Hungary" title="Economy of Hungary">Economy of Hungary</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"><div class="thumb tmulti tleft"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" style="width:312px;max-width:312px"><div class="trow"><div class="tsingle" style="width:92px;max-width:92px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:160px;overflow:hidden"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Szabads%C3%A1g_Square,_Stock_Exchange_Palacve.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Szabads%C3%A1g_Square%2C_Stock_Exchange_Palacve.jpg/90px-Szabads%C3%A1g_Square%2C_Stock_Exchange_Palacve.jpg" decoding="async" width="90" height="159" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Szabads%C3%A1g_Square%2C_Stock_Exchange_Palacve.jpg/135px-Szabads%C3%A1g_Square%2C_Stock_Exchange_Palacve.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/44/Szabads%C3%A1g_Square%2C_Stock_Exchange_Palacve.jpg/180px-Szabads%C3%A1g_Square%2C_Stock_Exchange_Palacve.jpg 2x" data-file-width="2684" data-file-height="4752" /></a></span></div></div><div class="tsingle" style="width:216px;max-width:216px"><div class="thumbimage" style="height:160px;overflow:hidden"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Stock_Exchange_Palace_by_Ign%C3%A1c_Alp%C3%A1r,_detail,_2011_Budape%C5%A1%C5%A5_0799.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/Stock_Exchange_Palace_by_Ign%C3%A1c_Alp%C3%A1r%2C_detail%2C_2011_Budape%C5%A1%C5%A5_0799.jpg/214px-Stock_Exchange_Palace_by_Ign%C3%A1c_Alp%C3%A1r%2C_detail%2C_2011_Budape%C5%A1%C5%A5_0799.jpg" decoding="async" width="214" height="161" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/Stock_Exchange_Palace_by_Ign%C3%A1c_Alp%C3%A1r%2C_detail%2C_2011_Budape%C5%A1%C5%A5_0799.jpg/321px-Stock_Exchange_Palace_by_Ign%C3%A1c_Alp%C3%A1r%2C_detail%2C_2011_Budape%C5%A1%C5%A5_0799.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/7/72/Stock_Exchange_Palace_by_Ign%C3%A1c_Alp%C3%A1r%2C_detail%2C_2011_Budape%C5%A1%C5%A5_0799.jpg/428px-Stock_Exchange_Palace_by_Ign%C3%A1c_Alp%C3%A1r%2C_detail%2C_2011_Budape%C5%A1%C5%A5_0799.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4416" data-file-height="3312" /></a></span></div></div></div><div class="trow" style="display:flex"><div class="thumbcaption">Hungarian Stock Exchange Palace on <a href="/wiki/Liberty_Square_(Budapest)" title="Liberty Square (Budapest)">Liberty Square</a></div></div></div></div>
<p>Hungary is an OECD <a href="/wiki/World_Bank_high-income_economy" title="World Bank high-income economy">high-income</a> <a href="/wiki/Mixed_economy" title="Mixed economy">mixed economy</a> with <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_Human_Development_Index" title="List of countries by Human Development Index">very high human development index</a> and skilled labour force with the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_income_equality" title="List of countries by income equality">16th lowest income inequality</a> in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-169" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-169">&#91;168&#93;</a></sup> Furthermore, it is the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_economic_complexity" title="List of countries by economic complexity">9th most complex economy</a> according to the Economic Complexity Index.<sup id="cite_ref-170" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-170">&#91;169&#93;</a></sup> The economy is the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_(PPP)" title="List of countries by GDP (PPP)">57th-largest in the world</a> (out of 188 countries measured by <a href="/wiki/IMF" class="mw-redirect" title="IMF">IMF</a>) with $265.037&#160;billion output<sup id="cite_ref-imf2_171-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-imf2-171">&#91;170&#93;</a></sup> and ranks <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_GDP_(PPP)_per_capita" title="List of countries by GDP (PPP) per capita">49th in the world in terms of GDP per capita</a> measured by <a href="/wiki/Purchasing_power_parity" title="Purchasing power parity">purchasing power parity</a>. Hungary is an export-oriented <a href="/wiki/Market_economy" title="Market economy">market economy</a> with a heavy emphasis on foreign trade, thus the country is the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_exports" title="List of countries by exports">36th largest export economy</a> in the world. The country has more than $100&#160;billion export in 2015 with high, $9.003&#160;billion <a href="/wiki/Balance_of_trade" title="Balance of trade">trade surplus</a>, of which 79% went to the EU and 21% was extra-EU trade.<sup id="cite_ref-172" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-172">&#91;171&#93;</a></sup> Hungary has a more than 80% <a href="/wiki/Private_sector" title="Private sector">privately owned</a> economy with <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_tax_revenue_to_GDP_ratio" class="mw-redirect" title="List of countries by tax revenue to GDP ratio">39.1% overall taxation</a>, which provides the basis for the country's <a href="/wiki/Welfare_state" title="Welfare state">welfare economy</a>. On the expenditure side, household consumption is the main component of <a href="/wiki/Gross_domestic_product" title="Gross domestic product">GDP</a> and accounts for 50% of its total use, followed by gross fixed <a href="/wiki/Capital_formation" title="Capital formation">capital formation</a> with 22% and government expenditure with 20%.<sup id="cite_ref-173" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-173">&#91;172&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Hungary_Product_Exports_(2019).svg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4b/Hungary_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/290px-Hungary_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png" decoding="async" width="290" height="189" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4b/Hungary_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/435px-Hungary_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/4b/Hungary_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg/580px-Hungary_Product_Exports_%282019%29.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="1100" data-file-height="715" /></a><figcaption>A proportional representation of Hungary's exports, 2019</figcaption></figure>
<p>Hungary continues to be one of the leading nations for attracting <a href="/wiki/Foreign_direct_investment" title="Foreign direct investment">foreign direct investment</a> (FDI) in <a href="/wiki/Central_and_Eastern_Europe" title="Central and Eastern Europe">Central and Eastern Europe</a>, the inward FDI in the country was $119.8&#160;billion in 2015, while investing more than $50&#160;billion abroad.<sup id="cite_ref-174" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-174">&#91;173&#93;</a></sup> As of 2015<sup class="plainlinks noexcerpt noprint asof-tag update" style="display:none;"><a class="external text" href="https://en.wikipedia.org/w/index.php?title=Hungary&amp;action=edit">&#91;update&#93;</a></sup>, the key trading partners were Germany, Austria, Romania, Slovakia, France, Italy, Poland and Czech Republic.<sup id="cite_ref-175" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-175">&#91;174&#93;</a></sup> Major industries include food processing, pharmaceuticals, motor vehicles, information technology, chemicals, metallurgy, machinery, electrical goods, and tourism (with 12.1&#160;million international tourists in 2014).<sup id="cite_ref-176" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-176">&#91;175&#93;</a></sup> Hungary is the largest electronics producer in Central and Eastern Europe. Electronics manufacturing and research are among the main drivers of innovation and economic growth in the country. In the past 20 years Hungary has also grown into a major centre for <a href="/wiki/Mobile_technology" title="Mobile technology">mobile technology</a>, <a href="/wiki/Information_security" title="Information security">information security</a>, and related hardware research.<sup id="cite_ref-177" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-177">&#91;176&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Employment-to-population_ratio" title="Employment-to-population ratio">employment rate</a> was 68.3% in 2017;<sup id="cite_ref-178" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-178">&#91;177&#93;</a></sup> the employment structure shows the characteristics of <a href="/wiki/Post-industrial_society" title="Post-industrial society">post-industrial economies</a>, 63.2% of employed workforce work in service sector, the industry contributed by 29.7%, while agriculture with 7.1%. Unemployment rate was 4.1% in 2017,<sup id="cite_ref-179" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-179">&#91;178&#93;</a></sup> down from 11% during the <a href="/wiki/Financial_crisis_of_2007%E2%80%932008" class="mw-redirect" title="Financial crisis of 2007–2008">financial crisis of 2007–2008</a>. Hungary is part of the <a href="/wiki/European_single_market" title="European single market">European single market</a> which represents more than 508&#160;million consumers. Several domestic commercial policies are determined by agreements among European Union members and by EU legislation.
</p>
<figure class="mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Magyar_Nemzeti_Bank.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Magyar_Nemzeti_Bank.jpg/150px-Magyar_Nemzeti_Bank.jpg" decoding="async" width="150" height="227" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Magyar_Nemzeti_Bank.jpg/225px-Magyar_Nemzeti_Bank.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/4/40/Magyar_Nemzeti_Bank.jpg/300px-Magyar_Nemzeti_Bank.jpg 2x" data-file-width="3259" data-file-height="4924" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Hungarian_National_Bank" title="Hungarian National Bank">Hungarian National Bank</a>'s building in <a href="/wiki/Budapest" title="Budapest">Budapest</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Large <a href="/wiki/List_of_companies_of_Hungary" title="List of companies of Hungary">Hungarian companies</a> are included in the <a href="/wiki/BUX" title="BUX">BUX</a>, the stock market index listed on <a href="/wiki/Budapest_Stock_Exchange" title="Budapest Stock Exchange">Budapest Stock Exchange</a>. Well-known companies include the <a href="/wiki/Fortune_Global_500" title="Fortune Global 500">Fortune Global 500</a> firm <a href="/wiki/MOL_Group" class="mw-redirect" title="MOL Group">MOL Group</a>, the <a href="/wiki/OTP_Bank" title="OTP Bank">OTP Bank</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gedeon_Richter_Plc." class="mw-redirect" title="Gedeon Richter Plc.">Gedeon Richter Plc.</a>, <a href="/wiki/Magyar_Telekom" title="Magyar Telekom">Magyar Telekom</a>, <a href="/wiki/CIG_Pannonia" title="CIG Pannonia">CIG Pannonia</a>, <a href="/wiki/FHB_Mortgage_Bank" title="FHB Mortgage Bank">FHB Bank</a>, <a href="/wiki/Zwack_liqueur" class="mw-redirect" title="Zwack liqueur">Zwack Unicum</a> and more.<sup id="cite_ref-180" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-180">&#91;179&#93;</a></sup> Besides this Hungary has a large portion of specialised <a href="/wiki/Small_and_medium-sized_enterprises" title="Small and medium-sized enterprises">small and medium enterprise</a>, for example a significant number of automotive suppliers and technology <a href="/wiki/Startup_company" title="Startup company">start ups</a> among others.<sup id="cite_ref-181" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-181">&#91;180&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Budapest is the financial and business capital, classified as an Alpha <a href="/wiki/Global_city" title="Global city">world city</a> in the study by the <a href="/wiki/Globalization_and_World_Cities_Research_Network" title="Globalization and World Cities Research Network">Globalization and World Cities Research Network</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-182" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-182">&#91;181&#93;</a></sup> On the national level, Budapest is the <a href="/wiki/Primate_city" title="Primate city">primate city</a> of Hungary regarding business and economy, accounting for 39% of the national income, the city has a <a href="/wiki/Gross_metropolitan_product" title="Gross metropolitan product">gross metropolitan product</a> more than $100&#160;billion in 2015, making it one of the largest regional economies in the European Union.<sup id="cite_ref-183" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-183">&#91;182&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-184" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-184">&#91;183&#93;</a></sup> Budapest is also among the Top 100 GDP performing cities in the world, measured by <a href="/wiki/PricewaterhouseCoopers" class="mw-redirect" title="PricewaterhouseCoopers">PricewaterhouseCoopers</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-185" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-185">&#91;184&#93;</a></sup> Furthermore, Hungary's corporate tax rate is only 9%, which is relatively low for EU states.<sup id="cite_ref-186" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-186">&#91;185&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Hungary maintains its own currency, the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_forint" title="Hungarian forint">Hungarian forint</a> (HUF), although the economy fulfills the <a href="/wiki/Euro_convergence_criteria" title="Euro convergence criteria">Maastricht criteria</a> with the exception of public debt, <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_public_debt" class="mw-redirect" title="List of countries by public debt">but it is also significantly below the EU average</a> with the level of 75.3% in 2015. The <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_National_Bank" title="Hungarian National Bank">Hungarian National Bank</a>—founded in 1924, after the dissolution of Austro-Hungarian Empire—is currently focusing on price stability with an inflation target of 3%.<sup id="cite_ref-187" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-187">&#91;186&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Science_and_technology">Science and technology</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Science_and_technology_in_Hungary" title="Science and technology in Hungary">Science and technology in Hungary</a> and <a href="/wiki/List_of_Hungarian_Nobel_laureates" title="List of Hungarian Nobel laureates">List of Hungarian Nobel laureates</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Szentgyorgyi_Albert_es_a_c_vitamin_keplete_1937_okt_31_Pesti_Naplo.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/Szentgyorgyi_Albert_es_a_c_vitamin_keplete_1937_okt_31_Pesti_Naplo.jpg/220px-Szentgyorgyi_Albert_es_a_c_vitamin_keplete_1937_okt_31_Pesti_Naplo.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="161" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/Szentgyorgyi_Albert_es_a_c_vitamin_keplete_1937_okt_31_Pesti_Naplo.jpg/330px-Szentgyorgyi_Albert_es_a_c_vitamin_keplete_1937_okt_31_Pesti_Naplo.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/1/19/Szentgyorgyi_Albert_es_a_c_vitamin_keplete_1937_okt_31_Pesti_Naplo.jpg/440px-Szentgyorgyi_Albert_es_a_c_vitamin_keplete_1937_okt_31_Pesti_Naplo.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1118" data-file-height="817" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Albert_Szent-Gy%C3%B6rgyi" title="Albert Szent-Györgyi">Albert Szent-Györgyi</a> won the <a href="/wiki/Nobel_Prize_in_Physiology_or_Medicine" title="Nobel Prize in Physiology or Medicine">Nobel Prize in Physiology or Medicine</a> for his discovery of <a href="/wiki/Vitamin_C" title="Vitamin C">Vitamin C</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Nobel_Prize" title="Nobel Prize">Nobel Prize</a> has been awarded to <a href="/wiki/List_of_Hungarian_Nobel_laureates" title="List of Hungarian Nobel laureates">13 Hungarians</a>.</figcaption></figure>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Hungary-02046_-_Budapest_University_of_Technology_and_Economics_(31670993764).jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cc/Hungary-02046_-_Budapest_University_of_Technology_and_Economics_%2831670993764%29.jpg/220px-Hungary-02046_-_Budapest_University_of_Technology_and_Economics_%2831670993764%29.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="147" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cc/Hungary-02046_-_Budapest_University_of_Technology_and_Economics_%2831670993764%29.jpg/330px-Hungary-02046_-_Budapest_University_of_Technology_and_Economics_%2831670993764%29.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/c/cc/Hungary-02046_-_Budapest_University_of_Technology_and_Economics_%2831670993764%29.jpg/440px-Hungary-02046_-_Budapest_University_of_Technology_and_Economics_%2831670993764%29.jpg 2x" data-file-width="6000" data-file-height="4000" /></a><figcaption>Founded in 1782, the <a href="/wiki/Budapest_University_of_Technology_and_Economics" title="Budapest University of Technology and Economics">Budapest University of Technology and Economics</a> is the oldest <a href="/wiki/Institute_of_technology" title="Institute of technology">institute of technology</a> in the world.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Hungary's achievements in science and technology have been significant, and research and development efforts form an integral part of the country's economy. Hungary spent 1.61% of its gross domestic product (GDP) on civil research and development in 2020, which is the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_research_and_development_spending" class="mw-redirect" title="List of countries by research and development spending">25th highest ratio in the world</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-oecd_188-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-oecd-188">&#91;187&#93;</a></sup> Hungary ranks 32nd among the most innovative countries in the <a href="/wiki/Bloomberg_L.P." title="Bloomberg L.P.">Bloomberg Innovation Index</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-189" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-189">&#91;188&#93;</a></sup> Hungary was ranked 34th in the <a href="/wiki/Global_Innovation_Index" title="Global Innovation Index">Global Innovation Index</a> in 2021, down from 33rd in 2019.<sup id="cite_ref-190" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-190">&#91;189&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-191" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-191">&#91;190&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-192" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-192">&#91;191&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-193" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-193">&#91;192&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-194" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-194">&#91;193&#93;</a></sup> In 2014, Hungary counted 2,651 full-time equivalent researchers per million inhabitants, steadily increasing from 2,131 in 2010 and compares with 3,984 in the U.S. or 4,380 in Germany.<sup id="cite_ref-195" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-195">&#91;194&#93;</a></sup> Hungary's high technology industry has benefited from both the country's skilled workforce and the strong presence of foreign high-tech firms and research centres. Hungary also has one of the highest rates of filed patents, the sixth highest ratio of high-tech and medium high-tech output in the total industrial output, the 12th highest research FDI inflow, placed 14th in research talent in business enterprise and has the 17th best overall innovation efficiency ratio in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-196" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-196">&#91;195&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>The key actor of research and development in Hungary is the National Research, Development and Innovation (NRDI) Office, which is a national strategic and <a href="/wiki/Funding_of_science" title="Funding of science">funding agency for scientific research, development and innovation</a>, the primary source of advice on RDI policy for the Hungarian government and the primary RDI funding agency. Its role is to develop RDI policy and ensure that Hungary adequately invest in RDI by funding excellent research and supporting innovation to increase competitiveness and to prepare the RDI strategy of the government, to handle the NRDI Fund and represents the government and RDI community in international organisations.<sup id="cite_ref-197" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-197">&#91;196&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Scientific research is supported partly by industry and partly by the state, through universities and by scientific state-institutions such as <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_Academy_of_Sciences" title="Hungarian Academy of Sciences">Hungarian Academy of Sciences</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-198" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-198">&#91;197&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-199" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-199">&#91;198&#93;</a></sup> Hungary has been the home of some of the most prominent researchers in various scientific disciplines, notably physics, mathematics, chemistry and engineering. As of 2018, <a href="/wiki/List_of_Hungarian_Nobel_laureates" title="List of Hungarian Nobel laureates">thirteen Hungarian scientists</a> have been recipients of a <a href="/wiki/Nobel_Prize" title="Nobel Prize">Nobel Prize</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-200" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-200">&#91;199&#93;</a></sup> Until 2012 three individuals—Csoma, <a href="/wiki/J%C3%A1nos_Bolyai" title="János Bolyai">János Bolyai</a> and Tihanyi—were included in the UNESCO <a href="/wiki/Memory_of_the_World_Programme" title="Memory of the World Programme">Memory of the World</a> register as well as the collective contributions <a href="/wiki/Tabula_Hungariae" title="Tabula Hungariae">Tabula Hungariae</a> and <a href="/wiki/Bibliotheca_Corviniana" title="Bibliotheca Corviniana">Bibliotheca Corviniana</a>. Contemporary scientists include mathematician <a href="/wiki/L%C3%A1szl%C3%B3_Lov%C3%A1sz" title="László Lovász">László Lovász</a>, physicist <a href="/wiki/Albert-L%C3%A1szl%C3%B3_Barab%C3%A1si" title="Albert-László Barabási">Albert-László Barabási</a>, physicist <a href="/wiki/Ferenc_Krausz" title="Ferenc Krausz">Ferenc Krausz</a>, and biochemist <a href="/wiki/%C3%81rp%C3%A1d_Pusztai" title="Árpád Pusztai">Árpád Pusztai</a>. Hungary has excellent <a href="/wiki/Mathematics_education" title="Mathematics education">mathematics education</a> which has trained numerous outstanding scientists. Famous Hungarian mathematicians include father <a href="/wiki/Farkas_Bolyai" title="Farkas Bolyai">Farkas Bolyai</a> and son <a href="/wiki/J%C3%A1nos_Bolyai" title="János Bolyai">János Bolyai</a>, who was one of the founders of <a href="/wiki/Non-Euclidean_geometry" title="Non-Euclidean geometry">non-Euclidean geometry</a>; <a href="/wiki/Paul_Erd%C5%91s" title="Paul Erdős">Paul Erdős</a>, famed for publishing in over forty languages and whose <a href="/wiki/Erd%C5%91s_number" title="Erdős number">Erdős numbers</a> are still tracked, and <a href="/wiki/John_von_Neumann" title="John von Neumann">John von Neumann</a>, a key contributor in the fields of <a href="/wiki/Quantum_mechanics" title="Quantum mechanics">quantum mechanics</a> and <a href="/wiki/Game_theory" title="Game theory">game theory</a>, a pioneer of digital computing, and the chief mathematician in the <a href="/wiki/Manhattan_Project" title="Manhattan Project">Manhattan Project</a>. Notable Hungarian inventions include the <a href="/wiki/Lead_dioxide" title="Lead dioxide">lead dioxide</a> <a href="/wiki/Match" title="Match">match</a> (<a href="/wiki/J%C3%A1nos_Irinyi" title="János Irinyi">János Irinyi</a>), a type of <a href="/wiki/Carburetor" title="Carburetor">carburetor</a> (<a href="/wiki/Don%C3%A1t_B%C3%A1nki" title="Donát Bánki">Donát Bánki</a>, <a href="/wiki/J%C3%A1nos_Csonka" title="János Csonka">János Csonka</a>), the electric (AC) train engine and generator (<a href="/wiki/K%C3%A1lm%C3%A1n_Kand%C3%B3" title="Kálmán Kandó">Kálmán Kandó</a>), <a href="/wiki/Holography" title="Holography">holography</a> (<a href="/wiki/Dennis_Gabor" title="Dennis Gabor">Dennis Gabor</a>), the <a href="/wiki/Kalman_filter" title="Kalman filter">Kalman filter</a> (<a href="/wiki/Rudolf_E._K%C3%A1lm%C3%A1n" title="Rudolf E. Kálmán">Rudolf E. Kálmán</a>), and <a href="/wiki/Rubik%27s_Cube" title="Rubik&#39;s Cube">Rubik's Cube</a> (<a href="/wiki/Ern%C5%91_Rubik" title="Ernő Rubik">Ernő Rubik</a>).
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Transport">Transport</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Transport_in_Hungary" title="Transport in Hungary">Transport in Hungary</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Esztergom.desiro.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Esztergom.desiro.JPG/220px-Esztergom.desiro.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="105" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Esztergom.desiro.JPG/330px-Esztergom.desiro.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/59/Esztergom.desiro.JPG/440px-Esztergom.desiro.JPG 2x" data-file-width="2271" data-file-height="1088" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Siemens_Desiro" title="Siemens Desiro">Siemens Desiro</a> passenger trains on the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_State_Railways" title="Hungarian State Railways">Hungarian State Railways</a> network, which is <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_rail_transport_network_size" title="List of countries by rail transport network size">one of the densest in the world</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Hungary has a highly developed road, railway, air, and water transport system. Budapest serves as an important hub for the <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_State_Railways" title="Hungarian State Railways">Hungarian railway system</a> (<i>MÁV</i>). The capital is served by three large train stations called <i><a href="/wiki/Budapest_Keleti_station" title="Budapest Keleti station">Keleti</a></i> (Eastern), <i><a href="/wiki/Budapest_Nyugati_station" title="Budapest Nyugati station">Nyugati</a></i> (Western), and <i><a href="/wiki/Budapest_D%C3%A9li_station" title="Budapest Déli station">Déli</a></i> (Southern) <i>pályaudvar</i>s (termii). <a href="/wiki/Szolnok" title="Szolnok">Szolnok</a> is the most important railway hub outside Budapest, while <a href="/wiki/Miskolc_Tiszai_railway_station" title="Miskolc Tiszai railway station">Tiszai Railway Station</a> in Miskolc and the main stations of <a href="/wiki/Szombathely" title="Szombathely">Szombathely</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gy%C5%91r" title="Győr">Győr</a>, <a href="/wiki/Szeged" title="Szeged">Szeged</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Sz%C3%A9kesfeh%C3%A9rv%C3%A1r" title="Székesfehérvár">Székesfehérvár</a> are also key to the network.
</p><p>Budapest, <a href="/wiki/Debrecen" title="Debrecen">Debrecen</a>, Miskolc, and <a href="/wiki/Szeged" title="Szeged">Szeged</a> have tram networks. The <a href="/wiki/Budapest_Metro" title="Budapest Metro">Budapest Metro</a> is the second-oldest underground <a href="/wiki/Rapid_transit" title="Rapid transit">metro</a> system in the world; its <a href="/wiki/Metro_Line_M1_(Budapest_Metro)" title="Metro Line M1 (Budapest Metro)">Line 1</a> dates from 1896. The system consists of four lines. A <a href="/wiki/Regional_rail" title="Regional rail">commuter rail</a> system, <i><a href="/wiki/BH%C3%89V" title="BHÉV">HÉV</a></i>, operates in the Budapest metropolitan area.
Hungary has a total length of approximately 1,314&#160;km (816.48&#160;mi) motorways (<a href="/wiki/Hungarian_language" title="Hungarian language">Hungarian</a>: <i lang="hu">autópálya</i>). Motorway sections are being added to the existing network, which already connects many major economically important cities to the capital. Ports are located at Budapest, <a href="/wiki/Duna%C3%BAjv%C3%A1ros" title="Dunaújváros">Dunaújváros</a> and <a href="/wiki/Baja,_Hungary" title="Baja, Hungary">Baja</a>.
</p><p>There are five international airports: <a href="/wiki/Budapest_Ferenc_Liszt_International_Airport" title="Budapest Ferenc Liszt International Airport">Budapest Ferenc Liszt</a> (informally called "Ferihegy"), <a href="/wiki/Debrecen_International_Airport" title="Debrecen International Airport">Debrecen</a>, <a href="/wiki/H%C3%A9v%C3%ADz%E2%80%93Balaton_Airport" title="Hévíz–Balaton Airport">Hévíz–Balaton</a> (also called Sármellék Airport), <a href="/wiki/Gy%C5%91r-P%C3%A9r_International_Airport" title="Győr-Pér International Airport">Győr-Pér</a>, and <a href="/wiki/P%C3%A9cs-Pog%C3%A1ny_International_Airport" title="Pécs-Pogány International Airport">Pécs-Pogány</a>, but only two of these (Budapest and Debrecen) receive scheduled flights. Low-budget airline <a href="/wiki/Wizz_Air" title="Wizz Air">Wizz Air</a> is based at Ferihegy.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Energy">Energy</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Energy_in_Hungary" title="Energy in Hungary">Energy in Hungary</a> and <a href="/wiki/Renewable_energy_in_Hungary" title="Renewable energy in Hungary">Renewable energy in Hungary</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Atomer%C5%91m%C5%B1_f%C5%91bej%C3%A1rat.JPG" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/Atomer%C5%91m%C5%B1_f%C5%91bej%C3%A1rat.JPG/220px-Atomer%C5%91m%C5%B1_f%C5%91bej%C3%A1rat.JPG" decoding="async" width="220" height="165" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/Atomer%C5%91m%C5%B1_f%C5%91bej%C3%A1rat.JPG/330px-Atomer%C5%91m%C5%B1_f%C5%91bej%C3%A1rat.JPG 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/f/f2/Atomer%C5%91m%C5%B1_f%C5%91bej%C3%A1rat.JPG/440px-Atomer%C5%91m%C5%B1_f%C5%91bej%C3%A1rat.JPG 2x" data-file-width="3648" data-file-height="2736" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Paks_Nuclear_Power_Plant" title="Paks Nuclear Power Plant">Paks Nuclear Power Plant</a> produced more than 50% of Hungary's electricity production.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Hungary's total energy supply is dominated by <a href="/wiki/Fossil_fuel" title="Fossil fuel">fossil fuels</a>, with <a href="/wiki/Natural_gas" title="Natural gas">natural gas</a> occupying the largest share, followed by <a href="/wiki/Petroleum" title="Petroleum">oil</a> and <a href="/wiki/Coal" title="Coal">coal</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_201-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-201">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> In June 2020, Hungary passed a law binding itself to a target of <a href="/wiki/Carbon_neutrality" class="mw-redirect" title="Carbon neutrality">net-zero emissions</a> by 2050. As part of a broader restructuring of the nation's energy and climate policies, Hungary also extended its National Energy Strategy 2030 to look even further, adding an outlook until 2040 that prioritizes carbon-neutral and cost-effective energy while focusing on reinforcing <a href="/wiki/Energy_security" title="Energy security">energy security</a> and <a href="/wiki/Energy_independence" title="Energy independence">energy independence</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_201-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-201">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup> Key forces in the country's 2050 target include <a href="/wiki/Renewable_energy" title="Renewable energy">renewables</a>, <a href="/wiki/Nuclear_power" title="Nuclear power">nuclear</a> electricity, and <a href="/wiki/Electrification" title="Electrification">electrification</a> of end-use sectors. Significant investments in the power sector are expected, including for the construction of two new nuclear energy generating units. Renewable energy capacity has increased significantly, but in recent years growth in the renewables sector has stagnated. What is more, certain policies that limit development of <a href="/wiki/Wind_power" title="Wind power">wind power</a> are expected to negatively impact the renewables sector.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_201-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-201">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Hungary's emission of greenhouse gases has dropped alongside the economy's decreasing use of carbon-based fuels. However, independent analysis has identified space for Hungary to set more ambitious emissions reduction targets.<sup id="cite_ref-:0_201-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-:0-201">&#91;200&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Demographics">Demographics</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_Hungary" title="Demographics of Hungary">Demographics of Hungary</a>, <a href="/wiki/Hungarians" title="Hungarians">Hungarians</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Women_in_Hungary" title="Women in Hungary">Women in Hungary</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Population_density_in_Hungary.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/Population_density_in_Hungary.png/290px-Population_density_in_Hungary.png" decoding="async" width="290" height="180" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/Population_density_in_Hungary.png/435px-Population_density_in_Hungary.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/e/e6/Population_density_in_Hungary.png/580px-Population_density_in_Hungary.png 2x" data-file-width="870" data-file-height="540" /></a><figcaption>Population density in Hungary by <a href="/wiki/Districts_of_Hungary" title="Districts of Hungary">district</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Hungary's population was 9,689,000 in 2021, according to the Hungarian Central Statistical Office, making it the fifth most populous country in Central and Eastern Europe, and a <a href="/wiki/Demographics_of_the_European_Union" title="Demographics of the European Union">medium-sized member state of the European Union</a>. As in other former <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Bloc" title="Eastern Bloc">Eastern bloc countries</a>, its population has decreased markedly since the fall of the Soviet Union, having peaked at 10.8 million in 1980.<sup id="cite_ref-202" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-202">&#91;201&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Population_density" title="Population density">Population density</a> stands at 107 inhabitants per square kilometre, which is about two times higher than the <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_and_dependencies_by_population_density" title="List of countries and dependencies by population density">world average</a>. Around 70% of the population lives in cities and towns overall, which is well above the global rate of 56% but <a href="/wiki/Urbanization_by_sovereign_state" class="mw-redirect" title="Urbanization by sovereign state">lower than most developed countries</a>; one quarter of Hungarians live in the <a href="/wiki/Budapest_metropolitan_area" title="Budapest metropolitan area">Budapest metropolitan area</a> in north-central region.
</p><p>Like most European countries, Hungary is experiencing <a href="/wiki/Sub-replacement_fertility" title="Sub-replacement fertility">sub-replacement fertility</a>; its estimated <a href="/wiki/Total_fertility_rate" title="Total fertility rate">total fertility rate</a> of 1.43 children per woman is well below the replacement rate of 2.1,<sup id="cite_ref-203" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-203">&#91;202&#93;</a></sup> and considerably lower than the peak of 5.59 children born per woman in 1884.<sup id="cite_ref-204" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-204">&#91;203&#93;</a></sup> Consequently, its population has been gradually declining and rapidly aging; the average age is 42.7 years, among the highest in the world.<sup id="cite_ref-205" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-205">&#91;204&#93;</a></sup> This trend has been exacerbated by a high rate of emigration, particularly among young adults, and anti-immigration policies, which accelerated in the 1990s but have since somewhat abated.<sup id="cite_ref-206" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-206">&#91;205&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In 2011, the conservative government began a programme to increase the birth rate among <a href="/wiki/Hungarians" title="Hungarians">ethnic Magyars</a> by reinstating three-year maternity leave and boosting the availability of part-time jobs; the fertility rate has since gradually increased from its nadir of 1.27 children per woman in 2011,<sup id="cite_ref-207" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-207">&#91;206&#93;</a></sup> in some years rising as high as 1.5.<sup id="cite_ref-208" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-208">&#91;207&#93;</a></sup> In 2023, the population grew by 0.77% compared to the prior year, due mostly to an influx of immigrants from neighboring countries.<sup id="cite_ref-209" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-209">&#91;208&#93;</a></sup> In 2015, 47.9% of births were to unmarried women.<sup id="cite_ref-210" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-210">&#91;209&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Life_expectancy" title="Life expectancy">Life expectancy</a> was 71.96 years for men and 79.62 years for women in 2015,<sup id="cite_ref-211" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-211">&#91;210&#93;</a></sup> growing continuously since the fall of Communism.<sup id="cite_ref-212" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-212">&#91;211&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Hungary recognises two sizeable minority groups, designated as "national minorities" because their ancestors have lived in their respective regions for centuries in Hungary: a <a href="/wiki/Germans_of_Hungary" title="Germans of Hungary">German community</a> of about 130,000 that lives throughout the country, and a <a href="/wiki/Romani_people_in_Hungary" title="Romani people in Hungary">Romani minority</a> that numbers around 300,000 and mainly resides in the northern part of the country. Some studies indicate a considerably larger number of Romani in Hungary (876,000 people – c. 9% of the population.).<sup id="cite_ref-213" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-213">&#91;212&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-214" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-214">&#91;213&#93;</a></sup> According to the 2011 census, there were 8,314,029 (83.7%) ethnic <a href="/wiki/Hungarians" title="Hungarians">Hungarians</a>, 308,957 (3.1%) <a href="/wiki/Romani_people" title="Romani people">Romani</a>, 131,951 (1.3%) <a href="/wiki/Germans" title="Germans">Germans</a>, 29,647 (0.3%) <a href="/wiki/Slovaks" title="Slovaks">Slovaks</a>, 26,345 (0.3%) <a href="/wiki/Romanians" title="Romanians">Romanians</a>, and 23,561 (0.2%) <a href="/wiki/Croats" title="Croats">Croats</a> in Hungary; 1,455,883 people (14.7% of the total population) did not declare their ethnicity. Thus, Hungarians made up more than 90% of people who declared their ethnicity.<sup id="cite_ref-Census2011det_5-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Census2011det-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> In Hungary, people can declare more than one ethnicity, so the sum of ethnicities is higher than the total population.<sup id="cite_ref-215" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-215">&#91;214&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Approximately <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_diaspora" title="Hungarian diaspora">5&#160;million Hungarians live outside Hungary</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Languages">Languages</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_language" title="Hungarian language">Hungarian language</a> and <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Hungary" title="Languages of Hungary">Languages of Hungary</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:MagyarsOutsideHungary.png" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/51/MagyarsOutsideHungary.png/290px-MagyarsOutsideHungary.png" decoding="async" width="290" height="197" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/51/MagyarsOutsideHungary.png/435px-MagyarsOutsideHungary.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/5/51/MagyarsOutsideHungary.png/580px-MagyarsOutsideHungary.png 2x" data-file-width="857" data-file-height="581" /></a><figcaption>Regions of Central and Eastern Europe inhabited by Hungarian speakers today</figcaption></figure>
<p><a href="/wiki/Hungarian_language" title="Hungarian language">Hungarian</a> is the <a href="/wiki/Official_language" title="Official language">official</a> and predominant spoken language. Hungarian is the <a href="/wiki/Languages_of_Europe" title="Languages of Europe">13th most widely spoken first language in Europe</a> with around 13&#160;million native speakers and it is one of 24 official and working languages of the European Union.<sup id="cite_ref-216" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-216">&#91;215&#93;</a></sup> Outside Hungary, it is also spoken in neighbouring countries and by Hungarian diaspora communities worldwide. According to the 2011 census, 9,896,333 people (99.6%) speak Hungarian in Hungary, of whom 9,827,875 people (99%) speak it as a <a href="/wiki/First_language" title="First language">first language</a>, while 68,458 people (0.7%) speak it as a <a href="/wiki/Second_language" title="Second language">second language</a>.<sup id="cite_ref-Census2011det_5-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Census2011det-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup> English (1,589,180 speakers, 16.0%), and German (1,111,997 speakers, 11.2%) are the most widely spoken foreign languages, while there are several recognised minority languages in Hungary (<a href="/wiki/Armenian_language" title="Armenian language">Armenian</a>, <a href="/wiki/Bulgarian_language" title="Bulgarian language">Bulgarian</a>, <a href="/wiki/Croatian_language" title="Croatian language">Croatian</a>, <a href="/wiki/German_language" title="German language">German</a>, <a href="/wiki/Greek_language" title="Greek language">Greek</a>, <a href="/wiki/Romanian_language" title="Romanian language">Romanian</a>, <a href="/wiki/Romani_language" title="Romani language">Romani</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rusyn_language" title="Rusyn language">Rusyn</a>, <a href="/wiki/Serbian_language" title="Serbian language">Serbian</a>, <a href="/wiki/Slovak_language" title="Slovak language">Slovak</a>, <a href="/wiki/Slovenian_language" class="mw-redirect" title="Slovenian language">Slovenian</a>, and <a href="/wiki/Ukrainian_language" title="Ukrainian language">Ukrainian</a>).<sup id="cite_ref-Census2011_217-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Census2011-217">&#91;216&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Hungarian is a member of the <a href="/wiki/Uralic_languages" title="Uralic languages">Uralic language family</a>, unrelated to any neighbouring language and distantly related to <a href="/wiki/Finnish_language" title="Finnish language">Finnish</a> and <a href="/wiki/Estonian_language" title="Estonian language">Estonian</a>. It is the largest of the Uralic languages in terms of the number of speakers and the only one spoken in Central Europe. There are sizeable populations of Hungarian speakers in Romania, the Czech Republic, Slovakia, the former Yugoslavia, Ukraine, Israel, and the U.S. Smaller groups of Hungarian speakers live in Canada, Slovenia, and Austria, but also in Australia, Brazil, Argentina, Mexico, Venezuela, and Chile. Standard Hungarian is based on the variety spoken Budapest. Although the use of the standard dialect is enforced, Hungarian has several urban and rural <a href="/wiki/Dialect" title="Dialect">dialects</a>.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Religion">Religion</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Religion_in_Hungary" title="Religion in Hungary">Religion in Hungary</a></div>
<p>Hungary is a historically <a href="/wiki/Christianity" title="Christianity">Christian</a> country. Hungarian historiography identifies the foundation of the Hungarian state with Stephen I's <a href="/wiki/Baptism" title="Baptism">baptism</a> and coronation with the <a href="/wiki/Holy_Crown_of_Hungary" title="Holy Crown of Hungary">Holy Crown</a> in A.D. 1000. Stephen promulgated <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church" title="Catholic Church">Catholicism</a> as the state religion, and his successors were traditionally known as the <a href="/wiki/Apostolic_Majesty" title="Apostolic Majesty">Apostolic Kings</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Hungary" title="Catholic Church in Hungary">Catholic Church in Hungary</a> remained strong through the centuries, and the <a href="/wiki/Roman_Catholic_Archdiocese_of_Esztergom%E2%80%93Budapest" title="Roman Catholic Archdiocese of Esztergom–Budapest">Archbishop of Esztergom</a> was granted extraordinary temporal privileges as <a href="/wiki/Prince-primate" title="Prince-primate">prince-primate</a> (<i>hercegprímás</i>) of Hungary.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Bencz%C3%BAr_-_Painting_of_St_Stephen_in_the_Basilica_of_Budapest.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/31/Bencz%C3%BAr_-_Painting_of_St_Stephen_in_the_Basilica_of_Budapest.jpg/220px-Bencz%C3%BAr_-_Painting_of_St_Stephen_in_the_Basilica_of_Budapest.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="317" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/31/Bencz%C3%BAr_-_Painting_of_St_Stephen_in_the_Basilica_of_Budapest.jpg/330px-Bencz%C3%BAr_-_Painting_of_St_Stephen_in_the_Basilica_of_Budapest.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/3/31/Bencz%C3%BAr_-_Painting_of_St_Stephen_in_the_Basilica_of_Budapest.jpg/440px-Bencz%C3%BAr_-_Painting_of_St_Stephen_in_the_Basilica_of_Budapest.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1796" data-file-height="2585" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Stephen_I_of_Hungary" title="Stephen I of Hungary">King Saint Stephen</a> offering the Hungarian crown to <a href="/wiki/Mary,_mother_of_Jesus" title="Mary, mother of Jesus">Virgin Mary</a> – painting by Gyula Benczúr, in the St. Stephen's Basilica</figcaption></figure>
<p>Although contemporary Hungary has no <a href="/wiki/State_religion" title="State religion">official religion</a> and recognises <a href="/wiki/Freedom_of_religion" title="Freedom of religion">freedom of religion</a> as a fundamental right, the constitution "recognises Christianity's nation-building role" in its preamble<sup id="cite_ref-218" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-218">&#91;217&#93;</a></sup> and in Article VII affirms that "the state may cooperate with the churches for community goals."<sup id="cite_ref-219" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-219">&#91;218&#93;</a></sup> The 2011 census showed that the majority of Hungarians were Christians (54.2%), with <a href="/wiki/Catholic_Church_in_Hungary" title="Catholic Church in Hungary">Roman Catholics</a> (<i>római katolikusok</i>) (37.1%) and <a href="/wiki/Reformed_Church_in_Hungary" title="Reformed Church in Hungary">Hungarian Reformed</a> <a href="/wiki/Calvinism" title="Calvinism">Calvinists</a> (<i>reformátusok</i>) (11.1%) making up the bulk of these alongside <a href="/wiki/Lutheranism" title="Lutheranism">Lutherans</a> (<i>evangélikusok</i>) (2.2%), <a href="/wiki/Greek_Catholic_Church" title="Greek Catholic Church">Greek Catholics</a> (1.8%), and other Christians (1.3%). Jewish (0.1%), Buddhist (0.1%) and Muslim (0.06%) communities are in the minority. 27.2% of the population did not declare a religious affiliation while 16.7% declared themselves explicitly irreligious, another 1.5% atheist.<sup id="cite_ref-Census2011det_5-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Census2011det-5">&#91;4&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>During the initial stages of the Protestant <a href="/wiki/Reformation" title="Reformation">Reformation</a>, most Hungarians adopted first Lutheranism and then Calvinism in the form of the Hungarian Reformed Church. In the second half of the 16th century, the <a href="/wiki/Society_of_Jesus" class="mw-redirect" title="Society of Jesus">Jesuits</a> led a <a href="/wiki/Counter-Reformation" title="Counter-Reformation">Counter-Reformation</a> campaign, and the population once again became predominantly Catholic. This campaign was only partially successful, however, and the (mainly Reformed) Hungarian nobility were able to secure freedom of worship for Protestants. In practice, this meant <i><a href="/wiki/Cuius_regio,_eius_religio" title="Cuius regio, eius religio">cuius regio, eius religio</a></i>; thus, most individual localities in Hungary are still identifiable as historically Catholic, Lutheran, or Reformed. The country's eastern regions, especially around Debrecen (the "Calvinist Rome"), remain almost completely Reformed,<sup id="cite_ref-220" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-220">&#91;219&#93;</a></sup> a trait they share with historically contiguous ethnically Hungarian regions across the Romanian border. <a href="/wiki/Eastern_Orthodox_Church" title="Eastern Orthodox Church">Orthodox Christianity</a> in Hungary is associated with the country's ethnic minorities: Armenians, Bulgarians, Greeks, Romanians, Rusyns, Ukrainians, and Serbs.
</p><p>Historically, Hungary was home to a <a href="/wiki/History_of_the_Jews_in_Hungary" title="History of the Jews in Hungary">significant Jewish community,</a> with a pre-World War II population of more than 800,000; however, it is estimated that just over 564,000 Hungarian Jews were killed between 1941 and 1945 during the Holocaust in Hungary.<sup id="cite_ref-221" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-221">&#91;220&#93;</a></sup> Between 15 May and 9 July 1944 alone, over 434,000 Jews were deported on 147 trains,<sup id="cite_ref-FOOTNOTEBraham2016771,_774–775_222-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-FOOTNOTEBraham2016771,_774–775-222">&#91;221&#93;</a></sup> most of them to Auschwitz, where about 80% were gassed on arrival. Some Jews were able to escape, but most were either deported to concentration camps, or killed by <a href="/wiki/Arrow_Cross_Party" title="Arrow Cross Party">Arrow Cross</a> members. From over 800,000 Jews living within Hungary's borders in 1941–1944, about 255,500 are thought to have survived. There are about 120,000 Jews in Hungary today.<sup id="cite_ref-223" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-223">&#91;222&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-224" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-224">&#91;223&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Education">Education</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Education_in_Hungary" title="Education in Hungary">Education in Hungary</a> and <a href="/wiki/List_of_universities_and_colleges_in_Hungary" title="List of universities and colleges in Hungary">List of universities and colleges in Hungary</a></div>
<p>Education is predominantly public, run by the <a href="/wiki/Minister_of_Education_(Hungary)" title="Minister of Education (Hungary)">Ministry of Education</a>. <a href="/wiki/Preschool" title="Preschool">Preschool</a>-<a href="/wiki/Kindergarten" title="Kindergarten">kindergarten</a> education is <a href="/wiki/Compulsory_education" title="Compulsory education">compulsory</a> and provided for all children between three and six years old, after which school attendance is also compulsory until the age of sixteen.<sup id="cite_ref-europa_32-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-europa-32">&#91;31&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Primary_education" title="Primary education">Primary education</a> usually lasts for eight years. <a href="/wiki/Secondary_education" title="Secondary education">Secondary education</a> includes three traditional types of schools focused on different academic levels: the <a href="/wiki/Gymnasium_(school)" title="Gymnasium (school)">Gymnasium</a> enrolls the most gifted children and prepares students for university studies; the secondary vocational schools for intermediate students lasts four years and the technical school prepares pupils for <a href="/wiki/Vocational_education" title="Vocational education">vocational education</a> and the world of work. The system is partly flexible and bridges exist, graduates from a vocational school can achieve a two years programme to have access to vocational higher education for instance.<sup id="cite_ref-225" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-225">&#91;224&#93;</a></sup> The <a href="/wiki/Trends_in_International_Mathematics_and_Science_Study" title="Trends in International Mathematics and Science Study">Trends in International Mathematics and Science Study</a> rated 13–14-year-old pupils in Hungary among the best in the world for maths and science.
</p>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1096954695/mw-parser-output/.tmulti"><div class="thumb tmulti tright"><div class="thumbinner multiimageinner" <p>Most of the universities are <a href="/wiki/Public_university" title="Public university">public institutions</a>, and students traditionally study without fee payment. The general requirement for university is the <a href="/wiki/Matura" title="Matura">Matura</a>. The Hungarian public <a href="/wiki/Higher_education" title="Higher education">higher education</a> system includes universities and other higher education institutes, that provide both education curricula and related degrees up to doctoral degree and also contribute to research activities. Health insurance for students is free until the end of their studies. English and German language are important in Hungarian higher education, there are a number of degree programmes that are taught in these languages, which attracts thousands of <a href="/wiki/Student_exchange_program" title="Student exchange program">exchange students</a> every year. Hungary's higher education and training has been ranked 44 out of 148 countries in the Global Competitiveness Report 2014.<sup id="cite_ref-226" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-226">&#91;225&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Hungary has a long tradition of higher education and an established <a href="/wiki/Knowledge_economy" title="Knowledge economy">knowledge economy</a>. Several universities are among the <a href="/wiki/List_of_oldest_universities_in_continuous_operation" title="List of oldest universities in continuous operation">oldest in continuous operation in the world</a>, including the <a href="/wiki/University_of_P%C3%A9cs" title="University of Pécs">University of Pécs</a>, founded in 1367. <a href="/wiki/Sigismund,_Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Sigismund, Holy Roman Emperor">Sigismund</a> established <a href="/wiki/%C3%93buda_University" title="Óbuda University">Óbuda University</a> in 1395. Another, <a href="/wiki/Universitas_Istropolitana" title="Universitas Istropolitana">Universitas Istropolitana</a>, was established 1465 in Pozsony by <a href="/wiki/Matthias_Corvinus" title="Matthias Corvinus">Matthias Corvinus</a>. <a href="/wiki/Nagyszombat" class="mw-redirect" title="Nagyszombat">Nagyszombat</a> University was founded in 1635 and moved to Buda in 1777, and it is called <a href="/wiki/E%C3%B6tv%C3%B6s_Lor%C3%A1nd_University" title="Eötvös Loránd University">Eötvös Loránd University</a> today. The world's first <a href="/wiki/Institute_of_technology" title="Institute of technology">institute of technology</a> was founded in <a href="/wiki/Selmecb%C3%A1nya" class="mw-redirect" title="Selmecbánya">Selmecbánya</a> in 1735; its legal successor is the <a href="/wiki/University_of_Miskolc" title="University of Miskolc">University of Miskolc</a>. The <a href="/wiki/Budapest_University_of_Technology_and_Economics" title="Budapest University of Technology and Economics">Budapest University of Technology and Economics</a> is considered the oldest institute of technology in the world with university rank and structure, its legal predecessor the Institutum Geometrico-Hydrotechnicum was founded in 1782 by <a href="/wiki/Joseph_II,_Holy_Roman_Emperor" title="Joseph II, Holy Roman Emperor">Emperor Joseph II</a>.
</p><p>Hungary ranks fourth (above neighbour Romania, and after China, the United States and Russia) in the all-time medal count at the <a href="/wiki/International_Mathematical_Olympiad" title="International Mathematical Olympiad">International Mathematical Olympiad</a> with 336 total medals, dating back to 1959.
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Health">Health</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Healthcare_in_Hungary" title="Healthcare in Hungary">Healthcare in Hungary</a></div>
<figure class="mw-halign-right" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Szentistvankorhazcivertanlegi.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/90/Szentistvankorhazcivertanlegi.jpg/250px-Szentistvankorhazcivertanlegi.jpg" decoding="async" width="250" height="181" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/90/Szentistvankorhazcivertanlegi.jpg/375px-Szentistvankorhazcivertanlegi.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/9/90/Szentistvankorhazcivertanlegi.jpg 2x" data-file-width="450" data-file-height="325" /></a><figcaption>Szent István Hospital on <a href="/wiki/%C3%9Cll%C5%91i_Avenue" class="mw-redirect" title="Üllői Avenue">Üllői Avenue</a>, <a href="/wiki/Budapest" title="Budapest">Budapest</a>. Together with Szent László Hospital, they form the largest hospital complex in Hungary, built at the turn of the 19th and 20th centuries.</figcaption></figure>
<p>Hungary maintains a <a href="/wiki/Universal_health_care" title="Universal health care">universal health care</a> system largely financed by government <a href="/wiki/National_health_insurance" title="National health insurance">national health insurance</a>. According to the <a href="/wiki/OECD" title="OECD">OECD</a>, 100% of the population is <a href="/wiki/List_of_countries_by_health_insurance_coverage" title="List of countries by health insurance coverage">covered by universal health insurance</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-auto_31-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-auto-31">&#91;30&#93;</a></sup> which is free for children, students, pensioners, people with low income, handicapped people, and church employees.<sup id="cite_ref-227" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-227">&#91;226&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-228" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-228">&#91;227&#93;</a></sup> Hungary spends 7.2% of GDP on healthcare, spending $2,045 per capita, of which $1,365 is provided by the government.<sup id="cite_ref-229" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-229">&#91;228&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Hungary is one of the main destinations of <a href="/wiki/Medical_tourism" title="Medical tourism">medical tourism</a> in Europe, particularly for dentistry,<sup id="cite_ref-230" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-230">&#91;229&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-BBJ1_231-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBJ1-231">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup> in which its share is 42% in Europe and 21% worldwide.<sup id="cite_ref-BBJ1_231-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBJ1-231">&#91;230&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-BBJ2_232-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBJ2-232">&#91;231&#93;</a></sup> <a href="/wiki/Plastic_surgery" title="Plastic surgery">Plastic surgery</a> is also a key sector, with 30% of the clients coming from abroad. Hungary is well known for its spa culture and is home to <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Hungary#Spa_culture" title="Culture of Hungary">numerous medicinal spas</a>,<sup id="cite_ref-IMTJ_233-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-IMTJ-233">&#91;232&#93;</a></sup> which attract "spa tourism".<sup id="cite_ref-BBJ3_234-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-BBJ3-234">&#91;233&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>In common with developed countries, cardiovascular disease is a leading cause of mortality, accounting for 49.4% (62,979) of all deaths in 2013.<sup id="cite_ref-KSH_235-0" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KSH-235">&#91;234&#93;</a></sup> However, this number peaked in 1985 with 79,355 deaths, and has been declining continuously since the fall of communism.<sup id="cite_ref-KSH_235-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KSH-235">&#91;234&#93;</a></sup> The second leading cause of death is cancer with 33,274 (26.2%), which has been stagnant since the 1990s.<sup id="cite_ref-KSH_235-2" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KSH-235">&#91;234&#93;</a></sup> Deaths from accidents dropped from 8,760 in 1990 to 3,654 in 2013; the number of suicides has declined precipitously from 4,911 in 1983 to 2,093 in 2013 (21.1 per 100,000 people), the lowest since 1956.<sup id="cite_ref-KSH_235-3" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-KSH-235">&#91;234&#93;</a></sup> There are considerable health disparities between the western and eastern parts of Hungary; heart disease, hypertension, stroke, and suicide is prevalent in the mostly agricultural and low-income Great Plain region in the east, but infrequent in the high-income, middle class areas of Western Transdanubia and Central Hungary.<sup id="cite_ref-236" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-236">&#91;235&#93;</a></sup> Smoking is a leading cause of death in the country, although it is in steep decline: The proportion of adult smokers declined to 19% in 2013 from 28% in 2012, owing to strict regulations such as a nationwide smoking ban in every indoor public place and the limiting of tobacco sales to state-controlled "National Tobacco Shops".<sup id="cite_ref-237" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-237">&#91;236&#93;</a></sup>
</p><p>Hungary ranks as the <a href="/wiki/Global_Peace_Index" title="Global Peace Index">17th safest country</a> in the world, with a homicide rate of 1.3 per 100,000 people.<sup id="cite_ref-238" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-238">&#91;237&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h2><span class="mw-headline" id="Culture">Culture</span></h2>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main articles: <a href="/wiki/Culture_of_Hungary" title="Culture of Hungary">Culture of Hungary</a>, <a href="/wiki/List_of_World_Heritage_Sites_in_Hungary" title="List of World Heritage Sites in Hungary">List of World Heritage Sites in Hungary</a>, and <a href="/wiki/National_symbols_of_Hungary" title="National symbols of Hungary">National symbols of Hungary</a></div>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">See also: <a href="/wiki/Cinema_of_Hungary" title="Cinema of Hungary">Cinema of Hungary</a>, <a href="/wiki/List_of_films_shot_in_Budapest" title="List of films shot in Budapest">List of films shot in Budapest</a>, and <a href="/wiki/List_of_domesticated_animals_from_Hungary" title="List of domesticated animals from Hungary">List of domesticated animals from Hungary</a></div>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Architecture">Architecture</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1097763485"><table class="box-More_citations_needed plainlinks metadata ambox ambox-content ambox-Refimprove" role="presentation"><tbody><tr><td class="mbox-image"><div class="mbox-image-div"><span typeof="mw:File"><a href="/wiki/File:Question_book-new.svg" class="mw-file-description"><img alt="" src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/50px-Question_book-new.svg.png" decoding="async" width="50" height="39" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/75px-Question_book-new.svg.png 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/en/thumb/9/99/Question_book-new.svg/100px-Question_book-new.svg.png 2x" data-file-width="512" data-file-height="399" /></a></span></div></td><td class="mbox-text"><div class="mbox-text-span">This article <b>needs additional citations for <a href="/wiki/Wikipedia:Verifiability" title="Wikipedia:Verifiability">verification</a></b>.<span class="hide-when-compact"> Please help <a href="/wiki/Special:EditPage/Hungary" title="Special:EditPage/Hungary">improve this article</a> by <a href="/wiki/Help:Referencing_for_beginners" title="Help:Referencing for beginners">adding citations to reliable sources</a>. Unsourced material may be challenged and removed.<br /><small><span class="plainlinks"><i>Find sources:</i>&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.google.com/search?as_eq=wikipedia&amp;q=%22Hungary%22">"Hungary"</a>&#160;–&#160;<a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.google.com/search?tbm=nws&amp;q=%22Hungary%22+-wikipedia&amp;tbs=ar:1">news</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.google.com/search?&amp;q=%22Hungary%22&amp;tbs=bkt:s&amp;tbm=bks">newspapers</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.google.com/search?tbs=bks:1&amp;q=%22Hungary%22+-wikipedia">books</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://scholar.google.com/scholar?q=%22Hungary%22">scholar</a>&#160;<b>·</b> <a rel="nofollow" class="external text" href="https://www.jstor.org/action/doBasicSearch?Query=%22Hungary%22&amp;acc=on&amp;wc=on">JSTOR</a></span></small></span>  <span class="date-container"><i>(<span class="date">April 2022</span>)</i></span><span class="hide-when-compact"><i> (<small><a href="/wiki/Help:Maintenance_template_removal" title="Help:Maintenance template removal">Learn how and when to remove this template message</a></small>)</i></span></div></td></tr></tbody></table>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Architecture_of_Hungary" title="Architecture of Hungary">Architecture of Hungary</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Esterh%C3%A1zy-kast%C3%A9ly_(4051._sz%C3%A1m%C3%BA_m%C5%B1eml%C3%A9k)_2.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Esterh%C3%A1zy-kast%C3%A9ly_%284051._sz%C3%A1m%C3%BA_m%C5%B1eml%C3%A9k%29_2.jpg/310px-Esterh%C3%A1zy-kast%C3%A9ly_%284051._sz%C3%A1m%C3%BA_m%C5%B1eml%C3%A9k%29_2.jpg" decoding="async" width="310" height="123" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Esterh%C3%A1zy-kast%C3%A9ly_%284051._sz%C3%A1m%C3%BA_m%C5%B1eml%C3%A9k%29_2.jpg/465px-Esterh%C3%A1zy-kast%C3%A9ly_%284051._sz%C3%A1m%C3%BA_m%C5%B1eml%C3%A9k%29_2.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/9/9f/Esterh%C3%A1zy-kast%C3%A9ly_%284051._sz%C3%A1m%C3%BA_m%C5%B1eml%C3%A9k%29_2.jpg/620px-Esterh%C3%A1zy-kast%C3%A9ly_%284051._sz%C3%A1m%C3%BA_m%C5%B1eml%C3%A9k%29_2.jpg 2x" data-file-width="10303" data-file-height="4077" /></a><figcaption><a href="/wiki/Eszterh%C3%A1za" title="Eszterháza">Eszterháza Palace</a>, the "Hungarian Versailles", in <a href="/wiki/Fert%C5%91d" title="Fertőd">Fertőd</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gy%C5%91r-Moson-Sopron_County" title="Győr-Moson-Sopron County">Győr-Moson-Sopron County</a></figcaption></figure>
<p>Hungary is home to the <a href="/wiki/Doh%C3%A1ny_Street_Synagogue" title="Dohány Street Synagogue">largest synagogue in Europe</a>, built in 1859 in <a href="/wiki/Moorish_Revival_architecture" title="Moorish Revival architecture">Moorish Revival</a> style with a capacity of 3,000 people; the <a href="/wiki/Sz%C3%A9chenyi_thermal_bath" title="Széchenyi thermal bath">largest medicinal bath in Europe</a>, completed in 1913 in Modern <a href="/wiki/Renaissance_architecture" title="Renaissance architecture">Renaissance style</a> and located in the Budapest city park; one of the <a href="/wiki/Esztergom_Basilica" title="Esztergom Basilica">largest basilicas in Europe</a>; the <a href="/wiki/Pannonhalma_Archabbey" title="Pannonhalma Archabbey">second-largest territorial abbey</a> in the world; and the largest <a href="/wiki/P%C3%A9cs" title="Pécs">early Christian necropolis</a> outside Italy. Notable architectural styles include <a href="/wiki/Historicism" title="Historicism">Historicism</a> and <a href="/wiki/Art_Nouveau" title="Art Nouveau">Art Nouveau</a>, or rather several variants of Art Nouveau. In contrast to Historicism, Hungarian Art Nouveau is based on national architectural characteristics. Taking the eastern origins of the Hungarians into account, <a href="/wiki/%C3%96d%C3%B6n_Lechner" title="Ödön Lechner">Ödön Lechner</a>, the most important figure in Hungarian Art Nouveau, was initially inspired by Indian and Syrian architecture and later by traditional Hungarian decorative designs. In this way, he created an original synthesis of architectural styles. By applying them to three-dimensional architectural elements, he produced a version of Art Nouveau that was specific to Hungary. Turning away from the style of Lechner, yet taking inspiration from his approach, the group of "Young People" (<i>Fiatalok</i>), which included <a href="/wiki/K%C3%A1roly_K%C3%B3s" title="Károly Kós">Károly Kós</a> and Dezsö Zrumeczky, used the characteristic structures and forms of traditional Hungarian architecture to achieve the same end.
</p>
<figure class="mw-default-size mw-halign-left" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Ap%C3%A1ts%C3%A1gi_templom_(8941._sz%C3%A1m%C3%BA_m%C5%B1eml%C3%A9k)_7.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Ap%C3%A1ts%C3%A1gi_templom_%288941._sz%C3%A1m%C3%BA_m%C5%B1eml%C3%A9k%29_7.jpg/220px-Ap%C3%A1ts%C3%A1gi_templom_%288941._sz%C3%A1m%C3%BA_m%C5%B1eml%C3%A9k%29_7.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="146" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Ap%C3%A1ts%C3%A1gi_templom_%288941._sz%C3%A1m%C3%BA_m%C5%B1eml%C3%A9k%29_7.jpg/330px-Ap%C3%A1ts%C3%A1gi_templom_%288941._sz%C3%A1m%C3%BA_m%C5%B1eml%C3%A9k%29_7.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/d/dd/Ap%C3%A1ts%C3%A1gi_templom_%288941._sz%C3%A1m%C3%BA_m%C5%B1eml%C3%A9k%29_7.jpg/440px-Ap%C3%A1ts%C3%A1gi_templom_%288941._sz%C3%A1m%C3%BA_m%C5%B1eml%C3%A9k%29_7.jpg 2x" data-file-width="1556" data-file-height="1032" /></a><figcaption>Romanesque <a href="/w/index.php?title=J%C3%A1k_Abbey&amp;action=edit&amp;redlink=1" class="new" title="Ják Abbey (page does not exist)">Ják Abbey</a>, <a href="/wiki/Vas_County" title="Vas County">Vas County</a>, built between 1220 and 1256</figcaption></figure>
<p>Besides the two principal styles, Budapest also displays local versions of trends originating from other European countries. The Sezession from <a href="/wiki/Vienna" title="Vienna">Vienna</a>, the German <a href="/wiki/Jugendstil" title="Jugendstil">Jugendstil</a>, Art Nouveau from Belgium and France, and the influence of English and Finnish architecture are all reflected in the buildings constructed at the turn of the 20th century. <a href="/wiki/B%C3%A9la_Lajta" title="Béla Lajta">Béla Lajta</a> initially adopted Lechner's style, subsequently drawing his inspiration from English and Finnish trends; after developing an interest in the <a href="/wiki/Ancient_Egypt" title="Ancient Egypt">Egyptian</a> style, he finally arrived at modern architecture. <a href="/wiki/Alad%C3%A1r_%C3%81rkay" title="Aladár Árkay">Aladár Árkay</a> took almost the same route. <a href="/wiki/Istv%C3%A1n_Medgyaszay" title="István Medgyaszay">István Medgyaszay</a> developed his own style, which differed from Lechner's, using stylised traditional motifs to create decorative designs in concrete. In the sphere of applied arts, those chiefly responsible for promoting the spread of Art Nouveau were the School and Museum of Decorative Arts, which opened in 1896.
</p><p>Foreigners have unexpectedly "discovered" that a significantly large portion of the citizens lives in old and architecturally valuable buildings. In the Budapest downtown area almost all the buildings are about one hundred years old, with thick walls, high ceilings, and motifs on the front walls.<sup id="cite_ref-Eliznik.org.uk_56-1" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-Eliznik.org.uk-56">&#91;55&#93;</a></sup><sup id="cite_ref-239" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-239">&#91;238&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
<h3><span class="mw-headline" id="Music">Music</span></h3>
<link rel="mw-deduplicated-inline-style" href="mw-data:TemplateStyles:r1033289096"><div role="note" class="hatnote navigation-not-searchable">Main article: <a href="/wiki/Music_of_Hungary" title="Music of Hungary">Music of Hungary</a></div>
<figure class="mw-default-size" typeof="mw:File/Thumb"><a href="/wiki/File:Budapest_Opera_1.jpg" class="mw-file-description"><img src="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/68/Budapest_Opera_1.jpg/220px-Budapest_Opera_1.jpg" decoding="async" width="220" height="149" class="mw-file-element" srcset="//upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/68/Budapest_Opera_1.jpg/330px-Budapest_Opera_1.jpg 1.5x, //upload.wikimedia.org/wikipedia/commons/thumb/6/68/Budapest_Opera_1.jpg/440px-Budapest_Opera_1.jpg 2x" data-file-width="4845" data-file-height="3286" /></a><figcaption>The <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_State_Opera_House" title="Hungarian State Opera House">Hungarian State Opera House</a> on <a href="/wiki/Andr%C3%A1ssy_%C3%BAt" title="Andrássy út">Andrássy út</a> (a <a href="/wiki/World_Heritage_Site" title="World Heritage Site">World Heritage Site</a>)</figcaption></figure>
<p>Hungarian music consists mainly of traditional <a href="/wiki/Hungarian_folk_music" title="Hungarian folk music">Hungarian folk music</a> and music by prominent composers such as <a href="/wiki/Franz_Liszt" title="Franz Liszt">Franz Liszt</a> and <a href="/wiki/B%C3%A9la_Bart%C3%B3k" title="Béla Bartók">Béla Bartók</a>, considered to be among the greatest Hungarian composers. Other renowned composers are <a href="/wiki/Ernst_von_Dohn%C3%A1nyi" title="Ernst von Dohnányi">Ernst von Dohnányi</a>, <a href="/wiki/Franz_Schmidt_(composer)" title="Franz Schmidt (composer)">Franz Schmidt</a>, <a href="/wiki/Zolt%C3%A1n_Kod%C3%A1ly" title="Zoltán Kodály">Zoltán Kodály</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gabriel_von_Wayditch" title="Gabriel von Wayditch">Gabriel von Wayditch</a>, <a href="/wiki/Rudolf_Wagner-R%C3%A9geny" title="Rudolf Wagner-Régeny">Rudolf Wagner-Régeny</a>, <a href="/wiki/L%C3%A1szl%C3%B3_Lajtha" title="László Lajtha">László Lajtha</a>, <a href="/wiki/Franz_Leh%C3%A1r" title="Franz Lehár">Franz Lehár</a>, <a href="/wiki/Emmerich_K%C3%A1lm%C3%A1n" title="Emmerich Kálmán">Kálmán Imre</a>, <a href="/wiki/S%C3%A1ndor_Veress" title="Sándor Veress">Sándor Veress</a> and <a href="/wiki/Mikl%C3%B3s_R%C3%B3zsa" title="Miklós Rózsa">Miklós Rózsa</a>. Hungarian traditional music tends to have a strong <a href="/wiki/Dactyl_(poetry)" title="Dactyl (poetry)">dactylic</a> rhythm, as the language is invariably stressed on the first syllable of each word.
</p><p>Hungary has renowned composers of contemporary classical music, <a href="/wiki/Gy%C3%B6rgy_Ligeti" title="György Ligeti">György Ligeti</a>, <a href="/wiki/Gy%C3%B6rgy_Kurt%C3%A1g" title="György Kurtág">György Kurtág</a>, <a href="/wiki/P%C3%A9ter_E%C3%B6tv%C3%B6s" title="Péter Eötvös">Péter Eötvös</a>, <a href="/wiki/Zolt%C3%A1n_Kod%C3%A1ly" title="Zoltán Kodály">Zoltán Kodály</a> and <a href="/wiki/Zolt%C3%A1n_Jeney" title="Zoltán Jeney">Zoltán Jeney</a> among them. Bartók was among the most significant musicians of the 20th century. His music was invigorated by the themes, modes, and rhythmic patterns of the Hungarian and neighbouring folk music traditions he studied, which he synthesised with influences from his contemporaries into his own distinctive style.<sup id="cite_ref-240" class="reference"><a href="#cite_note-240">&#91;239&#93;</a></sup>
</p>
